Live at https://neonstickfight.com (it was askscottpierce.com/stick-fight until 2026-11-11; see Hosting, The move to neonstickfight.com)

# stick-fight

A browser fighting game. Stick figures animated at Xiao Xiao quality, lit like a modern 2D game.

## Design pillars

Read these before adding a mechanic. When a change is a judgment call, these decide it.

- **Timing, not speed.** Skill is pressing one thing at the right moment because you read what the other player committed to. It is never how many inputs you can enter a second. Nobody should win on fast, simple input, and nothing should need a reflex younger than its player.
- **Give and take, like tai chi and aikido.** Wait for the other player to commit, then use their own force. The harder someone commits, the harder they fall when it's read. Every option has an answer, and every answer costs something when it's guessed wrong.
- **Mashing loses.** A press thrown where it doesn't belong botches (Botches), every swing costs stamina, and the last point leaves you fainted and open. A new mechanic must not hand mashers a way back in: no defense that a held direction or a repeated button triggers by accident, no escape you mash out of.
- **Simple bodies, deep systems.** The stick figures and their inputs are the easy part: a direction plus one button, no motion inputs, strings with a rhythm rather than a speed. The sophistication lives in the botch and technique system, where the same physics that tangles a mistimed press rewards an exactly timed one.
- **Windows a person can learn.** A timing window can be generous if missing it, or using it when nothing was coming, is committal and punishable. Tight-and-free is a reflex test; wider-and-committal is a read.

## Run

```sh
npm install
npm run dev          # Vite on http://localhost:8077/: the homepage, the game at /play/, fighter pages at /fighters/
npm run dev:worker   # the online-rooms Worker under wrangler dev on :8787 (Vite proxies ./api to it)
npm run typecheck    # tsc over src/, then over worker/
npm run netsim:watch # spectators: real matches through the room's code to spectators, every hash compared (Multiplayer, Spectators)
npm run netsim:invariants # the sim's per-step invariants over 648,000 CPU-vs-CPU steps: every character, versus and horde, three seeds; stops at the first violation with the seed, step, unit and last log entries (Fundamentals, States and the flags they own). CI runs it before every deploy. --durations prints how long each state lasted; ?invariants on the dev server checks any scene in the browser
npm run test:invariants:scenes # the same invariants over every ?scene=, 20 s each, in headless Chromium on the raw source (no dev server)
npm run test:watch   # spectators in real browsers; needs `npm run build` and `npm run dev:worker` first (it reads :8787)
npm run test:qr      # the join link's QR code read back by a separate decoder (Multiplayer, Scan to join)
npm run test:join    # a join link lands a first-time visitor in the room with nothing typed; needs `npm run build` and `npm run dev:worker` first
npm run test:ios     # the iPhone Safari fit and the Add to Home Screen helper; needs `npx vite --port 8191` running (Install as an app)
npm run test:movelist # the MOVE LIST scrolls by finger, wheel and keys, and a drag in a fight still pans nothing; needs `npx vite --port 8193` running (Controls, Move list)
npm run test:square  # a phone held upright: the view's layout at five sizes (two with browser bars showing) and the camera keeping the fight inside it; needs `npm run build` first (Controls, Held upright: the view)
npm run test:horde   # horde is endless: no clock, the slow regen, 12 CPU runs that never stall between floors, and every way a goon goes down keeps the run going (Arcade flow, Horde)
npm run test:ko      # no hit path decides what a KO means: a goon at 0 health with no KO reported never ends a run (Fundamentals, How a KO resolves)
npm run test:names   # the name filter's blocked and allowed examples, no browser (Privacy and terms)
npm run test:admin   # the Worker's name filter and the admin rename/close route; needs `npm run dev:worker` with ADMIN_TOKEN in .dev.vars (Privacy and terms)
npm run test:delete  # (and test:delete:ui, on screen) DELETE MY PROFILE: D1 rows gone, out of its crews, the same email signs up fresh; needs `npm run dev:worker` with DEV_LOGIN_CODES=1 (Privacy and terms)
npm run test:support # the support desk: issues, auto-replies, forged senders, non-members, limits, prompt injection, replies as comments, the scrub; starts its own wrangler dev with GitHub, the model and webmail mocked (--real-ai for the real model, --show to print what was posted) (Privacy and terms, Support desk)
npm run test:age     # the age question before email, the privacy links, the name filter on screen; needs `npm run build` and `npm run dev:worker` (Privacy and terms)
npm run test:career  # (and test:career:worker, test:career:ui) career and sticks: the payout math and the mash case, the Worker's awards, the offline cap, the guest merge, palettes on screen and online (Progression)
npm run test:closet  # (and test:closet:ui) the Supply Closet: the catalog, unlock text, locked states and counts; :ui buys, equips and the silhouettes in Chromium (Progression, The Supply Closet)
npm run test:mastery # (and test:mastery:ui) fighter mastery: ranks, pay, NEW HIRE, unlocks, the merge, taunts only under the ROUND card in the real sim; :ui the rank-up, the closet and character select in Chromium (Progression, Fighter mastery)
npm run test:shifts  # (and test:shifts:ui) daily shifts: the date seed, completion, swap and merge, and the real sim with a masher on P1 finishing nothing; :ui in Chromium against the built game (Progression, Daily shifts)
npm run test:unfurl  # crew/<slug> and room/<CODE> links unfurl as that room for every bot, and the old ?crew= and ?room= forms 301 to them; needs `npm run build` and `npm run dev:worker` first (Site, Share image)
npm run test:move    # neonstickfight.com, www, and the old address serving and redirecting, over HTTP and in real browsers; needs `npm run build` (Hosting, The move)
npm run test:books   # a run or match cut short still counts once: src/bookkeeping.js on a stand-in sim, then the real flow with its beacons caught (a horde run quit after a floor, a bounce, the tab closing or hidden, an arcade RESTART STAGE) (Analytics, Match bookkeeping, Cut short)
npm run test:books:worker   # the Worker's side, against `npm run dev:worker`: an arcade quit adds time and no matchup, the room's clock for an online match with no agreed result
npm run test:crew    # crew high scores and stats, play time and the live count: the Worker over its sockets, then the lobby's tabs and a run reaching a crew's table in real pages; needs the migrations applied, `npm run build` and `npm run dev:worker` first (Crew rooms, High scores and Stats; Analytics, Play time)
```

Online play needs both: `npm run dev` for the game and `npm run dev:worker` for the room server. Vite forwards `/api/*` (HTTP and WebSocket) to `http://127.0.0.1:8787/api/*`. To run the production layout instead, `npm run build` and then `npx wrangler dev` serves the site at `http://localhost:8787/` (the game at `/play/`) with the API beside it. wrangler dev hands the script the first route's host, `neonstickfight.com`, so the links the Worker writes say that; `--local-upstream askscottpierce.com` stands in for the old address instead (`npm run test:move` does both).

The game is plain ES modules with canvas 2D for drawing and WebGL for the lighting composite. Vite serves it in development and bundles it for deploys. New modules can be TypeScript (`.ts`) next to the JavaScript ones; `tsconfig.json` has `allowJs` on and `checkJs` off, so add `// @ts-check` to a JS file to type-check it. The demo pages (`demo/*.html`) and the capture tool work against the dev server as before.

## Hosting

The game is a Cloudflare Worker at **https://neonstickfight.com**, on the Scott Personal Account (`0223b96fe77599b23ff8ec7fcd32e2f1`). The domain was registered through Cloudflare Registrar on 2026-11-11, so its zone is on the same account. Two custom domains attach the Worker: `neonstickfight.com` and `www.neonstickfight.com` (`custom_domain: true` in `wrangler.jsonc`, so Cloudflare makes the DNS records and certificates). www answers every page with a 301 to the same page on the bare domain. The homepage is `/`, the game `/play/`, the fighter pages `/fighters/`, the guides `/how-to-play/`, the API `/api/*` (see Site). The assets binding's `auto-trailing-slash` redirects `/play` and `/fighters/courier` to the same path with a slash, so each page's relative asset paths resolve.

The old address, `askscottpierce.com/stick-fight` and `askscottpierce.com/stick-fight/*`, keeps its two zone routes on the same Worker (every other path on askscottpierce.com stays with the main site). What it does is the var `OLD_PATH` in `wrangler.jsonc` (see The move to neonstickfight.com below).

Static files come from the assets binding (`dist/`, binding `ASSETS`). The Worker script (`worker/index.ts`) runs first for `/api/*`, for the crew and room links `/crew/*` and `/room/*` (the game page with the room's share tags), and for every page (`run_worker_first`: `/`, `/play/*`, `/fighters/*`, `/how-to-play/*`), so it can send www away; a page goes straight back to the assets unless it's `play/` or the homepage with an old `?crew=` or `?room=` link in the query, which gets a 301 to the clean one (Site, Crew and room links unfurl as that room). Scripts, styles and pictures never wake it, so a page view costs one Worker request. Under `api/` it serves quick rooms through a Durable Object class `Room` (binding `ROOMS`, migration `v1` with `new_sqlite_classes: ["Room"]`), crew rooms through a second class `Crew` (binding `CREWS`, migration `v2` with `new_sqlite_classes: ["Crew"]`, `worker/crew.ts`), profiles and email sign-in (`worker/auth.ts`), and careers (`worker/career.ts`, Progression). Anything the assets don't have is a 404. The first deploy with the script creates each Durable Object class from its migration. Workers rate limiting bindings cap things per IP (see Multiplayer, Limits): `ROOM_LIMIT` (namespace `7101`, quick rooms), `JOIN_LIMIT` (`7102`, WebSocket joins to either kind of room), `CREW_LIMIT` (`7103`, 5 new crew rooms a minute), `AUTH_LIMIT` (`7104`, 20 profile and sign-in calls a minute), `EVENT_LIMIT` (`7105`, 60 analytics posts a minute) and `STATS_LIMIT` (`7106`, 60 reads of `api/stats` a minute) `OG_LIMIT` (`7107`, 20 share pictures drawn a minute) and `CAREER_LIMIT` (`7108`, 40 career calls a minute; Progression); they're free on every plan, and a namespace id must be unique on the account.

**D1, email and cookies** (for profiles; see Multiplayer, Profiles and sign-in). The D1 database `stick-fight-auth` (id `8a0ea2d9-9d74-466f-a6ff-5a0446d35653`, created 2026-11-08, binding `DB`) holds accounts, hashed sign-in codes, hashed sessions and the send and guess counters; its schema is the files in `migrations/`, applied in order: `0001_profiles.sql` (the four tables), `0002_prefs.sql` (adds `accounts.prefs`, JSON text with default `'{}'`, for settings that follow the account; see Profiles and sign-in) `0003_stats.sql` (the match bookkeeping's all-time rollups, `stats_picks` and `stats_matchups`, which have no player data in them; see Analytics) `0004_playtime.sql` (`stats_time`, seconds played per mode per UTC day, no player data either; see Analytics, Play time; 2026-11-11) `0005_finishers.sql` (`stats_finishers`, how many matches each move finished, for the title's ticker; see Analytics, The title's ticker; 2026-11-12) `0006_career.sql` (career XP, sticks, the daily-bonus dates, the offline cap's count, owned and equipped palettes, as columns on `accounts`; see Progression; 2026-11-12) and `0007_support.sql` (`support_tickets`, the support desk's issue number to player id; see Privacy and terms, Support desk; 2026-11-12) and `0008_shifts.sql` (`accounts.shifts`, today's daily-shift progress; see Progression, Daily shifts; 2026-11-12). The `send_email` binding `EMAIL` sends the sign-in codes from `EMAIL_FROM` (`hey@neonstickfight.com`, a var in `wrangler.jsonc`; Email Sending on the neonstickfight.com zone since 2026-11-12, with DKIM, SPF and DMARC records). Mail sent to any address on neonstickfight.com, replies to the sign-in email and player reports included, lands in the catch-all mailbox at https://webmail.neonstickfight.com (the `webmail` Worker, `~/code/webmail`; Email Routing's catch-all points at it). The only cookie is `sf_session` (HttpOnly, Secure, SameSite=Lax, `Path=/`, host-only on neonstickfight.com, 30 days; on the old address while it serves, `Path=/stick-fight`, because that origin is shared). The sign-in email comes from `hey@neonstickfight.com` (since 2026-11-12; it was `hey@askscottpierce.com` before), whichever address served the page. Its link always points at `https://neonstickfight.com/play/`, whichever address asked. `.dev.vars` (gitignored) holds `DEV_LOGIN_CODES=1`, which sends no email and returns the code to the page instead, and `DEV_HOOKS=1`, which turns on the crew expiry test route; neither is ever set in production. Locally, apply the schema with `npx wrangler d1 migrations apply stick-fight-auth --local`, and again after pulling a new migration. `npm run types:worker` regenerates `worker/worker-configuration.d.ts` after a change to `wrangler.jsonc`.

```sh
npx wrangler d1 migrations apply stick-fight-auth --remote   # once per new migration, before the deploy that needs it
npm run deploy       # vite build into dist/ (file path = URL path), then wrangler deploy
```

**Deploys run on push.** Every push to `main` deploys through GitHub Actions (`.github/workflows/deploy.yml`): `npm ci`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run netsim:invariants` (about 25 s), the D1 migrations (`apply stick-fight-auth --remote`, a no-op when there's nothing new), then `npm run deploy`. One deploy runs at a time, in push order, and a running one is never cancelled; Actions, Run workflow deploys `main` by hand. The repo needs the variable `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID` (`0223b96fe77599b23ff8ec7fcd32e2f1`) and the secret `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`, a token named "stick-fight GitHub Actions deploy" with Workers Scripts Write and D1 Write on the account and Workers Routes Write on both zones, askscottpierce.com and neonstickfight.com (Cloudflare wants it on every zone a deploy attaches to; custom domains need nothing more). `CF_TOKEN_MANAGER=... NEW_ZONE_ID=<neonstickfight.com's zone id> scripts/ci-token.sh` makes it and pipes it straight into `gh secret set`, never printing it; running it again makes a second token, so delete the old one in the dashboard. Migrations only add tables and columns, so they run before the deploy and the old Worker keeps working on top of them. The commands above still work for a deploy from this machine.

**Secrets.** `ADMIN_TOKEN` turns on the admin route (Privacy and terms, Admin); without it that route is a 404. `GITHUB_TOKEN` lets the support desk open issues (Privacy and terms, Support desk); without it every support email still reaches webmail, marked HELD, and no ticket is made. The other two are for Cloudflare Realtime TURN (see Multiplayer): `TURN_KEY_ID`, the id of the TURN key named `stick-fight` on the account, and `TURN_KEY_API_TOKEN`, that key's secret. `GET /api/ice` uses them to mint TURN credentials that expire after an hour. Without them the endpoint answers with public STUN only, which is what local dev gets unless `.dev.vars` (gitignored) holds the same two lines. Secrets persist across deploys. To rotate the key, create a new one and point the Worker at it, then delete the old one:

```sh
# a token with "Calls Write" on the account (the dashboard, or minted with CF_TOKEN_MANAGER)
curl -X POST -H "Authorization: Bearer $CALLS_TOKEN" -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/accounts/0223b96fe77599b23ff8ec7fcd32e2f1/calls/turn_keys \
  -d '{"name":"stick-fight"}' > key.json             # result.uid and result.secret
jq -r .result.uid key.json    | npx wrangler secret put TURN_KEY_ID
jq -r .result.secret key.json | npx wrangler secret put TURN_KEY_API_TOKEN
rm key.json
# then DELETE .../calls/turn_keys/<old uid>; credentials minted from it stop working
```

The same key list is in the dashboard under Realtime, TURN Server. TURN bills $0.05 per GB sent out past the free allowance (the account's first 1,000 GB of Realtime a month, as of 2026-11), and a match's input packets are tiny. Only matches whose phones can't reach each other directly use it.

Vite builds with `base: './'` and the pages use only relative paths, so they run the same at `/`, on the dev tunnel and under `/stick-fight/` on the old address. The one page served from somewhere else is the game at `crew/<slug>` and `room/<CODE>`, one level off `play/`: the Worker puts `<base href="../play/">` first in its head (relative, so it resolves to `/play/` here and to `/stick-fight/play/` while the old address serves; the dev server, `vite.config.ts`, uses `/play/`), so its paths resolve as they do at `play/`. `src/root.js` pins a `<base>` on any page that doesn't have one, so moving the address bar with `history.replaceState` (into a crew and back out) never changes what a relative URL means. Anything shared at the root (the API, `sw.js`, `version.json`, `vendor/`) goes through `src/root.js`, which reads how deep the page is from `<html data-root="../">`. The places that know the layout are `wrangler.jsonc` (routes, `run_worker_first`, `OLD_PATH`), `worker/env.ts` (`API`, `SITE_HOST`, `OLD_PREFIX`, `siteOrigin`), `worker/index.ts` (the front door: www, the old path), and the absolute URLs in the canonical and Open Graph tags (`index.html`, `play/index.html`, `site/templates.mjs` `SITE`). localStorage keys start with `stick-fight-`, from when the origin was shared with every app on askscottpierce.com; they're kept, so nothing resets. Every page carries the Plausible tag with `data-domain="neonstickfight.com"` (see Analytics).

### The move to neonstickfight.com

The game moved from `askscottpierce.com/stick-fight` to `neonstickfight.com` on 2026-11-11, before the first promotion went out. The same Worker serves both, so the Durable Objects (`Room`, `Crew`, `Lobby`), the D1 database and the TURN secrets carry over: every crew room and saved profile is still there.

**`OLD_PATH`** (a var in `wrangler.jsonc`) says what the old address does. The script's front door (`oldPath` in `worker/index.ts`) handles every request under `/stick-fight` on askscottpierce.com:
- **`"serve"`** (what this branch ships): the game, as before. The request goes to the app with `/stick-fight` taken off, and the answer gets it back: a `Location` header that starts with `/` gets the prefix, and the session cookie gets `Path=/stick-fight`. `/stick-fight` without a slash is a 301 to `/stick-fight/`. Share tags and the sign-in email's link already point at neonstickfight.com (`siteOrigin` in `worker/env.ts`); share links the page makes follow the page's own address.
- **`"redirect"`**: every page is a 301 to the same path on `https://neonstickfight.com`, query and all. `/stick-fight/room/K7QX` lands on `/room/K7QX` and `/stick-fight/crew/<slug>` on `/crew/<slug>`; an old `?crew=` or `?room=` link on `/stick-fight/` or `/stick-fight/play/` goes straight to its clean link (`/stick-fight/play/?room=K7QX` to `/room/K7QX`), the rest of the query kept; `/stick-fight` and `/stick-fight/` go to `/`. A fragment rides along, since the browser keeps it across a redirect, so a sign-in link's `#signin=...&code=...` still signs in. The API under `/stick-fight/api/` keeps answering, so a tab opened before the switch finishes its match. `/stick-fight/sw.js` becomes a service worker that deletes this app's caches (`stick-fight-*`) and unregisters itself: a browser won't take a redirect in place of a service worker script, and an old install would otherwise keep answering from its cache. The 301s cache for an hour.

**Moving day**, in order:
1. Check that the neonstickfight.com zone is on the Scott Personal Account and has no DNS record on `neonstickfight.com` or `www` (a custom domain can't replace a CNAME).
2. Mint the deploy token for both zones: `CF_TOKEN_MANAGER=... NEW_ZONE_ID=... scripts/ci-token.sh` on Scott's Mac. Without it the deploy fails when it attaches the custom domains.
3. Add `neonstickfight.com` as a site in Plausible (plausible.ljs.app). Until then its pageviews aren't counted.
4. Merge with `OLD_PATH` on `"serve"`. The deploy brings up neonstickfight.com next to the old address.
5. Check the new domain by hand: the homepage, a fighter page, a quick match between two phones (one on cellular, for TURN: the readout says DIRECT LINK), the same with `?relay=1` (RELAYED LINK), a crew match, SAVE MY PROFILE (the email's link opens neonstickfight.com), and installing the app.
6. Set `OLD_PATH` to `"redirect"` and push.

**What doesn't carry over: this browser's local data.** localStorage is per origin, so on neonstickfight.com a player starts with a fresh player id, no rival records, no crew list, no high scores and default settings. Signed-in players get their id, name, crews and touch size back by signing in with their email (Multiplayer, Profiles and sign-in), and with the id every crew standing. Nothing carries a guest's id across. The server can't read localStorage, and a 301 has no page to run a script in. An iframe of the old address inside the new one would get partitioned storage in Safari and Chrome, so it would see nothing. A query parameter would put the id, which is the player's key to their crew records, into history, logs, analytics and every link they copy. So a guest who comes back through a crew link joins as a new member, with a new row in the standings. An app installed from the old address opens its old start URL, which then redirects, so it runs in a browser view; install again from neonstickfight.com.

**Checking it.** `npm run test:move` (`scripts/move/check.mjs`, after `npm run build`) starts four `wrangler dev` instances over local https, each standing in for one host (`--local-upstream`): neonstickfight.com, www, and askscottpierce.com with `OLD_PATH` on serve and on redirect. It checks the pages, tags, sitemap, manifest, service worker, sign-in link and cookie on each over HTTP. Then real Chromium, its resolver pointed at those instances (`--host-resolver-rules`), loads the sites by name and follows the redirects itself: old room, crew and fighter links land in the right place, an old sign-in link signs in on the new domain with a `Path=/` cookie, the service worker installs, a quick match connects peer to peer and with `?relay=1` through the room, a crew match opens with the lobby showing a neonstickfight.com link, and the old address still plays while it serves. TURN can't be checked locally (no key), so `/api/ice` answers with STUN there. Check it on the real domain (step 5).

Serving the game publicly ships the vendored Strudel bundle, which is AGPL-3.0, so the game is AGPL-3.0 too (see License and credits).

## Install as an app

The game is installable (`public/manifest.webmanifest`, icons in `public/icons/`, made by rendering a neon fighter on a canvas): it opens full screen with its own icon, and turns freely (`"orientation": "any"`, since 2026-11-11; it was locked to landscape before), so it plays upright or sideways. The manifest's `start_url` is `./play/` (the game) and its `scope` is `./`, so the homepage and fighter pages are inside the app too; `id` stays `"./"` so an app installed before the move is the same app. The homepage and the game both link the manifest, so the browser offers to install from either, and the homepage shows an INSTALL hint (the browser's prompt where there is one, the Add to Home Screen how-to on an iPhone). An app installed before the game moved to `play/` (its saved start URL is the old `/stick-fight/`, which an iPhone never updates) opens the homepage, which sees it's running standalone and goes on to `play/`. An installed app belongs to its origin, so since 2026-11-11 the app is neonstickfight.com's, with `scope` the whole site and `start_url` `/play/` there. One installed from askscottpierce.com/stick-fight stays that origin's app: while the old address serves it works as before, and once it redirects its pages open neonstickfight.com in a browser view and its service worker retires (Hosting, The move to neonstickfight.com). Install again from neonstickfight.com to get the new one.
- **Android and desktop Chrome or Edge:** an INSTALL button appears on the title when the browser offers to install, and runs the browser's own prompt (`src/install.js` keeps the `beforeinstallprompt` event).
- **iPhone and iPad Safari:** Safari has no install prompt and no Fullscreen API, so a home-screen app is the only way to full screen, and its bars are big: an iPhone 14 held sideways with the tab bar on leaves the page 844 by about 280. Two things make that bearable (`src/homescreen.js`, loaded by the touch layer; it applies when `install.kind` is `'ios'`):
  - **The stage fits what you can see.** It's sized with `100dvh` (`play/index.html`), the visible height. `100vh` on iPhone Safari is the height with the bars hidden, so the stage ran under the fold of a page that can't scroll; that was the bug until 2026-11-10. The `vh` rule stays as the fallback for browsers without `dvh`. The SETTINGS panel and the old host panel cap their height the same way, and the touch layer refits when `visualViewport` resizes.
  - **A way to the home screen from every screen.** The touch layer's fullscreen pill stays (other phones lose it where fullscreen can't work) and opens a three-step how-to: Share, Add to Home Screen, open it from there. A PLAY FULL SCREEN callout shows on every menu, never in a fight, until it's opened or closed once (`localStorage['sf-homescreen-seen']`): at the bottom of the screen held upright, in the gutter left of the stage held sideways, and not at all when that gutter is under 4.5rem (an iPhone SE sideways), where the pill is the way in. It can't live only on the title, because a friend who scans a join link never sees the title. The title's INSTALL button opens the same how-to (the event `sf:homescreen`). The how-to fits a 280px-tall window without scrolling. Opened from a room or crew link (`room/<CODE>`, `crew/<slug>`) or in an online match, it adds that the app opens at the title, so play this one here first: an iPhone's home-screen app has its own storage and starts at `play/`, not at the link.
  - The `apple-mobile-web-app-*` tags make the home-screen app run full screen under a see-through status bar; the touch layer already pads for the safe areas.
  - **Checked** (`npm run test:ios`, `scripts/ios/check.mjs`; 2026-11-10): headless Chromium with Safari's user agent and the Fullscreen API removed, at 844x280, 844x330, 667x325, 390x664 and 375x553. The stage is whole on screen, the callout shows on menus and touches no button, it's gone in a fight, the pill and the callout and INSTALL each open the how-to, the how-to fits, a fight goes on under it, X and opening it both retire the callout across a reload, and neither an installed app nor an Android phone gets any of it. **Not checked on a real iPhone.** Chromium's `vh` and `dvh` are the same number, so the under-the-fold bug itself can't be reproduced there; the script only checks that the stage's rule is the `dvh` one.
- **Installed:** `<html>` gets `.standalone`, and INSTALL and the fullscreen buttons (touch and desktop, `src/fullscreen.js`) go away; F11 is left to the browser.
- **In the homepage's frame:** no INSTALL on the title (`install.kind` is null when `EMBEDDED`; browsers don't offer installs from a frame). The homepage's own install hint covers it.
- **Updates:** every build writes `version.json` (`{ build, at }`, the short git commit plus the build time, from `vite.config.ts`) and bakes the same id into the page as `__BUILD_ID__`. `src/update.js` checks `version.json` 20s after load, every 5 minutes, and when the tab or app comes back; when a newer build is out it offers UPDATE: a button on the title (reachable by controller) and a small NEW VERSION pill on menu screens. Never mid-fight or in an online session (a reload would drop the room). UPDATE asks the service worker to update and reloads.
- **Service worker** (`public/sw.js` at the root, so its scope covers every page; registered by `src/install.js` on https only and never on `?scene=` test pages, from the game and the site's pages): hashed `assets/*` files are cache-first, pages and everything else network-first with the cache as the fallback, so a deploy shows up on the next launch and the installed game plays offline. The core cache is the game (`play/`), the manifest and the icons; offline, a page that was never cached falls back to the game, and `play/?room=...` matches the cached `play/`. A crew or room link (`crew/<slug>`, `room/<CODE>`) is inside the scope too: online it's cached under its own address like any page, and offline and never cached it gets the cached game with `<base href=".../play/">` added, so its paths resolve and the game still reads the slug from the address (2026-11-11; checked in Chromium on localhost with the worker registered by hand, offline, on a crew link it had never seen: the game loaded into the crew lobby with no failed requests). `version.json` always goes to the network. The room API and other origins are left to the network. Bump `CACHE` in `sw.js` (now `stick-fight-v3`) if the cached core list changes. On neonstickfight.com the service worker starts fresh: a new origin has no old caches.

**Balance pass after faster stamina (2026-11-08).** Faster regen favored the heavy kits: SECURITY won about 70% against TEMP and the INTERN, mostly on jabs and crosses plus the armored Shoulder Check. SECURITY is now health 100 (was 106), regen 0.85 (0.95), damage 0.86 (0.92), and Escort reaches 92 px for 10 damage and 30 stamina (116, 12, 24); COURIER is health 97 (90), weight 0.9 (0.88), regen 1.25 (1.15), damage 1.03 (0.97). Measured at CPU 0.7, 24 matches a pairing: SECURITY 63% v TEMP, 57% v COURIER, 43% v HELPDESK; COURIER 48% v TEMP; INTERN 41% v TEMP, 49% v THE MANAGER.

**Colors in a matchup.** Each character has a main and an alt color (`src/characters/`). P2 switches to its alt when P1 is the same fighter or when the two main colors are close in hue (`colorFor` in `src/characters/registry.js`; INTERN's amber against ACCOUNTANT's yellow read as one color), and to cyan or pink if even the alt clashes. TEMP against TEMP stays white against orange.

**Shoulder Check after the nine new characters (2026-11-09).** SECURITY's armored Shoulder Check landed about 80% of the time and he beat the CEO and the RECEPTIONIST around 65%. Its armor now takes 70% of the absorbed hit (was 50%) and it costs 30 stamina (was 26). At CPU 0.7, 30 matches a pairing: SECURITY 59% v TEMP, 53% v THE CEO, 54% v BARISTA, 55% v COURIER, 47% v RECEPTIONIST.

## Site

The address everyone has, `https://neonstickfight.com/`, is a traditional web page with the game playable in a frame near the top and the content around it; the game itself is one level down. Every page is a real HTML file in the build, so links share well and search engines can read them.

| Route | File | What it is |
|---|---|---|
| `/` | `index.html` | the homepage: header and nav (logo, Fighters, How to play, Online, About, GitHub, PLAY), the game in a 16:9 frame with FULL SCREEN and OPEN IN ITS OWN PAGE under it, the install hint, the story (a TEMP working their way up the tower) with a screenshot, life in the tower (the modes in the game's own voice), the cast (every fighter, linking to its page), the LIVE NOW strip (quick online matches anyone can watch, each a link to `play/?watch=CODE`; hidden while nothing is on; see Multiplayer, Spectators), the books (pick rates, win rates and the matchup chart, live from `api/stats`; see Analytics), how to play (the four buttons and links to the three guide pages), playing online, about and credits, footer |
| `/play/` | `play/index.html` | the game, unchanged (every `?scene=`, `?watch=`, `?pick=` and `#signin=` works here; `?crew=` and `?room=` are a 301 to the two below) |
| `/crew/<slug>` | `play/index.html`, from the Worker | the game, straight into that crew's lobby, with the crew's share tags (2026-11-11) |
| `/room/<CODE>` | `play/index.html`, from the Worker | the game, straight into that quick room, with the room's share tags (2026-11-11) |
| `/fighters/` | generated | the roster: a card per fighter with its idle loop (a sprite, see below), the tagline, health and unique moves |
| `/how-to-play/` | generated | the rules: the buttons, the rules list and the controls tables |
| `/how-to-play/philosophy/` | generated | the design pillars, written for players |
| `/how-to-play/strategy/` | generated | what beats what, stamina, punishing, the parry, the floor and the air, walls, and a tip per stage of the ladder |
| `/privacy/` | generated | the privacy notice, written for a 12-year-old (Privacy and terms) |
| `/terms/` | generated | the house rules: trash talk yes, bullying no, how to report (Privacy and terms) |
| `/fighters/<id>/` | generated | one fighter's page (`<id>` is the character file's id: `courier`, `windowWasher`, `hrRep`...), with its record from `api/stats` (ON THE BOOKS; see Analytics) |

**Fighter pages come from the character files, never written by hand.** `site/data.mjs` imports the registry in Node (the character files, the frame data and the rig need no DOM) exactly as the game does, then reads, for each registered character in roster order (TEMP included):
- name, tagline, colors (body, pants, accent, mirror) and the moveList;
- stats as bars against TEMP's (the tick on each bar), with the number and the difference;
- body: size, height, arms, legs, limb thickness and lean from `body`, and the reach of the jab, cross, snap kick and roundhouse measured the way the sim measures it (`calibrate()`: play the clip on that body and take the farthest hitbox while it's active);
- mechanics from the hooks its moves carry (armor, parry, throw counter, invulnerability, stance, status effects, shock, root, feint, side swap, command grab, juggle ender), `mechanics` (double jump, wall jump) and the CPU style;
- unique moves: one card per own move, with every input that presses it (its command, its place in a string, out of a stance, as a parry's or throw counter's reversal, a feint's follow-up), the moveList note, and frame data: damage (a multi-hit as `3+3+3+8`), height, startup, active and recovery frames, on block, stamina, reach or grab range, and properties (knockdown, launch, wall splat, armor, ducks highs, glides N px...). A card's animation plays the move on its rig in the character's colors, a string's earlier presses or a stance first (the clips the card names in `data-seq`), while it's on screen and again on a hover or tap;
- strategy: how to beat each unique move and unique string, from the character file's `strategy` field (STRATEGY, below);
- strings and enders: every string it keeps (unique ones first), its hits, the payoff, the damage the whole string does on TEMP with combo decay, and its stamina; dropped shared strings are named;
- shared basics (collapsed): the shared button table (`SHARED_INPUTS` in `src/combat/moveinfo.js`, shared with the game's MOVE LIST, which mirrors `resolveButton()` in `src/combat/sim.js` plus the stomp from `resolvePress()` and the wake-up strikes from `wakeFor()`; change them together), struck through where a command replaces one, then a line on the shared techniques with no button (parry, getting up, air recovery). The wake-up strikes and the stomp show no on block (nobody blocks a stomp; a wake-up strike springs from the floor, so its standing measurement is wrong, and its note carries getup-test's 17 to 24 frames minus) and the wake-up strikes no reach.

Timing is in 60fps frames from the rig's clips, as the sim reads them: startup is blend plus the active key's time less the sim's 50 ms pre-roll (it matches the measured numbers above: a jab 13 sim steps, Mandatory Training 27, Cold Call 19, Close the Deal 12); active runs from the active key to the recover key; recovery from there to the end of the clip; on block is blockstun less the frames from contact to the end of the clip (a multi-hit's from its last hit). On block is close, not measured: Fine Print comes out +3 against the +2.5 accountant-test measures. The happy accidents are never listed: the shared table and the character files are the only sources, and the techniques are in neither.

**STRATEGY: how to beat each move.** Every fighter page has a STRATEGY section after UNIQUE MOVES: one block per unique move and unique string, its name and input, then two to four short tips on beating it, in the player's terms (buttons 1 to 4, → toward the fighter and ← away, stand-block, crouch-block, "parry with ↘"). TEMP has no moves of its own, so its page covers the twelve shared strings, and every other page links there for them. It lives only on the fighter pages. The game's move list, HUD, pause menu and character select never show it: the tips are for players who go looking.
- **The field.** Each character file has `strategy`, next to `moveList`: `[{ move: 'expressSlide', tips: ['...', '...'] }, { string: 'SAME DAY', tips: [...] }]`. `move` is an own move's id, `string` a string's name, and `also: ['ticketReversal']` names own moves the same tips cover (a parry's reversal, a throw counter's throw). Write the field as `  strategy: [` on its own line, closed by `  ],` on its own line.
- **Only the site reads it.** `site/data.mjs` reads it in Node (`strategyOf`) and `site/templates.mjs` renders it (`strategySection`). `site/vite-plugin.mjs` cuts the field out of every character file the browser loads, so no bundle carries the tips, the game's included; a bundle that still holds one fails the build. The game's JavaScript is byte for byte what it was before the field existed.
- **Coverage is checked.** The build fails if a unique move or a unique string has no entry (a move inside a covered string counts as covered), if an entry names a move or string the fighter doesn't have, or if an entry has fewer than 2 or more than 4 tips.
- **Every tip is measured, not guessed.** `node gauntlet/counters.mjs <id>` (or `all`: about an hour on one core, so run a process per fighter) runs the real sim in Node, the way `scripts/netsim` does, with no browser, P1 the fighter and P2 a TEMP in the middle of the floor. P1 does the move the way a player presses it: a dash and a press, a string with each press buffered, a stance and its follow-up (pressed at once, and after holding it), a feint, and for a move that only sets up the next one (`FOLLOW` in the script: LAWYER's Cross-Examination, then a throw, a snap kick or an overhead) each thing that comes next. P2 tries every answer a player has, at three start distances across the move's reach:
  - **holds:** nothing, stand-block, crouch-block, crouch, and the right guard for each hit on reaction;
  - **timed:** a parry tap (→ and ↘) at every step, and a jump (up and back), a crouch held for a third of a second, and each of thirteen presses (1 2 3 4, ↓1 to ↓4, →1 →2 →4, ←4, a throw) at every second step from the start of the move (or of its dash) to its end. A string is blocked up to the press;
  - **after it:** behind each hold that survives it, every press at every second step from the last block (or from the step it whiffed) until he has recovered, standing still and stepping in first.

  An answer works when P1 is hit out of his move or his dash and P2 isn't touched (a hit on a P1 who had already recovered counts for nothing), when P2 parries or breaks a throw, or, for the crouch, when nothing touches P2. The script prints, per move, what each hold does, the window of steps where each timed answer works (from the step the move connects; 1 step is 1/120 s), which punishes land after it and for how long, and the tips on the page under those numbers, so a tip can be checked against its row. `--move <id or string name>` runs one, `--trace "crouch-block@80"` or `--trace "3@-10@180"` prints one exchange's states and log, `--json` the raw rows, `--list` the cases and which have tips.
- **Re-measure when the numbers change.** A tip is only true for the frame data it was measured on. After a change to a move's frame data, clip, reach or travel, to stamina, or to the guard, parry or botch rules, run `gauntlet/counters.mjs` for the fighters it touches (a change to a shared move touches all of them, since TEMP's shared buttons are the answers) and rewrite any tip its table no longer backs. Both sides are frame-perfect scripts, so a window in the table is the most a person can get: don't write a tip around a window of a step or two.
- **What the measuring turned up** (2026-11-11), worth knowing before writing a tip:
  - A guard's height is fixed for as long as blockstun lasts (`lowGuard` in `hit()`, `src/combat/sim.js`). Block a string's highs standing and a low ender that arrives inside that blockstun hits, whatever you hold; crouch-block from the start and the highs pass over your head instead.
  - A string only carries on from a strike that connected or was blocked. Duck its first high and the rest never comes out.
  - A press in the first part of your own blockstun is dropped; one in its last few frames is buffered and comes out on the first free step.

**How it's built.** `site/generate.mjs` writes `fighters/index.html`, `fighters/<id>/index.html`, the How to Play pages (`how-to-play/`, from `site/guide.mjs`), `privacy/` and `terms/` (from `site/legal.mjs`), `.site/home.json` (the homepage's fills) and the idle sprites in `public/sprites/`. `site/vite-plugin.mjs` runs it in a fresh Node process before every `vite build` and dev start, lists every page as a build input, fills the homepage's markers from `home.json` (`<!--@roster-->` the cast grid, `<!--@count-->` the fighter count, `<!--@stages-->` the arcade ladder's length, `<!--@fighters-->` the roster's ids, names and colors as JSON for the stats section; a marker with no fill fails the build), and in dev regenerates and reloads when a character file, the frame data, the rig, `src/flow/controls.js`, `src/flow/ladder.js` or the templates change (and redirects `/play` to `/play/` like the Worker does). `fighters/`, `how-to-play/`, `privacy/`, `terms/`, `.site/` and `public/sprites/` are build products (gitignored); run `node site/generate.mjs` to look at them by hand. Templates are plain template strings in `site/templates.mjs` (the header is `topbar()`; `index.html` carries a copy of it, so change both); styles in `site/site.css` (the game's neon: Bungee and Teko, glow only on the wordmarks and a page's one big title (Controls, The look), a plain sans for body copy and tables, square edges, each fighter's page in its own color). `site/site.js` runs on every page: the visit, the install hint, the homepage's FULL SCREEN button, and on a fighter page it loads `site/rigs.js`, which draws the move cards and the big rig live (`createFighter` + `play()`), only while they're on screen, never in a hidden tab, at a lower resolution on phones, and as still poses under `prefers-reduced-motion`. The homepage and the roster load none of the rig code.

**The homepage's content comes from the data where it can.** The cast cards are `rosterCards(..., { cast: true })`: name, tagline, a swatch each of body, pants and accent, and the first three entries of the character's `moveList`. The homepage's copy leads with the story: a TEMP working their way up the office tower, with the modes described in the game's voice. How the game was made (Xiao Xiao, the lighting, rollback) stays in About and Online, and the mechanics live on the How to Play pages. `site/guide.mjs` writes those three pages by hand from the README's numbers (Controls, Fundamentals), so change both together; they hint at the secret boss and the happy accidents but never name them. The controls tables on the rules page are `controlsTable()`, built from `FIGHT` and `MENUS` in `src/flow/controls.js` (the game's own CONTROLS screen table; `FACE` and `PILL` are exported for the gamepad glyphs) plus a touch column keyed by the row's label (`TOUCH` in `site/templates.mjs`, from `src/touch.js`'s layout; a row it doesn't know shows a dash). On phones each control row becomes a small card. The ladder hint reads `LADDER.length`; the secret boss and the happy accidents are hinted, never named.

**The embedded game.** The homepage's frame is just `<iframe src="./play/" allow="fullscreen; autoplay; gamepad" allowfullscreen>` showing the game's own attract demo, with nothing on top of it: a click into a same-origin frame focuses it (keys go to the game; the game's keys don't scroll the page) and is the gesture that turns its sound on, and the `gamepad` permission lets a pad work inside it (the policy's default is `self`, which covers a same-origin frame; the attribute says it out loud). The frame is as wide as the page allows, up to 1240px, but never taller than the screen under the header and the title: 280px comes off the height (`site/site.css`), measured at 900 to 1680 wide so the frame's bottom, where the character select's BACK and PICK sit, is on screen in a window with a bookmarks bar. It was 200px until 2026-11-12, which forgot the tagline wraps to four or five lines, and ran the frame 20px past a 1280x720 window. The title is as wide as the frame, so a shorter frame wraps it more; re-measure if the copy gets longer. The height is `svh` (the height with a phone's browser bars showing), with `vh` as the fallback. Under it, a quiet row: FULL SCREEN calls `requestFullscreen()` on the iframe and leaves the phone the way it's held (it locked landscape until 2026-11-11); where the element has no fullscreen (iPhone Safari) or the browser refuses, it opens `play/` instead. OPEN IN ITS OWN PAGE links `play/`. On a phone held upright (`(orientation: portrait) and (pointer: coarse)`) the frame is too small for thumbs: it still shows the demo, an invisible link over it opens `play/` on a tap (so a swipe there scrolls the page instead of landing in the game's touch layer), FULL SCREEN gives way to a big PLAY FULL SCREEN button, and the copy drops "click the game, then press Enter". A phone on its side gets a one-row header that scrolls away and a frame sized so header and frame fit the screen. Inside the frame the game only changes two things: `src/root.js` exports `EMBEDDED` (`window.top !== window`, in a try/catch, true if reading it throws), `src/install.js` then offers no INSTALL (the browser never fires `beforeinstallprompt` in a frame; the homepage around it has the install hint) and marks `<html class="embedded">`, and the visit is labeled `embed` (see Analytics). The redirect script never runs in the frame, since the frame loads `play/` itself. The update pill works inside the frame as on the game page. Note the cost: the frame runs the whole game, WebGL lighting and all, so on a slow phone the homepage costs what the game does (see the measurements below).

**Roster sprites.** The roster cards (the homepage's cast and `/fighters/`) don't run the rig in the browser: `site/sprites.mjs` draws each fighter's idle loop at build time with the game's own rig (`createFighter`, `setBody`, `draw`, in its body, colors, pants and the cards' glow and floor light) on [`@napi-rs/canvas`](https://github.com/Brooooooklyn/canvas) (Skia with prebuilt binaries for macOS and Linux, a devDependency, so `npm run build` needs no browser), into `public/sprites/<id>-idle.webp` (12 frames of 160x200 side by side) and `<id>-idle-2x.webp` (no `@` in the name: Cloudflare answers `@` with a redirect to `%40`). The idle clip is four 400 ms keys, so the loop is 1.6 s, sampled every 133 ms after 2.4 s of settling (checked: the joints at t and t+1.6 s match exactly). The sheets are drawn on the card's panel color (`PANEL` in `site/sprites.mjs`, `--panel` in `site.css`; keep the two together): an opaque sheet is about a fifth the size of one whose soft glow sits in a lossless alpha channel (WebP quality 75: about 17 KB at 1x and 38 KB at 2x per fighter). A card shows the sheet as a lazy `<img srcset>` twelve boxes wide inside an `overflow: hidden` box, slid a box at a time by a CSS `transform` animation with `steps(12)`, so the compositor runs it with no script and no repaint; cards start out of step (`--i`), and `prefers-reduced-motion` shows the first frame. A registered character that can't be drawn fails the build. The fighter pages keep their live rigs (measured cheap, below).

**Measured** (2026-11-09, `vite preview`, Playwright Chromium as a Pixel 7 with the CPU throttled 4x, cache off; an 8 s scripted scroll from top to bottom; two runs averaged). Main-thread time is Chrome's TaskDuration.

| Page | Main thread, load | Main thread, 8 s scroll | Frame rate scrolling | Bytes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| homepage, live rigs, with the game frame | 4.5 s | 8.1 s | 22 fps | 454 KB |
| homepage, sprites, with the game frame | 4.5 s | 8.2 s | 19 fps | 1,038 KB (all sprites loaded by the end) |
| homepage, live rigs, frame blanked | 0.29 s | 0.35 s | 105 fps | 212 KB |
| homepage, sprites, frame blanked | 0.29 s | 0.62 s | 120 fps | 280 KB at load, 796 KB by the end |
| `/fighters/`, live rigs | 0.36 s | 0.47 s | 66 fps | 97 KB |
| `/fighters/`, sprites | 0.10 s | 0.46 s | 120 fps | 681 KB |
| `/play/` alone | 4.3 s | 8.2 s | 20 fps | 243 KB |
| `/fighters/ceo/` (live move rigs) | 0.44 s | 0.41 s | 120 fps | 100 KB |

That table predates the performance pass, and its frame rates came from headless software GL, so they say nothing about a real phone; see Performance for the game's numbers since. The embedded game is what saturates a slow phone's main thread on the homepage, the same as `/play/` on its own; the cards are a small share either way. Sprites take script out of the cards entirely and hold the frame rate, at the price of image bytes.

**Share image.** `public/og.jpg` (1200x630) is a real frame of the game (COURIER against a fainted SECURITY, `?scene=ai-fight&p1=courier&p2=security`) under the title; `public/screenshot.jpg` is the same frame on the homepage. Every page has a title, a description, a canonical URL and Open Graph tags pointing at it, `play/` included (it had none before 2026-11-11, so a game link unfurled with no picture); the homepage adds `VideoGame` structured data.

**Crew and room links unfurl as that room** (2026-11-11, `worker/og.ts`). A link pasted into iMessage, Slack, Discord, WhatsApp, LinkedIn or X is fetched by a bot that reads the Open Graph tags and runs no script, so the Worker writes them. Since 2026-11-11 the links are clean: a crew's is `/stick-fight/crew/<slug>` and a quick room's `/stick-fight/room/<CODE>`. The Worker runs first for those (`run_worker_first` in `wrangler.jsonc`) and answers with a 200 and `play/index.html` from the assets, with no redirect, so the address bar keeps the clean link; it puts `<base href="/stick-fight/play/">` first in the head, so the page's `../src/...`, fonts, icons and manifest resolve as they do at `play/`. A slug in capitals or a code in lower case gets a 301 to the real one, and so does a trailing slash. For `crew/<slug>` it fetches the page and, at the same time, asks the crew's Durable Object for its name and member count (`Crew.card()`, a read that leaves no storage behind for a slug nobody made, and never a member's name or record), then swaps the tags with `HTMLRewriter`: `og:title` "Join TUESDAY CREW on Neon Stick Fight" (the name first, since iMessage cuts the line around 45 characters), `og:description` "Your crew's room on Neon Stick Fight, 6 fighters in the crew. Tap to join: no sign-up, pick a fighter and fight.", `og:url` the clean crew link, `og:image` the crew's own picture and `og:image:alt` naming the crew. Every client gets the same tags, with no user-agent sniffing; the page is otherwise the asset byte for byte, without its ETag. An unknown or expired crew, or a value that isn't a slug, gets the plain tags and still the game, never an error, and the game says NO CREW AT THAT LINK. `room/<CODE>` gets "Join room K7QM on Neon Stick Fight" and a picture of the code; it doesn't ask the room whether it's still open, since a room link is shared while the host waits.

- **The picture** (`GET /api/og/crew/<slug>.jpg?v=<v>`, `api/og/room/<CODE>.jpg`): 1200x630, the crew's name big in Bungee (as many as two lines, the biggest size up to 120 px that keeps it clear of the fighters; a 20-letter word gets 40 px), // CREW ROOM (or // QUICK MATCH), the member count, NEON STICK FIGHT and a square TAP TO JOIN chip, all solid with no glow (The look), over `public/og/bg.jpg`, the og.jpg frame with the fight on the right. The Worker writes it as SVG and renders it with resvg (`@resvg/resvg-wasm`), then encodes a JPEG with mozjpeg (`@jsquash/jpeg`): a PNG of that background is about 540 KB, past what WhatsApp shows, and the JPEG is 70 to 85 KB. The faces are `public/og/bungee-latin.ttf` and `teko-600-latin.ttf` (Latin subsets of the game's two, 12 and 10 KB; resvg can't read WOFF2), fetched from the assets once per isolate. resvg can't say how wide text came out, so the layout (`worker/og-layout.ts`) fits the name with each character's advance, measured from the same TTFs into `worker/og-metrics.ts`. `node scripts/og/build.mjs` remakes `bg.jpg` and the metrics; run it after changing the art or the fonts. Crew names are only A to Z, digits and spaces (`cleanName`), so a name typed with emoji or quotes arrives without them; the SVG and the tags are escaped anyway.
- **Caching.** A crew's name and member count are cached at the edge for 5 minutes (`api/og/card/<slug>`, 1 minute for no crew), so a link opened by a whole group chat asks the Durable Object once. The picture depends only on the name and the count, so its URL carries a hash of the two (`v`, with `LOOK` in `worker/og.ts`, bumped when the layout changes) and it's cached at the edge and in browsers for a week as `immutable`: a new member makes a new URL. A request with any other `v` gets a 302 to the current one, so a made-up version can't make the Worker draw. A room's picture depends only on its four letters, so it caches the same way even though rooms are short-lived. A render that isn't cached counts against `OG_LIMIT` (20 a minute per IP, namespace `7107`); over it, or for an unknown crew, the image URL sends `og.jpg` instead.
- **Old links** (2026-11-11). `/play/?crew=<slug>` and `/?crew=<slug>` are a 301 to `/crew/<slug>`, and `?room=CODE` on either to `/room/CODE`, with every other parameter kept (`play/?crew=lunch-7k2qm&pick=intern` goes to `crew/lunch-7k2qm?pick=intern`). Bots follow the 301 and unfurl the link as the room. A value that can't be a slug or a whole code isn't moved: `play/?room=AB` still opens JOIN to finish the code. On the old address, while it serves, the same 301s come back with `/stick-fight` in front; once it redirects, they go in one 301 straight to the clean link on neonstickfight.com (Hosting, The move to neonstickfight.com). Share tags always name neonstickfight.com, even on a page served from the old address.
- **What it costs.** The Worker's upload went from 73 KB to 2.8 MB (1.0 MB gzipped): resvg's WebAssembly is 2.4 MB and mozjpeg's 250 KB. `wrangler check startup` shows the same startup as before (a 17 to 21 ms window against 12 to 17, no script time in either). Measured locally (`wrangler dev`, 200 requests each, two rounds, median), the hop through the script costs about a millisecond: `play/` 2.3 and 2.9 ms before, 3.1 and 3.7 ms after; the homepage 2.6 and 2.8 ms before, 3.6 and 3.5 ms after; `play/?crew=` with its card cached 2.8 and 2.7 ms before, 3.8 and 3.7 ms after. A first render takes about 150 ms locally. Not measured on Cloudflare's edge.
- **Checking it.** `npm run test:unfurl` (`scripts/og/check.mjs`) fits 20,000 names, then against `wrangler dev` checks that `crew/<slug>` is a 200 with the game page, that every relative path in it loads through its `<base>`, the tags it gives eight user agents (the same for all), the member count with no member names, the defaults (still a 200 and the game) for an unknown crew, an expired one (`DEV_HOOKS=1`), a junk value and a bad room code, plain `play/` untouched, a `room/<CODE>` link, the 301s for every old form with the rest of the query kept, a bot following one landing on the crew's tags, and that each picture is a 1200x630 JPEG under 300 KB. 37 checks, all passing (2026-11-11). `node scripts/og/mock.mjs <base> <dir> <query>...` draws a mock iMessage bubble and Slack unfurl for any links, from the tags the Worker serves.

**Old links.** The game used to be the homepage, so an inline script at the top of `index.html` forwards to `play/` with the query and hash intact when the URL has a game parameter (`room`, `crew`, `scene`, `pick`, `p1`, `p2`, `mirror`, `botch`, `tier`, `perf`, `relay`, `ice`, `netsim`, `blend`) or a `#signin=` / `#code=` hash, and whenever it's running as an installed app. For `crew` and `room` the Worker answers with a 301 to the clean link before the page loads, so unfurl bots get there too (above). New share links are the clean ones: `src/links.js` builds `crew/<slug>` and `room/<CODE>` off the app's root (`crewLink()` in `src/net/crew.js`, `roomLink()` in `src/flow/netApi.js`, the host's link in `src/net/online.js`), so COPY LINK, SHARE, the QR codes and the link shown on screen all carry them, and `cleanSlug()` takes a pasted clean link or an old `?crew=` one. `worker/auth.ts` builds the sign-in link as `/stick-fight/play/#signin=...`.

**PLAY AS.** A fighter page's button opens `play/?pick=<id>`: the game starts on the title as usual and the select screen opens with that fighter under the cursor.

## Analytics

Cookieless and with no player ids, names or emails (what the game keeps, all of it, is under Privacy and terms, and the player-facing version is `/privacy/`): the page sends small events to its own Worker (`src/analytics.js` → `POST /api/e`), which writes them to Cloudflare **Workers Analytics Engine**, dataset `stick_fight_events` (binding `EVENTS` in `wrangler.jsonc`). Off on `?scene=` pages, on localhost, and when the browser sends Do Not Track or Global Privacy Control (the latter since 2026-11-11). The Worker only accepts a fixed list of event names, cuts labels short, and rate-limits per IP (`EVENT_LIMIT`, 60 requests a minute). One request carries one event or a list of up to 12 (a match's end sends its result and its move counts together).

| Event (blob1) | Labels (blob2…) | Numbers (double1…) | Sent by |
|---|---|---|---|
| `visit` | touch or mouse, app or web, referrer host, direct / crew-link / room-link (a `crew/<slug>` or `room/<CODE>` address, or the old `?crew=` / `?room=` forms, which reach the page through the 301 as clean ones), page (`play`, `home`, `fighters`, `fighter:<id>`, or `embed` for the game inside the homepage's frame, so a homepage visit counts once as `home` and isn't also a `play`) | | page load, on every page |
| `install` | | | the browser installed the app |
| `mode` | arcade / horde / training, fighter | | starting a mode |
| `pick` | mode (`arcade`, `horde`, `training`, `dummy` for the training dummy, `quick`, `crew`), fighter | | a fighter chosen at any character select: the three offline modes, the training dummy, and each player's locked-in pick on the online pick screen. Also counted in the `stats_picks` rollup |
| `match` | mode (`arcade`, `horde`, `quick`, `crew`), P1's fighter, P2's fighter (`goons` in horde), result (`p1`, `p2`, `draw`; `run` for horde; `quit` for an arcade stage cut short), `cpu` or `pvp`, how the last round ended (`ko`, `time`, or `perfect`: a KO by a winner at full health), the move that landed the last KO | 1 match length in seconds, 2 P1's rounds, 3 P2's rounds, 4 the CPU's level (0 to 1; 0 online), 5 P1's hits landed, 6 P2's, 7 P1's best combo, 8 P2's, 9 P1's perfect rounds, 10 P2's, 11 mirror match (1 or 0), 12 the arcade stage, or the floors cleared in horde | one per match (a match, not a round): each arcade stage fought, finished or cut short (`quit`), each horde run however it ends, each online match with an agreed result. See Match bookkeeping for who sends it |
| `match_reject` | mode, P1's fighter, P2's fighter | | the quick room, when the two players' reports of one match differ (nothing else is recorded for it) |
| `move` | fighter, move id, mode | 1 times used, 2 times it landed (a multi-hit counts once) | at a match's end, one per unique move the local player used (only the character's own moves, never the shared ones) |
| `technique` | technique id, fighter | | a happy accident found for the first time on this browser |
| `stage` | fighter | stage reached | arcade |
| `arcade_end` | fighter, over / clear | last stage | arcade |
| `horde_end` | fighter | floors cleared | horde |
| `online_match` | host's fighter, guest's fighter, crew / quick | match number | the host, once per match, at the start (no result; `match` has it) |
| `online_drop` | `peer-left` or `lost`, crew / quick, this side's fighter, the other's, which check gave up (`no-packets`: nothing for 15 s; `broken:history`: the other side stopped confirming inputs; `stalled`: WAITING FOR RIVAL 20 s; `silent`: nothing between matches for 15 s; `end-wait`: no agreement on the final state; `ws:<reason>:<close code>`: the room's socket closed; `rival:<its why>`: the other side gave up first and said why), the link as `<mode>:<candidate>` (`p2p:srflx`, `relay:-`) | 1 seconds into the match; 2 the frame, 3 round trip ms, 4 ms since the last packet, 5 ms stalled, 6 resyncs, 7 1 when desynced, 8 the other side's newest frame, 9 the newest of ours it confirmed | a page whose online match ended without a result (the rival left, or the connection was lost). The why and the numbers since 2026-11-12, after a crew match between Chrome and Safari dropped three times in a row with nothing but `lost` to go on |
| `room_create`, `crew_create` | | | the Worker |
| `watch_join` | | | the Worker, each time a spectator's socket opens (README, Spectators) |
| `crew_match` | host's fighter, guest's fighter, win / draw | | the crew room, per result in the crew's own standings (the host's word, as before; the public numbers use `match`) |
| `play` (no data point) | | | training's time played, a heartbeat `{ e: 'play', m: { mode: 'training', secs } }` a minute while a training fight is on screen and when it stops; the Worker adds it to `stats_time` and writes nothing to Analytics Engine (Play time, below) |
| `perf` | the tier the session settled on (`t0`..`t3`), the tier it started on (`start t1`), touch or mouse, frame time p95 (`p95 18`, ms), the device's coarse buckets (`os cros`, `gpu intel`, `cores 4 mem le4`: see below), what held the frame back (`bound main`, `bound gpu`, or `bound none` when it holds ~60 fps), the GRAPHICS choice (`set auto`, `set 0`..`set 3`, or `set url` for a `?tier=` link) | 1 frame time p50, ms; 2 the main thread's work in a frame, p50; 3 to 6 its split: sim, 2D draw, GL, HUD (p50 each); 7 drawing every other frame (2) or every frame (1) | the game, once a session: 30 s in with the tier unchanged for 15 s, or sooner (after 10 s) when the tab is hidden or closed (see Performance) |

The last blob is always the visitor's country (from Cloudflare; empty for events a Durable Object writes), so its number depends on how many labels the event has: `blob9` for `match`. Query with the SQL API using a token with **Account Analytics Read**:

```sh
curl -s https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/accounts/0223b96fe77599b23ff8ec7fcd32e2f1/analytics_engine/sql \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" \
  -d "SELECT blob1 AS event, count() AS n FROM stick_fight_events WHERE timestamp > NOW() - INTERVAL '7' DAY GROUP BY event ORDER BY n DESC"
```

Data is kept for three months. Analytics Engine samples at high volume, so sum `_sample_interval` instead of `count()` when the numbers get big. Some examples:

```sql
-- favorite fighters, by mode
SELECT blob2 AS mode, blob3 AS fighter, SUM(_sample_interval) AS picks FROM stick_fight_events WHERE blob1 = 'pick' GROUP BY mode, fighter ORDER BY picks DESC

-- the matchup matrix, player against player, last 30 days: one row per ordered pair as it was played
-- (host's fighter, guest's fighter). Mirror matches (double11 = 1) are left out.
SELECT blob3 AS p1, blob4 AS p2,
       SUM(_sample_interval) AS games,
       SUM(IF(blob5 = 'p1', _sample_interval, 0)) AS p1_wins,
       SUM(IF(blob5 = 'p2', _sample_interval, 0)) AS p2_wins,
       SUM(IF(blob5 = 'draw', _sample_interval, 0)) AS draws,
       SUM(double1 * _sample_interval) / SUM(_sample_interval) AS avg_seconds
FROM stick_fight_events
WHERE blob1 = 'match' AND blob6 = 'pvp' AND double11 = 0 AND timestamp > NOW() - INTERVAL '30' DAY
GROUP BY p1, p2 ORDER BY games DESC

-- the same against the CPU (blob6 = 'cpu' AND blob2 = 'arcade' AND blob5 != 'quit': a stage cut short is play time, not a game):
-- p1 is the player, p2 the CPU, and GROUP BY p1, p2, double4 splits it by CPU level

-- how matches end, and with what
SELECT blob7 AS how, blob8 AS move, SUM(_sample_interval) AS n FROM stick_fight_events WHERE blob1 = 'match' AND blob2 != 'horde' GROUP BY how, move ORDER BY n DESC

-- each unique move: how often it's thrown and how often it lands
SELECT blob2 AS fighter, blob3 AS move, SUM(double1 * _sample_interval) AS used, SUM(double2 * _sample_interval) AS landed FROM stick_fight_events WHERE blob1 = 'move' GROUP BY fighter, move ORDER BY used DESC

-- which devices struggle (perf: blob2 settled tier, blob3 start tier, blob6 os, blob7 gpu, blob8 cores and memory,
-- blob9 what held the frame back; double1 frame p50 ms, double2 main-thread work p50 ms): Chromebooks first
SELECT blob6 AS os, blob7 AS gpu, blob8 AS cores_mem, blob2 AS tier, blob9 AS bound,
       SUM(_sample_interval) AS sessions,
       SUM(double1 * _sample_interval) / SUM(_sample_interval) AS frame_p50,
       SUM(double2 * _sample_interval) / SUM(_sample_interval) AS work_p50
FROM stick_fight_events
WHERE blob1 = 'perf' AND timestamp > NOW() - INTERVAL '30' DAY
GROUP BY os, gpu, cores_mem, tier, bound ORDER BY os = 'os cros' DESC, sessions DESC
```

The Worker keeps up to 10 labels an event (`track` in `worker/env.ts`); `perf` is the only one past 8.

The all-time pair totals don't need the SQL API: they're in D1 (below), and `GET /api/stats` returns them.

**Match bookkeeping** (`src/bookkeeping.js` on the page, `worker/stats.ts` on the Worker). The page counts hits, combos, perfect rounds and unique moves from the sim's events while a match runs, and reads the result from the sim when it ends. Where the result goes depends on how far it can be trusted:

- **Arcade (vs CPU).** The flow begins a match in the books when a stage's scene starts and ends it on the result screen, with `POST api/e` `{ e: 'match', m: { mode, p1, p2, wins, secs, how, move, level, stage, hits, combo, perfects } }`. The Worker checks every field (known fighters, a known mode, numbers clamped) but can't check the result itself, so these are always labeled `cpu` and never mixed into the player-vs-player numbers. A stage cut short (Cut short, below) goes out with `quit: 1`. A continue retries the stage as a new match.
- **Horde.** One `match` per run, for its length, hits and floors, however it ends: the hero going down, or cut short (below), which counts the same, since a run has no win or loss. A run has no opposing fighter, so it goes to Analytics Engine and play time only and stays out of the matchups (its picks still count).
- **Training** has no result. Its picks count, the dummy's separately.
- **Online: both players have to agree.** The two sims already end a match on the same confirmed frame with the same state hash (Multiplayer, Between matches). Each page then sends its room `{ t: 'sys', ev: 'result', n, seed, mode, chars, wins, f, how, x }` over the room's WebSocket. A crew match runs in a quick room too, so this one path covers both. The room (`Room` in `worker/index.ts`) keeps a `sys` message for itself, never passing it to the other player. It stores the first report and, when the other side's arrives for the same match (same number and seed), compares what both must agree on: the match number, the seed, the mode, both fighters, the rounds each won, the frame the result showed on (`f`, which gives the length) and how the last round ended. Everything in that list is read from the final sim state, which both pages hold identically. If the two match, the room writes one `match` event and one rollup upsert, and remembers the match so a resend can't count twice. If they differ it writes `match_reject` and nothing else. A report whose other half never comes (a rival who left, a page with Do Not Track on, an old build) is dropped after 5 minutes or when the room ends. One page alone can never put a result on the books; two pages working together can, which is the same as playing the match.
- **The details in `x`** (the finishing move, hits, best combo, perfect rounds) come from each page's own count of the sim's effects. Rollback can leave those a hit apart between the two pages (Rollback, Effects), so they aren't part of the agreement; the room takes the host's.
- **The crew room** still keeps its own standings from the host's report (`crew_match`, Crew rooms, Recording). The public numbers don't read them.
- **Cut short** (2026-11-12, `cut()` in `src/bookkeeping.js`). A match that doesn't reach its result screen still counts, once, as it stands: quitting from the pause menu (MAIN MENU, RESTART STAGE, RESTART RUN), the attract timeout, any scene change (`setScene` calls `book.cut()`), a new match beginning, and the tab closing (`pagehide`). A horde run goes out exactly as if the hero had gone down (floors cleared, hits, best combo, length). An arcade stage goes out with `quit: 1`, its length and the rounds finished so far; the Worker writes its `match` data point with result `quit` and adds its seconds to `stats_time` with no match finished, and never touches `stats_matchups`, because a quit isn't a loss to that CPU. The `offlineMatch` caps still apply (an hour at most). An online match sends only its move counts from the page: its time comes from the room (below). Less than 5 seconds of fighting since the first FIGHT (`MIN_FIGHT_SECS`) counts for nothing, so bouncing through the menus adds no noise. A page that comes back from the bfcache after `pagehide` carries on with its match off the books, so nothing is sent twice. **A hidden tab** (mobile Safari can kill one without a `pagehide`) keeps the match as it stands in localStorage (`stick-fight-book-<page token>`, `book.hide()`), sends nothing, and takes it back when it's visible again; the next page load sends a leftover once (`sendLeftovers()`), and only when the page that left it is gone: each page holds a Web Lock named for its token while it lives, so a second tab never sends a hidden one's match. A leftover over a day old is dropped. **High scores don't change**: a cut-short run never reaches GAME OVER and the initials screen, so it never lands on a local or crew board.
- **Online time without a result** (2026-11-12, `roomLive()` and `roomSettle()` in `worker/stats.ts`). Both pages tell their room a match started (`{ t: 'sys', ev: 'live', n, chars }`; the guest's word counts for this, the live count still takes only the host's). The room keeps one small `clock` entry: when it first heard, the match number, and which sides said so. It stops at the first result report or when a player leaves. If both pages agree on the result, the match's own length counts as before and the clock is dropped; a `match_reject` counts no time at all. Otherwise (a rival who left, a lost link, a report that never got its other half) the room adds the seconds it saw the match running to `stats_time` (`quick` or `crew`, no match finished), capped at an hour and nothing under 8 seconds (`ROOM_MIN_SECS`: the intro plus about 5 s of fighting). The number is the room's own clock, never a page's, and it can't run longer than both pages stay connected. A page with Do Not Track says nothing, so the clock never has both sides and the match stays off the books for both, as for a result. Win or loss still needs both pages to agree, unchanged.
- **Do Not Track** turns all of it off for that browser, so an online match with a player who asks not to be tracked stays off the books for both. Since 2026-11-12 such a page still sends its room the result, flagged `dnt: 1`, because a signed-in rival's career is paid from the agreed result (Progression); the room compares it as before, pays the careers, and writes no `match` event and no rollup.

**Rollups (D1).** The numbers the site shows are all-time totals in the `stick-fight-auth` D1 database, next to the profiles (`migrations/0003_stats.sql`). One database is simpler than two: the same binding, the same migration step in CI, and the stats tables hold no player data to keep apart. Analytics Engine keeps the raw events for three months; the rollups never expire and cost the homepage no SQL API call.

| Table | Key | Columns | Written |
|---|---|---|---|
| `stats_picks` | `fighter`, `mode` | `picks` | one upsert per `pick` |
| `stats_matchups` | `a`, `b`, `vs` | `games`, `a_wins`, `b_wins`, `draws`, `secs` (total length), `a_rounds`, `b_rounds`, `a_perfects`, `b_perfects` (matches closed with a perfect round), `timeouts` (last round ran out the clock) | one upsert per finished match |
| `stats_finishers` | `move` | `kos` (matches that move finished) | one upsert per finished arcade stage or agreed online match that ended on a KO with a winner (not a horde run, a stage cut short, a time-out or a draw); 2026-11-12, for the title's ticker |
| `stats_time` | `day` (UTC, `YYYY-MM-DD`), `mode` | `secs`, `matches` | one upsert per finished match (horde runs included, however they end), one per arcade stage cut short and online match with no agreed result (seconds only, `matches` unchanged), and one per training heartbeat (Play time, below) |

In `stats_matchups`, `vs` is `cpu` or `pvp`. For `cpu`, `a` is the player's fighter and `b` the CPU's, so COURIER against the CPU's SECURITY and SECURITY against the CPU's COURIER are two rows. For `pvp` each pair is stored once with `a <= b`. `a = b` is a mirror match. A fighter's wins and losses are sums over these rows, so a match is exactly one upsert. Against the CPU a mirror counts toward the fighter (it's a person against the CPU); online it doesn't (it says nothing about the fighter) and is reported as `mirrors`. Both tables are `WITHOUT ROWID`, which keeps an upsert to one row written. Nothing in them names a player or a country. `worker/roster.ts` lists the fighter ids the Worker accepts, so made-up names can't fill the tables; `site/generate.mjs` fails the build if that list and the game's roster differ.

**`GET /api/stats`** is public JSON built from the rollups:

```jsonc
{
  "at": 1794332265286, "ttl": 600,
  "totals": { "cpu": { "games": 6466, "secs": 363254, "timeouts": 526 }, "pvp": { ... } },
  "fighters": { "courier": {
    "picks": { "arcade": 354, "horde": 136, "training": 100, "dummy": 44, "quick": 117, "crew": 53 },
    "cpu": { "games": 488, "wins": 359, "losses": 115, "draws": 14, "secs": 27256, "perfects": 18, "mirrors": 0 },
    "pvp": { "games": 268, "wins": 180, "losses": 78, "draws": 10, "secs": 16087, "perfects": 8, "mirrors": 22 } } },
  "matchups": { "cpu": [["courier", "security", 71, 38, 29, 4, 4686]], "pvp": [ ... ] },  // a, b, games, a's wins, b's wins, draws, seconds
  "time": { "day": "2026-11-11",                                          // play time (below); today is this UTC day
    "all":   { "secs": 912340, "matches": 15230,
               "mode": { "arcade": { "secs": 401200, "matches": 7100 }, "horde": { ... }, "training": { "secs": 88000, "matches": 0 }, "quick": { ... }, "crew": { ... } },
               "vs": { "cpu": { ... }, "pvp": { ... } } },                // cpu: arcade, horde, training; pvp: quick, crew
    "today": { ...the same shape, for today only } },
  "finishers": [["wellness_craneKick", 88], ["sweep", 60]]                // the moves that finished the most matches, at most 12, most first
}
```

The Worker keeps the answer in the edge cache (the Cache API, `s-maxage=600`, one entry per Cloudflare location whatever query string is tacked on) and tells browsers to keep it a minute, so a busy homepage reads D1 at most once per location every ten minutes. It's rate-limited per IP like the other public endpoints (`STATS_LIMIT`, namespace `7106`, 60 a minute). If D1 can't be read it answers 503 and caches nothing.

**Play time** (2026-11-11, `migrations/0004_playtime.sql`, `recordTime()` in `worker/stats.ts`). Every match that reaches the books also adds its length to `stats_time`, one row per UTC day and mode (`arcade`, `horde`, `training`, `quick`, `crew`), so the site can show minutes, hours and days played, all time and today. It's fed by the same path as `stats_matchups`: an arcade stage and a horde run from the page's `match` report (a horde run counts here though it stays out of the matchups; an arcade stage cut short counts its seconds and no match), and an online match from its agreed result in the room, or, when there's no agreed result, from the seconds the room's own clock saw it running (Match bookkeeping, Cut short and Online time without a result, above), with `crew` for a crew match. Training has no match, so the page sends the time spent in a training fight as a heartbeat (`{ e: 'play', m: { mode: 'training', secs } }`) once a minute and when it stops: a pause, leaving, a hidden tab or closing the page; a pause and a hidden tab don't count. The Worker takes `training` only from it (every other mode's time comes with its match), cuts each to 90 seconds, and `EVENT_LIMIT` caps how often a browser may send one, so a script can add at most 90 minutes a minute per IP. Do Not Track turns it off like every other event. No row holds a player id, name, email or country. `api/stats` reports the totals under `time` (above); the homepage doesn't show them yet.

**The live count: `GET /api/now`** (2026-11-11, `Lobby.count()` in `worker/index.ts`), groundwork for a ticker of matches going on right now:

```jsonc
{ "at": 1794414266472, "matches": 3, "quick": 2, "crew": 1, "pairs": [["courier", "intern"], ["ceo", "barista"], ["lawyer", "chef"]] }   // newest first, at most 40 pairs
```

Every match counts, quick or crew, watchable or not, and nothing in it names a player, a room or a crew. It's built from what the rooms already know, without asking every Durable Object: when a match starts, the host's page tells its quick room `{ t: 'sys', ev: 'live', chars }` (a crew match runs in a quick room too, and the room knows it's a crew's from how it was claimed), and the room puts one entry, keyed by its code, into the one `Lobby` object that already keeps the LIVE list. The entry comes out when either page reports the result, or when a player leaves or the room closes; one left behind by a room that died quietly drops off after 20 minutes. That's one storage write when a match starts and one delete when it ends. A guest's word is ignored, and like a result, it's a `sys` message the room keeps for itself, never passed to the other player.
