- **Skipping it.** A fresh press of any button (1 to 4, jump or guard; one held when it starts doesn't count) after its first 0.3 s skips to the end. Offline any player's press skips it, never a CPU's. Online only the winner's press does (`sim.perfectSkip = 'winner'` in the `online` scene): it's the winner's show, and a press is part of the inputs both sims already share, so both skip on the same frame. A skip cuts the squeak.
- **On another level.** When the body landed on another floor or a flight the winner can't walk to (`sameLevel()`), he plays the throw where he stands and the body leaves the floor where it lies at the release.
- **How it's drawn** (`src/fx/perfect.js`, drawn only; it never writes to the sim). From the grab to the release the body is the loser's own rig drawn with joints put together in its hand, on the figures layer so the room lights it like the winner: hung by the collar or the ankle on a damped pendulum that the hand's acceleration swings, or laid across the hands (`carry`). From the release it's drawn on the HUD canvas in screen space, under the HUD, so the bars stay on top and the PERFECT banner moves up into the strip. In flight its depth closes linearly, so it grows the way something coming at you does, with whole-turn spins or flips that land upright, its arc, two fading copies behind it and speed lines from the middle of the screen. On the glass it's sized to 92% of the screen's height. Fighters stay flat solid color. Only the glass marks glow: the crack, the streaks the hands and head leave, and the stamp. The splat also has a white flash and its own shake on top of the world's. The 16:9 stage letterboxes on a phone either way up, so it works on phones in both orientations.
- **The throw comes at you** (2026-11-12, drawn only). The winner turns from the side view toward the lens as he winds up, is square to it (or close) at the release, and turns partway back for the pose he holds. Each key of the clip can carry `face` (0 the side view, 1 square to the camera), blended between keys like the other fields. `rig.draw` (`src/fighter/index.js`, `turnNow` and `drawTurned`) reads it from the key list and turns the joints it was handed into a reused map: it treats the side view as the body's forward axis and spins it about the upright through the chest, so the shoulders and hips come apart (the far arm and leg come out from behind), the torso shows its breadth, and whatever reached forward now reaches at you. A slight perspective (230 px to the eye at scale 1) makes it foreshortened: the throwing arm gets shorter on screen and its hand and forearm larger and a little lower. The limbs are drawn far to near by that depth, so an arm thrown at you is drawn over the chest. Accessories and cloth get the turned joints and a facing eased toward the camera (a cap's brim shortens); the cloth hangs at rest while he's turned. Around the `release` key he steps at the lens: up to 6% bigger about the top of his head and 8 px lower, in over 180 ms, held 100, eased back over 520, so his head never rises into the HUD strip. The camera frames the turned joints (`fitCameraToDrawn` and `drawnPoints` ask `rig.turnPoints`), and `scripts/shots/square-fit.mjs --scenes perfect-test --p1 <id>` keeps every joint of all 18 inside the square at 390x844 and 412x640. Nothing of it reaches `f.pose`, `f.joints`, the sim or the hash: `npm run netsim:proof` is identical to the build before, and `NET_VERSION` and `PERFECT` didn't change. The body in his hand goes where the hand is drawn, at the hand's size (`rig.turned`), and the flight starts from exactly there and that size; its spin and flip wait out the first 12% of the flight, so the body leaves the hand clearly in front of him before it turns (a pancake flip used to go edge-on, and so vanish, the frame after the release). Before and after: `node scripts/shots/perfect-sheet.mjs` (six moments through the windup, the release and the early flight, one row per fighter; `--size 390x844`, `--video <ids>` for frames to join).
- **Sound** (`src/audio/sfx.js`): the throw's swelling whoosh (`perfectWhoosh`), the body on the glass (`glassThud`: a padded thud, the pane's ring and the crack running out) with the music ducked under it, the squeak (`squeak`: rubber on glass, a squeal that jumps up in pitch on each of the slide's six slips and sags as it goes down, on the same beat the drawing slides on), and a little `unstick` as it peels off.

| Fighter | Finisher | Hold | On the glass | Stamp |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| TEMP | TWO-HANDED HEAVE: swung back between the knees and heaved over, a stagger, a stunned fist up | collar | one turn | CONTRACT ENDED |
| INTERN | ACCIDENTAL LOB: it slips, he juggles it, a panicked underhand lob, hands on his head | collar | two turns, a high wobbly arc | OOPS |
| HELPDESK | OFF AND ON AGAIN: a full spin and a release on the way round, a calm thumbs-up | collar | three turns; it blinks off and on | TICKET CLOSED |
| COURIER | SAME-DAY DROP KICK: carried like a parcel, tossed up and drop-kicked, a tip of the cap | carry | one turn, flat | DELIVERED |
| SECURITY | FACE-FIRST ESCORT: two marching steps and a two-handed shove, arms folded | collar | dead straight | ESCORTED OUT |
| JANITOR | MOP-HANDLE DRIVE: by the ankle, a full golf backswing and a drive, the follow-through held | ankle | two turns of backspin | FORE! |
| THE MANAGER | PERFORMANCE REVIEW FLING: held at eye level, a head shake, a sidearm fling, the tie and a point | collar | one turn | NEEDS IMPROVEMENT |
| ACCOUNTANT | PRECISE UNDERHAND: a measured half step, a sight down the pencil, an underhand lob | collar | no spin at all | AUDITED |
| WINDOW WASHER | SQUEEGEE PUSH: a wax-on circle, a two-armed push | collar | his squeegee pushes it across the glass on the way down | STREAK FREE |
| HR REP | MANDATORY OFFBOARDING: the presentation rise and the long two-handed shove, the clipboard | collar | one turn | OFFBOARDED |
| SALES REP | CLOSE THE DEAL: three handshake pumps and the rising phone-hand uppercut, finger guns | collar (rear hand) | one turn, a high arc | SOLD! |
| BARISTA | LATTE POUR: raised high and tipped, a wiggling pour-flick, the shaker | carry | a high arc that wobbles like a pour | EXTRA SHOT |
| CHEF | PANCAKE FLIP: a dip on the spatula hand and a flip, the chef's kiss | carry (rear hand) | two flips | ORDER UP |
| WELLNESS COACH | SALUTE TO THE LENS: overhead in tree pose, a pushing exhale, a bow | carry | a soft arc | NAMASTE |
| ELECTRICIAN | LIVE CIRCUIT FLICK: a few jolts from the tester hand, a flick, the boogaloo wave | collar | one turn, jittering | GROUNDED |
| RECEPTIONIST | TRANSFER TO THE LENS: heaved overhead and thrown down and forward, phone back at the ear | carry | one turn | TRANSFERRED |
| LAWYER | CASE CLOSED: the briefcase wound back and swung into it, the point, the lapel tug | collar | one turn | CASE CLOSED |
| THE CEO | GOLDEN PARACHUTE: a bored backhand push from arm's length, the tie straightened | collar | a gold parachute opens and it floats down slower | LET GO |

**Tested.** `?scene=perfect-test&p1=<id>` puts any fighter against a dummy that never fights back, with a low health bar, so every round is a perfect KO and ends in the finisher (`&p2=<id>&p2win=1` makes P2 the winner instead, `&hurt=1` takes a point off the winner each round, which is the same KO with no finisher). In Node, all 18 run the finisher and go on to the next round and the match end; with `hurt=1` the event log is the plain KO's (no `perfect` events, the next round 0.35 s after the body rests). Skipping, in Node: a player's press 0.6 s in skips it offline whether he won or lost; a CPU winner's presses never do; online (`net` on both, `perfectSkip = 'winner'`) the winner's press skips it and the loser's doesn't. Against `main`, a non-perfect KO is unchanged: the hurt `perfect-test` bout for TEMP, CHEF, THE CEO and JANITOR (3,000 steps each) and three CPU fights at 0.7 (THE MANAGER v WELLNESS COACH, CHEF v RECEPTIONIST, SECURITY v COURIER, 6,000 steps each, none of them a perfect round) gave the same positions, velocities, states, health, stamina, rng and every joint at every 50th step. Save and restore: a recorded `perfect-test` bout per fighter (2,400 steps each, both units on `net` controllers, the loser pressing buttons that mustn't skip and the winner skipping now and then) replayed with 1,260 rollbacks of 1 to 16 steps, 338 of them saved inside a finisher: all 53,825 restored steps matched a deep hash of `saveState()`. States saved mid-finisher, sent as text (`src/net/state.js`) and loaded into a sim that had played something else, matched for all 12,166 steps after.

**High scores.** A top-5 table for arcade and one for horde, kept in localStorage as `stick-fight-scores` (the prefix is from when the origin was shared with askscottpierce.com). After a game the table has CONTINUE (back to the title) and MAIN MENU; from the title or the attract loop, MAIN MENU and TITLE; either way a third button swaps the arcade and horde tables. The attract loop's table turns over after 7 seconds; one the player opened or just signed waits for 30 idle seconds. A score that makes the table gets three initials: type them, or pick them on the on-screen keyboard (D-pad and A, or tap), then ENTER. DEL (Backspace, pad X) steps back a slot. Left alone for 30 seconds the initials go in as they stand. A device with crews on its list also has the crews' own tables (Multiplayer, Crew rooms, High scores): a run that makes one of those gets the initials too (LUNCH CREW · 2ND PLACE), the initials screen says which crews the run goes to, the high score screen has a button that steps through this device's tables and each crew's, and the attract loop's tables go this device's ARCADE and HORDE, then the first crew's (LUNCH CREW · ARCADE, LUNCH CREW · HORDE) when it has scores.

**What ended the round is the sim's to say** (2026-11-11). The flow never works out a round's end from `sim.winner` and the mode. It reads `sim.result` (`{ over, winner, reason, final }`, Fundamentals, How a KO resolves): `over` moves fighting to roundEnd and back when the next round starts, `final` (the match or the run is over) moves to result, `winner` decides the round bonus, and `decide()` goes to GAME OVER in horde on `reason: 'heroDown'` (any other reason there logs a warning; the sim's rules don't produce one). A horde goon counts when the sim sends `goonDown`. The test hook `__flow.debug.debugKO(winner)` goes through the same resolution (`sim.forceKO`), so in horde `debugKO(1)` is a goon putting the hero down and `debugKO(0)` the hero putting a goon down.

**Horde** is the floor-by-floor mode, scored the same way. It's endless: the goons keep coming, floor after floor, and the run ends only when the hero goes down, then GAME OVER, initials and the horde table. It has no clock (the TIME rule doesn't apply; the ring shows ∞), and the hero gets his breath back: once he's gone 3 seconds without losing health he heals 1 a second, up to a full bar, and any hit starts the wait over (`HORDE_REGEN`, `HORDE_REGEN_DELAY` in `src/combat/sim.js`). Each goon he puts down still gives back its own chunk (`heal`). Before this a run could also end on TIME: the clock ran per goon, and a goon still standing when it ran out ended the run with the hero alive. That's how runs ended after a floor or two (2026-11-11, "my son was able to defeat all 5"): an attack pressed on the vault's drop to the next floor lands the hero high on the flight, which still counts as upstairs, and the next wave waited for him there with nobody to fight until the clock ran out. The next wave now starts once the vault is behind him wherever it left him (the goons come up the stairs for him), and after 8 seconds (`TRANSIT_MAX`) whatever he's doing. `npm run test:horde` (`scripts/horde/check.mjs`) checks the clock, the regen and 12 CPU runs of up to 12 floors each; on the old build 4 of those runs got stuck after a floor.

**Every way a goon goes down counts and keeps the run going** (2026-11-11, "after a player combos a goon to KO, it instantly shows game over"). A hit can take a goon's last point in four places in `applyHit` (`src/combat/sim.js`): a plain hit or a throw, a juggle hit, a counter on a wake-up strike, and a hit through armor. Only the plain hit knew about horde. The other three did a 1v1 KO, which ended the round, and the flow sent any horde round end to GAME OVER, so launching a goon and finishing the juggle ended the run with the hero at full health. That was a design problem, not a typo, so the KO handling was rebuilt so a hit path can't get it wrong: hit paths only deal damage, the mode's rules decide what a KO means, and the flow reads the outcome the sim announces (Fundamentals, How a KO resolves). In horde a goon's KO is `goonKO()`: the ragdoll, `N DOWN`, the wave count, the next goon in, and a `goonDown` event, which is what the flow scores, so `run.goons` goes up by exactly one whatever hit did it. Only the hero going down ends a run (`sim.result.reason` is `'heroDown'`), and `decide()` goes to GAME OVER on that. A goon with nobody to replace it leaves the floor standing, not lying, because the hero's run to the stairwell after FLOOR CLEAR stops at the near end of a lying body and used to stop at the invisible one. `?scene=horde-ko-test` plays one run with a scripted hero. He puts a goon down with a jab, a launcher into a juggle jab, a sweep and then a counter on its WAKE KICK while it lies there, a jab through armor, and a throw. That fifth goon clears the floor, and on the next floor a goon KOs the hero, which ends the run. Real goons are all TEMP, who has no armored move, so the armor phase borrows a CHEF goon and its CLEAVER. `__game.installHordeKo()` runs the same script on a run the arcade flow started. `npm run test:horde` checks it both ways: in the scene, and in the flow, where `run.goons` counts 1 to 5 and the flow goes fighting > roundEnd > result > GAME OVER only after the hero is down. **Training** is the dummy scene, no score.

**Pause** (P, Esc, pad Start or Select, or the pause button, the first of the corner buttons in the top right, by click or tap; see Controls, The corner buttons) in arcade, horde and training: RESUME, MOVE LIST (the fighter you're playing; F1 or pad Y open it too, see Controls, Move list), RESTART STAGE (RESTART RUN in horde, RESET in training), BOTCHES ON/OFF, ROUNDS and TIME (Match rules; not in horde), SETTINGS (opens the one SETTINGS panel over the menu, the same panel the menu button opens: sound, music and, on a touch screen, the size of the controls; closing it comes back to the pause menu with the focus still on SETTINGS), SHOW CONTROLS (swaps the right panel between the techniques found and the fight controls, keyboard and gamepad), MAIN MENU. BOTCHES, ROUNDS and TIME stay here and on the mode select rather than moving into the panel: they are match options that apply from the next fight, and the panel's settings apply at once. P, Esc, Backspace, B or Start resumes. Nothing runs while paused, the round clock included.

**Idle.** 30 seconds with no input goes back to the title only where nothing is lost: mode select, the online menu and join screen, the profile screen, the end of a quick-room match, and training. Never the crew lobby (friends wait there), the typing screens, or sign-in (the code takes a trip to the inbox). Never mid-match: an arcade or horde run left alone plays itself out (the CPU or the goons win), CONTINUE runs down, a table score is entered as it stands, and the cabinet is back on the demo. A paused game stays paused. An online match never times out, and neither do the screens between matches in an online room (post-match and NEW CHARACTERS): only a player's own LEAVE ROOM or MAIN MENU, or the other player leaving, ends a room.

**Versus online.** A join link asks nothing. A `room/CODE` link goes straight to JOINING and from there to the pick screen, and a `crew/<slug>` link goes straight to the crew lobby (the old `?room=` and `?crew=` forms too, through the Worker's 301), with no key, tap or letter in between: a friend who got a link is one pick away from playing. A visitor with no name yet is handed one, GUEST and three digits (`guestName()` in `src/net/profile.js`), kept in the profile like any name and marked `auto` until they pick their own. JOINING and the crew lobby say what it is (YOU'RE GUEST 427) and that PROFILE changes it; CHANGE NAME (or the lobby's SET YOUR NAME) on a handed-out name asks WHAT DO WE CALL YOU? rather than CHANGE YOUR NAME, and its field starts empty with the handed-out name greyed behind the caret, so typing ALEX and OK saves exactly ALEX (before [twmcsg] round 5 the field held GUEST 309, and typing gave GUEST 309ALE). BACK keeps the handed-out name. A name you picked yourself starts in the field, ready to edit. A handed-out name stays on the device: signing in to an account that has no name doesn't save it there. A returning player on a new device who came in by a link signs in from PROFILE (SAVE MY PROFILE) and gets their name back. A link to a room that's gone ends on NO ROOM WITH THAT CODE with TRY AGAIN, ONLINE MENU and MAIN MENU. `npm run test:join` (`scripts/join/check.mjs`) checks all of this in real browsers at 1280x720, 844x390 and 390x844, along with the clean links (2026-11-11) and the upright crew lobby ([twmcsg]): the address bar stays `crew/<slug>` or `room/<CODE>`, the lobby's link and QR code (read back by jsQR) are the clean link, nothing from the game's origin fails in the network log (no 404s), an old `?crew=&pick=` link lands in the lobby at the clean address with `pick` kept, an unknown `crew/<slug>` loads the game and says NO CREW AT THAT LINK, and leaving the crew for the menus puts the address back to `play/`. 100 checks, all passing.

The first time a player goes online from the menu (VERSUS ONLINE) the game does ask for a name first, since they're hosting or setting up a crew and nobody is waiting on them: WHAT DO WE CALL YOU?, 3 to 12 letters, digits and spaces, typed or picked on the on-screen keyboard, with OK, SIGN IN (a returning player on a new device restores the profile instead) and BACK. The online menu has two columns: CREW ROOMS (up to three recent crews by name, focused first when there is one, then CREATE CREW ROOM and JOIN A CREW) and QUICK MATCH (HOST A GAME, focused when there's no crew, and JOIN A GAME), with PROFILE and MAIN MENU under them (back goes to the mode select). VERSUS ONLINE goes straight here: nobody picks a fighter until they're in a room with their rival, and then both pick at once on the pick screen (below), the way most fighting games do it. The crew screens, the profile and sign-in are under Multiplayer, Crew rooms and Profiles and sign-in. The quick-match side works as before: HOST A GAME shows the room code, the link (COPY LINK, and SHARE where the phone has a share sheet; both dim until the room is open), WAITING FOR A FRIEND, CANCEL and MAIN MENU. JOIN A GAME takes the code from the keyboard or an on-screen keyboard (tap, mouse, or D-pad and A; DEL is Backspace or pad X); JOIN stays dim, and the focus skips it, until the code is whole. JOINING has CANCEL and MAIN MENU (CANCEL goes to JOIN with the code filled in). Once both are in the room, host and guest land on the pick screen together (VS BOB, the `onlineSelect` state below), and the match starts when both have locked in and the host's STARTING IN 3 runs out (or the host presses START). When the match starts the menus go away; Esc, P, pad Start or Select, or the touch LEAVE button opens `online.js`'s LEAVE / STAY confirm (online has no pause), with STAY focused; B, Esc, Backspace or Start again is STAY. When a match ends the room stays open and both players get the post-match screen (below). When a connection ends, or the other player leaves, the notice says why, with ONLINE MENU and MAIN MENU, plus HOST AGAIN or TRY AGAIN when a room didn't open or a join didn't connect. See Multiplayer for the network side.

**After an online match** (the `onlinePost` state) both players see the result (YOU WIN, YOU LOSE or DRAW, the match number, the fighters, the rounds), the room's running tally from their own side in big type (YOU 3 – 2 RIVAL, then the rounds, draws, and the all-time record against this rival when there's more than this room's), and REMATCH, NEW CHARACTERS, LEAVE ROOM and MAIN MENU, with REMATCH focused. The point is to keep a session going with as few presses as possible:

- **Nobody does anything:** REMATCH IN 5 counts down and the next match starts with the same fighters, so a couch session keeps going. Any press from either player (a direction, back, another button) stops it.
- **REMATCH from both** (one press each, in either order), or from the host while the guest hasn't said anything: REMATCH IN 3, then it starts. The host pressing REMATCH again starts it at once. The guest can hold it with back or WAIT, or by choosing NEW CHARACTERS.
- **The guest's choice is a request.** The host's screen says RIVAL WANTS: NEW CHARACTERS and marks that button, and the host's cursor jumps to it (unless the host moved it in the last second; a press within 0.3 s of the jump doesn't count), so one press accepts. The guest's screen says what the host's cursor is on (HOST IS ON: REMATCH) and what the guest asked for.
- **The host's choice is final.** NEW CHARACTERS from the host takes both to the pick screen at once. REMATCH from the host over a guest who asked for something else is the host's call: REMATCH IN 3 that only the host can stop (back or STOP), and the guest's screen says HOST'S CALL. Back, or the STOP button beside the countdown, stops any countdown on the host's side.
- **LEAVE ROOM** goes to the online menu and **MAIN MENU** to the mode select, for either player, at once; the other player gets YOUR RIVAL LEFT (a host's notice then offers HOST AGAIN first: a fresh room in one press). Back on this screen never leaves: it's WAIT for the guest and STOP for the host. STOP or WAIT sits beside the countdown and is dim (and skipped by the focus) while there's no countdown you can stop.

**The online pick screen** (the `onlineSelect` state) is the character select for both at once. Every room opens on it before the first match (titled VS and the rival's name; a crew match puts the crew's name at the front of the subtitle), and NEW CHARACTERS brings both back to it between matches. It opens on each player's last pick (or the fighter from a `?pick=` link) so one press confirms it. LEAVE ROOM backs out to the online menu (BACK TO CREW in a crew match), and a rival who leaves or drops here gets the same YOUR RIVAL LEFT or CONNECTION LOST notice as between matches, with HOST AGAIN for a host. Under the title a line reads YOU: PICKING or YOU: READY · COURIER, and RIVAL IS PICKING… INTERN (the rival's card under their cursor, live) or RIVAL READY: INTERN; the rival's card wears a RIVAL tag (green when locked in), and the subtitle carries the match number and the tally. PICK (Enter, A, or a second tap) locks your card in; back un-readies (CHANGE), and never leaves the room. The footer has LEAVE ROOM and PICK, and for the host START. Once both are ready the host's START lights up and STARTING IN 3 runs (START starts at once; either player un-readying stops it). The guest sees WAITING FOR HOST. If the guest hasn't sent anything (no cursor move, no pick) for 20 seconds while the host is ready, START lights up anyway as START · RIVAL: INTERN and starts with the guest's current card: a guest who walked away doesn't hold the room hostage, but the host has to press it; nothing starts without a ready guest on its own.


**Touch.** The stick and the buttons show only while fighting; menus are tapped directly. Nothing on the page scrolls or pans under a finger, in a fight or on a menu, except the few boxes made to (see Controls, "What scrolls under a finger"). The menu button (and fullscreen, where the phone has it) stays in the top right on every screen; the pause button joins them in a fight.

**Small stages: every menu fits.** A menu never runs past the stage. The menus are sized in container units with a few px floors (a button is never under about 40 px tall), so on a small stage a tall menu used to overflow the top and bottom: held upright, mode select lost its title and ARCADE, and the pause menu lost PAUSED and RESUME. Two things fix it, both in `src/flow/ui.js`:

- **`fit()`** measures what a screen holds against the stage and, when it's too tall or too wide, sets CSS `zoom` on the whole screen so it's inside, with a sliver of air. Zoom scales px and container units alike, so one pass is exact. It runs when a screen opens, when the window or the stage changes size, when the fonts arrive, and when a screen's content changes under it (a `ResizeObserver` on the screen's children: the pause menu's right panel, a lobby filling up). It covers every screen, including ones that were clipped before this on bigger stages: CONTROLS lost its heading even at 1280x720, and the pause menu lost PAUSED on a phone held sideways (nine items there now zoom to about 0.8, 32 px buttons).
- **A narrow stage lays out wide** (`@container (max-width: 520px)`: a phone held upright, where the stage is about 390 by 219). Zoom alone would leave seven or nine stacked buttons unreadable there, so mode select puts the four modes two by two, ROUNDS and TIME side by side and BACK across, and the pause menu goes three across. The pause menu's right panel (techniques and controls) and its SHOW CONTROLS button are left out at that width, because nothing in the panel is readable at 390 px; turn the phone and they're back. An item a layout doesn't show is skipped by the focus like a dim one. The character select isn't one of them: held upright it leaves the stage for the whole screen (see Arcade flow, Character select).

**Held upright: every screen takes the whole phone** (2026-11-11, `.fl-up` in `src/flow/ui.js`). The narrow-stage layout above still squeezed each menu into the 390 by 219 box and let `fit()` zoom it, so on a phone held upright the crew lobby's names were 9 px, its buttons 30 px tall, the on-screen keyboards' keys 15 by 30 px, and the bottom 60% of the phone was empty. Now every menu drawn over a dimmed scene (every screen but the title and the watch view, which stay in the view) leaves the box for the whole screen under the top buttons, the way the character select already did (`.fl-tall`, Character select). `show()` marks the screen `fl-up` whenever the square is on (`square.on`, `src/square.js`), and turning the phone flips the screen that's up between the two layouts at once. Sideways and on a desktop nothing changes: every rule is under `#flow > .fl-up`.
- **The floors.** #flow is the container there, so 1cqw is about 4 px. Text is `max(12px, …)` or bigger (labels 13 to 16 px, buttons 15 to 20 px), buttons are at least 44 px tall (46 for most), and button rows span the screen two to a line. The touch controls stand down while a full-screen menu is up. A page opened straight onto a crew or room link used to keep the old demo's `attract` mark on `<html>` after the menus came on, which hid the whole touch layer, the menu button included, and showed SOURCE & CREDITS over the lobby on any device; the flow now clears it (`classes()` in `src/flow/index.js`).
- **Lists that grow scroll in their own box** (`data-scroll="y"`, Controls, "What scrolls under a finger"): the crew's members (all of them upright, not the top seven, under a header that stays put), the LIVE list (every match, not five), high scores, CONTROLS (both tables and the notes in one column) and the pause menu's TECHNIQUES or CONTROLS panel, which upright is back, under the menu, with its SHOW CONTROLS button.
- **The layouts.** Mode select: the four modes stacked full width, ROUNDS and TIME side by side, BACK across. The online menu: CREW ROOMS over QUICK MATCH, your crews across the screen, CREATE and JOIN side by side, HOST, JOIN and WATCH three across. The typing screens (a name, a crew name or link, an email, a code, a room code, initials): the field across the screen and a keyboard as wide as the phone, keys at least 44 px tall, with the OK, JOIN or CREATE key under it in view; the email keyboard's extra keys share the last row. The host screen: the code, the link, the QR code as big as the screen leaves (150 to 300 px; 265 px at 390x844), then the buttons two by two. The pause menu: two across, the panel under it. The stage card: the two names stacked. The post-match screen: the score big, the buttons two by two. The crew lobby is under Multiplayer, Crew rooms; its STANDINGS, ARCADE, HORDE and STATS tabs (Crew high scores) stay at the top of the scrolling list, with the column heads sticking under them. The age question before SAVE MY PROFILE (Privacy and terms) is two answers side by side over a full-width BACK.
- **`fit()`** still runs on these, with no air at the edges (the screen's padding is that), so a screen that fills the phone isn't zoomed a hair at a time; before, a scrolling box that took what was left made it zoom down step after step to 0.77.
- **The update pill** goes to the top left upright, in the strip beside the corner buttons and 44 px tall, rather than over a full-screen menu's buttons; **the SETTINGS panel**'s buttons are 44 px tall on a phone held upright (they were 30 to 36); **the title**'s HIGH SCORES and CONTROLS are 14 px type on 42 px buttons under a 48 px START, which used to overlap them; **the watch view**'s bar and buttons are in phone px. The character select's ‹ › are 44 px tall.

Checked with `scripts/shots/menus.mjs` (below, Layout), which tours every screen of the flow: the crew screens first (CREATE, JOIN, the lobby with 13 members, upright its INVITE view, the lobby with PLAY pressed, CREW MATCH linking, NO CREW AT THAT LINK), then the online menu, PROFILE, the name screen, the age question (both answers), the email and code screens, HOST A GAME, JOIN A GAME, JOINING and NO ROOM WITH THAT CODE, a real room with a second browser (the pick screen, the match, the LEAVE confirm, the post-match screen, the rival leaving), the LIVE list and the watch view, then the title, SETTINGS, the home-screen how-to, mode select, CONTROLS, high scores, the character select and MOVE LIST, an arcade run (stage card, the fight, pause, the pause panel, SETTINGS and MOVE LIST over pause, stage clear, CONTINUE?, game over, initials, high scores, the ending) and the update pill. On each it fails anything a player reads or presses that's cut off by the screen (or at the side of a box, with no ellipsis; a flex or grid box's ellipsis covers only its own text, so a chip it clips after a name counts as cut off), overlapping something else, or under the top buttons, the HUD band or the touch controls; and upright only, text under 12 px and tap targets under 40 px tall. It also walks the D-pad from each screen's opening focus in every direction and fails any button it can't reach, and reads the QR codes back with jsQR from an upright screenshot at 1x (onlineHost, and crewLobby: upright, after pressing INVITE). Results, 2026-11-11, against a local Worker:
- **Upright, before** (main, the same tour): 761 to 863 problems a size, about 2,200 of them text under 12 px and 1,750 tap targets under 40 px; the crew lobby's QR code was 105 px at 390x844.
- **Upright, after:** nothing at 390x844, 412x915, 375x667, 395x700 or 412x640. The QR codes read back as the link at every size: the crew lobby's 270 to 297 px behind INVITE (2026-11-11, [twmcsg]; 140 to 165 px before, beside the link), the host screen's 150 to 280 px (375x667: 150 and 167).
- **Sideways and desktop** are the same as before to the pixel, apart from your own crew row ([twmcsg], below: your name is its own span, so (YOU) clips before the name and goes once your READY or PLAYING chip shows): `--dump` on main and on this branch at 1280x720, 844x390 and 667x375 gives every element the same box and font size on all 121 screens, apart from content that differs run to run (a room code's letters, the countdown's digit, the email typed, the ending's scroll). The QR size floor is an upright rule; sideways the tiles are 120 to 135 px, as before.
- **Fixes the new checks found** that were on main too, and the only changes sideways or on a desktop. Sideways, three: the online pick screen's sheet ran past its box onto the hints with a fighter that has four moves (TEMP since RISE AND SHINE), so its move notes go there (MOVE LIST has them); JOIN A CREW's line ran under the corner buttons at 667x375, so it's shorter (PASTE THE CREW LINK, OR TYPE ITS CODE, with the example in the line under the field); and the update pill sat on mode select's TIME at 667x375, so on a phone sideways it takes the bottom-left corner, a little smaller. On a desktop, one: the online LEAVE / STAY confirm's key legend ran past both sides of its panel (ARROWS read RROWS), so its gaps are tighter there.

**Swallowed presses.** On every screen change, whatever key or button is held is ignored until it's let go, both by the menus (`menuInput.swallow`) and by the fighter (`input.swallowHeld`), so the press that changed screens never acts on the next one or throws a punch. A round rolling over mid-fight isn't a screen change, so a held guard stays held. STAY on the online confirm swallows its press too.

**`?scene=` URLs skip all of this** and behave as before: the scene they name, no menus, no timeout, and the old hotkeys (G horde, R reset, P pause). `?scene=attract` is the old demo, where any key starts `play`. `__gauntlet.setScene` and `__game.setScene` also turn the flow off, so the capture tool works on plain `index.html` too.

Each demo bout draws a random pair from the registered roster (a mirror match gets the alt color). The demo fighters are the CPU at difficulty 10 in its **showcase** flavor (`sim.setAI(i, 1, { style: 'showcase' })`, `src/combat/ai.js`): it keeps spacing at the edge of reach, steps out of pokes and punishes the whiff, reads and guards highs, mids and lows, opens with string starters at all three heights instead of jabs, blocks rather than mashing out of hitstun, keeps its stamina up (it never presses the strike that would faint it), jumps in and meets jump-ins with an uppercut or a standing guard, and saves the stairwell for later in the round. THE MANAGER fights the same way.

**Human reaction time** (2026-11-09; the owner and his son couldn't get past SECURITY, who "counters all my hits immediately"). Every CPU reflex used to fire on the first sim step of what it answered, about 8 ms, where a person needs 200 to 250 ms to see a move. Now each reflex that answers the opponent (the guard, duck and parry at a strike's startup, the whiff and botch punishes, the anti-air reads, the wake-up answer to a strike at the body, the faint punish, the characters' reflex specials like LAWYER's Objection) sees him as he was some steps ago: 330 ms at difficulty 0.2, falling evenly to 180 ms at 1.0 (the showcase 20 ms quicker, never under 160), plus up to a fifth more, re-rolled after each reaction. `perceive()` in `src/combat/ai.js` keeps a ring of shallow copies of the opponent, one a sim step. A reaction to something that is already over (the strike landed, the whiff recovered, the jumper touched down) is dropped. So a jab (13 steps of startup, 108 ms) or a cross can't be reacted to at all, and a CPU only stops one with a guard it was already holding: the **habit guard**, a guard on a hunch now and then inside your reach while you've been swinging (3% plus 6% of its difficulty per think, a third of them low; the turtle a little more). Slow strikes, overheads and command grabs are still read and guarded. It's all sim step counts and the sim's rng, so `?scene=` pages and recordings replay identically. `NET_VERSION` stays 14: only `ai.js`, `ladder.js` and the flow changed, an online match drives both fighters with the netcode's `net` controllers (the `online` scene in `src/main.js`), so no CPU ever runs under rollback, and nothing in the sim or the snapshot changed. The timeline test scenes script both fighters, so they can't move either; `getup-test`, `parry-test`, `stomp-test`, `air-recovery-test` and `string-test` end on the same health as before.

**No instant counters.** Two things made the CPU hit back the moment your hit ended. The swing-back reflex fired 55% plus 60% of its difficulty of the time (97% at the old SECURITY). The bigger one wasn't in any table: the CPU kept planning while in hitstun, picked a strike, and the sim buffered it onto the first free frame. Now nothing is planned in hit- or blockstun, the first plan after waits half a reaction time, and the swing-back is decided one reaction time after your last hit landed: 3% plus 40% of difficulty squared, capped at 40% (5% at the INTERN, 15% at SECURITY, 29% at THE MANAGER's 0.8, 20% for the showcase). Otherwise half the time it just eats it and resets, a short guard or a step back out of range. The other chances that came near certain at the top were trimmed: the guard reflex at a startup it saw is 10% plus 50% of difficulty (was 65%, the turtle +15 not +25; 70% for the showcase, was 85%), a whiff or botch it saw is punished 30% plus 45% (20% plus 45% on a strike that connected; was up to 100%), the botch reflex 20% plus 50% (was 25% plus 60%), the turtle's and showcase's whiff punish 45% plus 30% and 75% (both were 85%), the old anti-air uppercut 10% plus 50% (was 15% plus 65%), and the grappler's close throw 10% plus 27% of difficulty (was a flat 40%; 25% at SECURITY's 0.55), with its dash-in throw approach at 20% plus 20% of difficulty (was 45%).

**Breathing room.** After the CPU lands a combo of 3 or more hits, or while it leads by 30% of the health bar (tried every 1 to 1.5 s), it takes a breath of 0.4 to 0.7 s: it walks off, stands, or drifts, with a rare short guard, and doesn't swing, doesn't meaty you on the floor and doesn't swing back out of hitstun. An opening you hand it (a whiff it saw) is still punished. The top of the ladder skips more of them: every one below 0.7, 85% at THE MANAGER's 0.8, 55% at 1.0, 40% for the showcase. Not in horde.

**Measured** (`window.__game` in headless Chromium, 120 to 200 s of sim a stage across three to five seeds, TEMP as the player against each stage's character and style). Two scripted players: **jab** walks in and jabs every 34 to 62 steps whatever happens, and **guard** also guards the CPU's strikes 250 ms after they start (at the right height 75% of the time), holds a guard on a hunch now and then, punishes a whiff with a cross, and finishes 1,1,2 on a jab that lands. *Swing-back* is how often the CPU starts a strike within 4 steps (33 ms) of leaving hitstun; *countered* is how often the CPU lands a hit within 0.5 s of one of the player's hits landing or being blocked. Before is the old AI on the old ladder; after is the new AI on the new ladder.

| Stage | CPU before → after | Jab: swing-back | Jab: countered | Guard: swing-back | Guard: countered | Guard: rounds won (player–CPU) | Guard: damage dealt / taken |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 INTERN | 0.2 → 0.2 | 61% → 8% | 10% → 9% | 60% → 15% | 9% → 5% | 8–18 → 6–19 | 0.57 → 0.50 |
| 2 HELPDESK | 0.4 → 0.32 | 79% → 9% | 18% → 11% | 87% → 24% | 9% → 10% | 5–18 → 1–25 | 0.46 → 0.27 |
| 3 COURIER | 0.55 → 0.44 | 86% → 10% | 20% → 2% | 89% → 36% | 4% → 3% | 3–26 → 1–29 | 0.31 → 0.25 |
| 4 SECURITY | 0.7 → 0.55 | 80% → 12% | 19% → 0% | 46% → 27% | 10% → 3% | 3–22 → 0–22 | 0.31 → 0.32 |
| 5 JANITOR | 0.85 → 0.66 | 100% → 14% | 8% → 5% | 87% → 38% | 4% → 6% | 2–28 → 0–28 | 0.25 → 0.16 |
| 6 THE MANAGER | 1.0 → 0.8 | 78% → 15% | 29% → 13% | 74% → 31% | 13% → 5% | 3–23 → 0–33 | 0.30 → 0.18 |

The counters are gone; the bots don't win more. Both scripted players lose nearly every round before and after, because what beats them is the CPU's own offense, jabs faster than a 250 ms reaction, which this change doesn't touch. And a bot that jabs on a fixed rhythm was actually feeding on the old instant counters: a CPU that swung straight out of hitstun ran into the next jab. A person who stops getting countered can step in with a string, wait out the breath, or guard on a hunch, and no bot here does. Treat the win columns as "no easier for a metronome", and get a person's read on the new ladder. Throws on the player at SECURITY fell from 27 to 13 in the guard runs.

CPU vs CPU at 0.7 (24 matches a pairing, sides swapped every other match, rounds won before → after): SECURITY v TEMP 35–25 → 33–28, COURIER v TEMP 26–35 → 28–32, HELPDESK v JANITOR 28–31 → 33–26, INTERN v THE MANAGER 27–35 → 30–32, SECURITY v COURIER 29–28 → 31–28. All of them moved within the noise of 24 matches. (With the grappler's close throw at 22% at 0.7, SECURITY fell to 44% and 42%; at 10% plus 27% of difficulty it throws 29% there, and its balance holds.) The balance numbers elsewhere in this README were measured before this change.

**Anti-airs.** Every CPU decides, when it sees your takeoff (a reaction time after it happens, and only if you're still in the air), whether it read the jump: 12% at difficulty 0.3 or below (INTERN mostly gets jumped on, but not always), then rising with difficulty to 65% (was 80%; 75% for showcase, was 85%; +15% for the aerial style). A read drops whatever it was doing, stands its ground until you come into the uppercut's reach about when it would go live, and presses d+2 (a jab instead if you're already on top of it with a strike out); a jump that will land short is let land. A CPU that doesn't read it still has the old once-per-jump uppercut reflex.

**Stamina and faints.** No CPU presses the strike that would empty its bar, except that the bottom of the ladder slips now and then ((1 - difficulty) x 40% of the times it would, never the showcase), so INTERN and HELPDESK faint a round or so and THE MANAGER doesn't. A CPU under about 30 stamina backs off or guards while it refills, and only starts a string's ender when the rest of the string fits in the bar. It knows the refunds (Controls, Stamina): after a clean hit it counts the rest of the string at half price, and a string's next press only needs the bar to hold half that press's price, since a press that empties the bar and hits leaves it standing. When you faint it runs in and opens a string on you: 35% plus 65% of its difficulty of the time, every time for the showcase. **Fainted itself, it shakes its head at a human pace** (`shakeOff()` in `src/combat/ai.js`, decided once per faint; it no longer staggers away, since a fainted body can't walk). It starts a reaction time in, and how often it shakes and how cleanly both go up with difficulty. The showcase always shakes, a press every 14 to 16 steps where 12 is the best allowed. The bottom of the ladder shakes in about half its faints, on a slower beat, sometimes pressing the same way twice or missing a beat. Measured over 40 faints each with nobody hitting it (`node gauntlet/measure.mjs cpufaint`, average / best): difficulty 0.1 1.40 / 1.20s (it shook in 43% of them), 0.3 1.34 / 1.10s (53%), 0.5 1.27 / 1.10s (68%), 0.7 1.18 / 1.00s (75%), 0.9 0.99 / 0.90s (95%), showcase 0.90 / 0.88s (100%).

## Controls

Tekken-style, one button per limb, from the fighter's own point of view:

| Key | Button | Limb |
|---|---|---|
| U | 1 | left punch (jab) |
| I | 2 | right punch (cross) |
| J | 3 | left kick |
| K | 4 | right kick (roundhouse) |

- **Move** A/D or arrows. **Hold back** (away from the opponent) to step back, Tekken style: you walk back (at the character's back-walk speed; TEMP's is the forward walk's) and the guard snaps on by itself only when a strike is coming that can reach you (one in startup or out, inside its reach plus any slide or charge travel plus a little, about to go live) or a jump-in is closing. It holds through blockstun and a beat after, then you're walking back again. Standing guard stops highs and mids, lows go under it (and don't make you snap: down-back is the answer), and throws beat it. A hit that lands while you hold back in a guard-capable state (walking back, idle, crouching back) is blocked even if the snap missed a frame. B, and the touch GUARD button, mean the same as holding back, so they walk back too. Walking back costs no stamina; blocking costs what it always did, and the guarding regen rate applies only while actually blocking.
- **Crouch** S or down. A crouch ducks highs completely; **down-back** guards lows. Mids hit a croucher.
- **Jump** W. **Dash:** tap toward the opponent twice for a quick burst forward, tap away twice for a hop back (Fundamentals, Dashes). **Run** Shift, or keep the dash's second tap held. **Throw** 1+3 or 2+4; the same press during the grab breaks a throw. Throws can't be guarded, but they whiff on a croucher.
- **In the air:** jump with W (hold a direction for a forward or back jump; you can steer a little in the air), then 1 jump jab, 2 jump cross, 3 flying kick, 4 air roundhouse (knockdown), down+4 dive kick (cuts the jump short). One air attack per jump, plus a quick jab on the way down once a whiffed one has recovered. Juggled, a button at the right moment recovers you onto your feet. See Fundamentals, In the air. Air attacks are overheads: they hit a croucher, so guard them standing. Land a jump-in hit and keep pressing for a ground string; whiff one and you land with a short lag. An uppercut (d+2, or rising 2) meeting a jump-in is a counter hit that launches; a jab stuffs a late one. In the rig an air attack eases its hip onto the jump's arc over its startup (and a dive kick never climbs the arc, it drops from where it was). Its homing lunge (up to 70px toward the defender) and torso swing don't land in the 50ms pre-roll the sim skips: the head travels in a straight line, evenly over the frames from the press to the moment the strike can hit, and the hips carry the difference; from then on, and at contact, the body is exactly where it was before. The jump's push-off is drawn with softer eases than the arc air attacks ride, so takeoff has no big frame. Leaving the air for anything grounded lowers the hip over 160ms (220 after a dive) instead of in one frame; a strike thrown on landing finishes that descent by its contact key, so it lands at standing height. Where the head still has to travel farther than 20px in one frame to make contact (a jump jab or dive kick from far out, a landing push-apart, the stairwell vault and its handoff to standing), `drawOffset()` in `src/main.js` holds the drawing to 20px per 60fps frame and lets it catch up. It's visual only: hit detection, contact and the netcode state use the real positions. A connecting strike snaps the drawing to the real pose on the contact frame, under the impact flash, so hits always draw touching. Launched, thrown and juggled bodies draw raw. On the ground the same idea is `frameDraw()`'s turnaround and snap fade (Controls, Sides).
- **Parry:** tap toward the opponent (down-forward for a low) just before a strike lands. Not a hold, and not too early: see Fundamentals, The parry.
- **Over a body on the floor:** down + 4 is the Stomp, and a low that visibly touches the body (down + 3, the low kick; down + 1, the low jab; a character's lows) is a lighter ground hit, once per knockdown (Fundamentals, Hitting a body on the floor).
- **Knocked down:** you stay on the floor until you pick a getup option: **up** stands in place, a fresh press **away** from the other fighter rolls back and keeps your side of him, a fresh press **toward** him rolls toward him (under him to his other side if he's standing over or near you), and **any button** is a wake-up strike from the floor (1 or 3 the Wake Kick, 2 or 4, or anything with down, the Wake Sweep). Leave it alone and you stand up by yourself after 1 second. Every way up rises on your own side of him, facing him; only the roll under him changes sides. See Fundamentals, Getting up and Sides.
- **Crouching:** all four crouch attacks come out of a crouch or a crouch-walk (hold down and forward) without standing up, and you stay down after a low while down is held. d+1, d+1, d+3 is the Low Rush string. On touch, stick down plus a button.
- **Directions:** d+1 low jab, d+2 uppercut (launcher), d+3 low kick, d+4 sweep, f+1 body blow, f+2 lunge cross, f+4 overhead (mid, beats crouch), b+4 spin kick. Release down, then a button is a rising attack, one per limb: 1 = rising jab, 2 = rising uppercut, 3 = rising kick, 4 = rising knee (2 and 4 launch). Out of a forward dash or a run, each button is a dash attack: 1 = bullet jab, 2 = comet cross, 3 = skid kick, 4 = running boot. See Fundamentals, Dash attacks and Rising attacks.
- **Heights:** high = jab, cross, roundhouse, spin kick, spin heel. Low = low jab, low kick, sweep. Everything else is mid.
- **Stamina** (the thin bar under health, out of 100) is your attack budget. Jab 17, low jab 15, cross 21, body blow 19, lunge cross 22, low kick 18, the other kicks 22, launchers 24, throws 22, any ender 30; air attacks 12 to 18, a jump 8, a dash 6, a back dash 8 (a dash needs more than its price in the bar and never faints you), running 14 a second (running stops at the last point, it never faints you). Mashing jab empties it on the 6th press, crosses and kicks on the 5th. A full four-hit string fits from full but leaves you low (1,1,2,4 costs 85, 1,2,3,4 costs 90, Sky Spike 92). **Landing hits gives some back** (`STAMINA.refundHit`, `refundBlock` in `src/combat/frameData.js`): a strike that hits refunds half of what it took the moment it connects, a blocked one a quarter (armor soaking it counts as a block), and a whiff, a parried strike or a botch nothing. That goes for strings, enders, juggle hits, the stomp, throws that land (a broken one gives nothing), character moves and wake-up strikes, once per strike. The **repeat decay** (`refundRepeat`, 1.5s): the same move refunded again within 1.5s of the last refund gives back half as much each time (jab, jab, jab on hit: 8.5, 4.25, 2.1), so mashing one button doesn't pay for itself, while a string of different moves keeps it all. NEON TORNADO on hit costs 90 and gets 45 back; two juggle jabs after it (8.5 back, then 4.25) and the stomp (9 back) leave about 25 from a full bar even with no regen in between (refund-test). The bar ticks up as each refund lands, and the gained piece flashes cyan on the bar and the overhead track for 0.45s. It comes back at 90 a second once you've gone 0.3s without spending (none while attacking or running), and at 36 a second while guarding, so it refills in about a second of walking. **Knocked down, it refills to the top:** the bar fills (with a white flash on the bar and the overhead track) the moment you hit the floor, from a hit, a throw, a trip, a floor break or a stair tumble, and a faint ends there too. Blocking a hit costs a third of its price and never takes the last point. Under 35 the bar turns pink and flashes LOW.
- **Sides:** you keep your side of the opponent, and sides only change through motion you can see. Classic 2D fighters work the same way: solid pushboxes that always resolve toward each body's own side, jumps that cross over, specific throws and moves that visibly carry someone past, and a turn when facing flips. Here:
  - **Bodies are solid and push back toward their own side.** When two standing bodies overlap, each is pushed back toward the side it was on when the step began (`separatePair()` goes by `prevX`, a pushbox), so a lunge, a glide or a slide into someone never comes out the far side. A body is moved at most 6px a step by the pushing apart (`SEP_CAP`, 720 px/s), so a deep overlap (a getup turning solid under someone at the wall, a landing on top of someone, a body coming to rest past the man standing over it) is a visible shove over a few frames, never one snap.
  - **The root goes where the body is.** A travelling clip (a cross's step, a stagger, the getup's rise, a botch's stumble, the vault) moves the rig's root when it ends or is cut short; the sim's root follows it the same step (`takeRoot()` after every rig update, and `safePlay()` around every `play()`), so the drawn body never jumps back by the step. The getup used to stand the fighter up at his old root, a body-length from where his hip lay, often on the far side of the man standing over his legs: that was most of the "teleports". Now he stands up where his hip is, on his own side. A body getting up isn't slid along the floor (its feet may be past the wall, its hip never is), and a body coming to rest from a tumble keeps the root and facing the rig gave it (a clamped root or the old facing re-posed it a body-length away).
  - **Lying bodies.** You can stand over a body on the floor (and step farther in under a juggled one): at its feet up to its drawn feet (84 px from its hip, `lieEnds()`; it used to be its root, a body-length past the feet, which kept every low but the stomp off the legs), at its head side no closer than 10 px short of its hip (`HIP_GAP`). You can't walk, run or lunge out the far side of it, and a body rolling or sliding along the floor stops under you. A body getting up rises on its own side, because a standing fighter can only reach a little way onto it. Sides and facing go by where a lying body actually is (its hip), since its root sits at its feet. After a knockdown the attacker's ride (he follows the launch to be on the body when it lands) stops at the body's near end, a string's chase step stops at contact, and a body getting up is pushed apart by the sides the two had. A body against the wall slides off it (300 px/s), and a rolling one stops against that pad instead of being thrown out of it.
  - **What still changes sides:** jump cross-ups (a real arc over him), launches and juggles that carry the body over, a floor break and the follow-down vault, a roll, Window Washer's double jump and wall jump, Janitor's Mop Spin landing behind him (`bothSides`), and a body sliding under a standing fighter. Hits knock the defender away from the attacker, not the way the attacker faces, and a roll faces the opponent. A throw holds the victim 70px in front: he is pulled there at 720 px/s over the grab's first frames (`grabTo`), never put there, and one caught behind the thrower turns the thrower round instead of passing through him.
  - **How it reads on screen** (`frameDraw()` in `src/main.js`, visual only: hit detection, contact and the netcode's state keep the real pose). A grounded fighter whose facing flips is drawn turning on his feet over 100ms, squashed horizontally about his root from the old facing through zero to the new one, instead of mirroring in place. Anything that would still move the drawn body farther than 18px in one frame, far more than it moved the frame before (a lying body re-posed for a roll, a knockdown clip snapped to its landing), is cross-faded from where it was drawn over 110ms, relative to the root so the root's own motion passes straight through. Launched, thrown, falling and ragdoll bodies draw raw, air moves keep `drawOffset()`'s catch-up, and both end on a hit's contact frame, so hits still draw touching. Last of all the floor guard (Floors, Bodies on the floor) keeps every drawn joint on top of the surface under it. The one instant relocation left in a fight, a body jammed in the stairwell set down on the floor (`unwedge()`, the `relocate` event), leaves an afterimage where the body was and a flash at both spots.
  - **Measured** with a logger (40 CPU fights across all nine characters, three seeds by 90s each, plus the attract showcase, horde-ai, horde-floors-ai and floor-break-test; drawn head and hip per 60fps frame after the smoothing, side swaps by `bodyX()`, every write to a unit's `x` trapped with its stack): see Characters, **Measurements (side swaps)**.
  - Each fighter's walls come from the floor he's on, so after a floor break the attacker upstairs isn't pulled across to the lower floor's wall before he vaults.
- **Faint:** spend the last point and you faint. **A strike that empties the bar waits to see whether it connects** (`u.faintPending`): if it hits, you're left on 1 plus its refund with the bar flashing LOW, and no faint; if it's blocked, parried, broken or whiffs (into its recovery with no contact, or you're hit out of it), you faint then. The save needs the bar to have held at least half the strike's price at the press (`STAMINA.saveShare`); pressed on fumes, you faint at once, as before, so a fighter on 1 can't jab forever on hits that leave him on 1. A jump, a botch or anything else that empties the bar faints at once. Once fainted, the strike that emptied the bar still comes out, but nothing cancels out of it; once you're back on your feet you're out on them for 1.5s (the dizzy sway, a FAINT callout and a violet bar refilling): no attacks, no guard, no crouch, no jump, no run, and no walking either, because you're rooted where you stand (hits still knock you back as usual). You come to with 30 stamina. A knockdown, a launch or a throw wakes you early, and so does taking 20 damage while fainted, so it's a free string for the other guy, not a free round. Every hit on a fainted body counts in the same combo, so the decay below keeps running through it.
  - **Shake it off** (`shakeOff()` in `src/combat/sim.js`, `STAMINA.shakeGap`, `shakeCut` and `faintMin` in `src/combat/frameData.js`). Left and right, pressed alternately, shake your head clear. A fresh press in the opposite direction to the last one counted, at least 0.1s after it, is a shake: the head whips side to side with the shoulders a little behind it (the `headShake` reaction on the rig's additive layer, over the faint sway), a small "brr" plays, a star flies off the ring circling your head, and 0.1s comes off the faint, so the FAINT bar drains faster. The first press only starts it (the head shakes, nothing comes off). Holding a direction is one press, and pressing the same direction again isn't an alternation, so neither gains anything. A press inside the 0.1s gap is dropped and doesn't count as the turn, so alternating faster than that counts less often than a steady beat: flipping every 42ms clears it in 0.93s, a press every 100ms in 0.88s. A calmer six presses a second gets out in about 1s. The cuts never bring a faint under 0.7s in all, a backstop that the 0.1s gap already keeps you clear of. It only counts on your feet; in hitstun the head is busy reeling. On touch the stick (or a drag) left and right counts the same, and on a pad both the stick and the d-pad do.
  - **How it fits Mashing loses.** The shake is a rhythm with a speed limit. Mashing one direction or holding it does nothing, mashing faster than the limit does worse than a steady beat, and the attack buttons still do nothing while fainted. At its best it turns 1.5s into 0.88s, which is still long enough for the other fighter to walk in and start a string, so a faint is still a punish. Mashers don't gain from it either; see the measurements in Botches.
- **Combo decay:** nothing loops. A button press cancels only as the next hit of a string from the table below, so mashing one button gives you that button's string and then you wait out the recovery (1,1,1 is three jabs; a fourth jab can't cancel out of the third). A move cancels into itself at most once in a row outside a string. Hitstun shrinks from the 3rd hit of a combo (100, 100, 85, 65, 45, then 30%), so a long chain drops and the defender can guard. Damage is full for the first three hits, then 80, 65 and 50%, and once one combo has done 30 damage the rest counts a quarter. Juggles keep their own scaling and the four-hit cap, whichever is lower. Counter hits still do 35% more damage and stun.
- P or Esc pauses (the pause menu, see Arcade flow), F1 shows the quick move strip along the bottom of a fight (the full MOVE LIST is in the pause menu), H shows hitboxes. On a `?scene=` page G toggles horde mode, R resets and P just pauses.
- **Online match:** Esc or P (pad Start or Select, touch LEAVE in the top-right gutter, where HORDE usually sits) asks whether to leave. Pause, reset, horde and the difficulty keys do nothing in an online match; H and F1 still work, since they only change your own screen.
- **Sound:** M turns the music on and off, N skips to the next track. The menu button (three bars, in the top right; see The corner buttons below) opens the SETTINGS panel, and so does SETTINGS in the pause menu: a SOUND switch for everything, a MUSIC switch, the track playing with NEXT, MUSIC and EFFECTS volume sliders, on a touch screen CONTROLS (the size of the stick and buttons; see the touch entry below), GRAPHICS (AUTO, HIGH, MEDIUM, LOW, LOWEST; see Performance, Chromebooks), and the credits. Everything in it is upper case, the track's name included. The settings are remembered in localStorage (`stickfight-audio`). Browsers keep a page silent until it's touched, so the first tap or key starts the sound (the menu button pulses until then, and on desktop PRESS ANY KEY FOR SOUND sits to the left of the corner buttons). With SOUND off the menu button says so: it turns pink, its bottom bar shortens and a small X takes the bar's end.
  - **By keyboard or pad** (while the panel is open over a menu, it owns the menu keys): up and down move a cursor through SOUND and MUSIC (left and right between the two), NEXT, the two sliders, CONTROLS and RESET on a touch screen, and CLOSE; left and right turn a slider 5 points or step the controls' size; confirm presses; back (Backspace, B, Select), Start or P closes. Opened by a click or a tap there's no cursor until a key is used. Escape always closes the panel and does nothing else, on any screen: it doesn't also resume a paused game, pause a fight or open the online leave confirm. Over a live fight the keys stay the fighter's (B is a kick), so only Escape and the X close it there.
- **The corner buttons** (`src/corner.js`): pause, menu, fullscreen. One set, in that order, the same pill (3.5 by 2.25rem, its radius half its height, a dark face, the icon in the button's neon) on desktop and touch, in the top right on every screen. Pause shows only in a fight you can pause (the flow's `fighting` and `roundEnd`), never online, where LEAVE takes its place on touch. Fullscreen shows only where it can work.
  - **Touch** draws them in the touch layer, scaled with the controls' size: a column in the gutter beside the stage held sideways, a row in the strip above the stage held upright (The top-corner buttons, below).
  - **Desktop** draws them as a row inside the stage. They aren't in the very corner during a fight, and here is why: at 16:9 the stage fills the window, so there is no gutter, and the stage's own top-right corner is P2's health bar, name and move line (the HUD strip, the top 14% of the stage). So while the HUD is up (the title's demo, a fight, the pause menu, a spectated match, and any `?scene=` page) the row sits right under the strip, where a phone with no gutter puts its buttons too. On the menus with no HUD (mode select, the character select, the online screens) it moves up into the corner itself, because under the strip it would sit on the character sheet's corner. A click never leaves the keyboard focus on a corner button, so Space or Enter in a fight can't press one again.
  - Until 2026-11-10 they were a speaker and a fullscreen square in the stage's bottom-left corner on desktop, and a square pause button in the stage's top-left on touch. The speaker became three bars because the panel holds more than sound.
- **Fullscreen:** on desktop, F11 or the corner-brackets button, the last of the corner buttons (top right, after the menu button), puts the game in fullscreen and takes it back out; Esc leaves it the browser's way, and the button's icon follows. It works from every screen (title, menus, a fight, the pause menu), and neither the key nor the click reaches the game as an input. A fight's key hints add `F11  FULLSCREEN`. One `toggleFullscreen()` in `src/fullscreen.js` serves F11, that button and the touch layer's fullscreen button; a touch screen only gets the touch layer's. Nothing is offered where it can't work: no Fullscreen API (iPhone Safari, where the touch pill opens the Add to Home Screen how-to instead), installed as an app, or in a frame that doesn't allow fullscreen (the homepage's frame does). On a Mac, F11 is usually the system's Show Desktop key, so the button is the way in there.
- Gamepad, USB or Bluetooth (any pad the browser reports with the standard mapping: Xbox, PlayStation, 8BitDo, most Android pads): X/Y/A/B = 1/2/3/4, D-pad or left stick moves (tap a direction twice to dash, the same as the keyboard), L1/L2 guard, R1 throw (1+3), R2 run, Start or Select pause (in an online match, both ask to leave). R3 does nothing in the arcade flow. On a `?scene=` page Select restarts and R3 toggles horde. Menus are in the table below. Pressing any button hides the touch controls; touching the screen brings them back. Browsers don't count a pad press as a user gesture, so tap the screen once to turn the sound on.
- Touch devices get a floating stick (down crouches, away walks back and guards what's coming, and a quick double flick the same way is a dash: Fundamentals, Dashes) and a 2x2 diamond of arcade buttons in the same 1 2 / 3 4 layout plus GUARD (src/touch.js).
  - **What scrolls under a finger.** Nothing, by default: `<html>` and `<body>` are `touch-action: none` and `overscroll-behavior: none`, and a listener on the document cancels every `touchmove`, so the page never scrolls, pans, zooms or rubber-bands in a fight or on a menu. Cancelling a `touchmove` also stops the browser scrolling whatever box is under the finger, so a box that's meant to scroll has to opt out. It does that with three things: the attribute `data-scroll="y"` (or `"x"`), its own `touch-action: pan-y` (or `pan-x`), and `overscroll-behavior: contain` so the scroll stops at its ends instead of passing to the page. The guard then leaves a one-finger drag on it alone, and only while its content is longer than the box; a box whose content fits is guarded like the rest. Today that's the MOVE LIST and its tabs (`src/flow/movelist.js`), the SETTINGS panel (`src/audio/ui.js`: it's taller than the screen on a phone held sideways with Safari's bars up, 344 px of panel in a 280 px window; its sliders stay `touch-action: none`, so a drag that starts on one is still the slider's) and the home-screen how-to (`src/homescreen.js`). The pause menu's TECHNIQUES and CONTROLS panel doesn't scroll and doesn't need to: `fit()` zooms the whole menu into the stage (Menus, below). Add `data-scroll` to any new box with `overflow: auto`, or it won't scroll on a phone.
  - **Sized in rem, and adjustable.** A thumb is the same size on every screen, so nothing in the touch layer is sized from the viewport: the buttons, the stick and its knob, the gaps, the labels, the borders and the top-corner buttons (pause included) are all multiples of `--tc-u`, which is `1rem` times `--tc-scale` on `<html>`. The numbers live in one table (`D` in `src/touch.js`) that both the CSS and the fit check read. At scale 1 a main button is 4.5rem (72 px; it was 60 px on an iPhone SE held sideways), the stick 7.5rem with a 3.5rem knob, a top-corner button 3.5 by 2.25rem. The root font size is never set from the viewport. Only the stick *zone* (the invisible area where a press starts the stick) and the safe-area insets follow the viewport, and the zone is never smaller than the resting stick. The stick's travel (dead zone, run, up and down) scales with it, so the knob reaching the rim means RUN at every size.
  - **The setting** is CONTROLS in the SETTINGS panel, on a touch screen only (a desktop never shows it): minus, five rings, plus, and RESET. The steps are SMALL 0.85, NORMAL 1 (the default), LARGE 1.13, LARGER 1.26 and EXTRA LARGE 1.4 (`TOUCH_STEPS` in `src/touchPrefs.js`). While the panel is open `<html>` has `.tc-preview` and the controls show behind it on every screen, menus included (there they're only a picture), so each press is seen at once. Held upright the panel sits over the stage, so the controls under it stay in view.
  - **Fit** (`fit()` in `src/touch.js`, on load, on a size change, and on resize or rotation). The scale in use is the player's, capped at the largest at which nothing touches: across, the stick and the buttons clear each other and the side insets; down, the top-corner buttons, a gap and the buttons fit between the top and bottom insets. When the cap bites, the panel says so beside the step's name (LARGER · SCREEN FITS 121%). It bites in one case on current phones: upright on a narrow phone, where the stick beside two buttons is wider than the screen (an iPhone SE, 375 px wide, tops out at about 1.21, so LARGER and EXTRA LARGE both show at that). Sideways, every step fits on a 667x375 iPhone SE and a 640x360 Android.
  - **The top-corner buttons** (pause in a fight, or LEAVE online, then menu, then fullscreen; never more than three at once) stack in the pillarbox gutter beside the stage when it's wide enough for them. When it isn't (an iPhone SE has no gutter, and a notch's safe area eats it) they go under the HUD strip instead, as a column, or as a row when a column plus the buttons under it would be taller than the screen. Held upright they sit in a strip above the HUD band, which grows with the size.
  - **Held upright: the view** (`src/square.js`, `fit()` in `src/touch.js`; `html.sq`). Upright, the 16:9 stage was a quarter of the screen with about 500 px of nothing between it and the controls. Now the page shows **a crop of the same 1280x720 frame, always as wide as the screen** and the frame's full height. It's square (the middle 720x720) when the screen has room, and on a shorter screen it gets shorter, not narrower, and shows a wider slice of the frame: the crop is 720 x (view width / view height) frame px, up to the whole 1280, which is the plain 16:9 frame the old stage showed (`cropOf()` in `src/square.js`). So it's a crop anywhere between 1:1 and 16:9. Until 2026-11-11 a short screen shrank the square instead and left a dark bar at each side; any phone with its browser's bars showing landed there. The numbers below are for the square. The frame is drawn 1.78x bigger, and up close a fighter comes out about 1.6x the size he was (116 px tall on a 390 px phone, from 73, in the same frame of the same seeded fight), because the camera sits a little further out to keep both fighters inside the narrower view. The render, the sim and online play are untouched: both canvases are still 1280x720 and CSS clips the sides. From the top:
    1. **The top buttons**, in the strip they were in (`--tc-strip`).
    2. **The HUD band** (`#hudband`, a canvas `main.js` makes): both health and stamina bars, the names and their P1 / CPU markers, the round pips (or the horde's FLOOR and WAVE), the timer and ROUND, and the move hints, P1 on the left and P2 on the right. Under ROUND goes the run's score or the online tally. It's drawn by the same code as the in-frame strip (`bar()`, `timer()` and `hint()` in `src/combat/hud.js`, given the `BAND_L` layout instead of `WIDE`), so the two can't drift, and while it's showing the frame draws no bars or names. It's 80 units tall on a 390-unit width, scaled to the screen (0.9x to 1.5x). A name too long for its bar (WELLNESS COACH) gets smaller and then gives up its marker; a hint too long for one line shrinks, never under 12.5 units. The compact form (56 units, on a short screen) drops the hint line, and the score or tally takes ROUND's place.
    3. **The view.** `#stage` stays a 16:9 box, as wide as the view and centred on it, so every menu and overlay lays out exactly as it did sideways-up before (title, mode select, pause, the online and crew screens, the spectator bar, COPY LINK). The character select is the exception: it takes the whole viewport as a carousel (see Arcade flow, Character select). Only the two canvases grow past the box to the view's height, with their sides clipped to the crop. The CSS gets that from three numbers `fit()` sets from the crop, never from constants: `--sq-z` (the canvas over the box, 1280 / crop), `--sq-over` (how much of the box hangs past each edge) and `--sq-clip` (how much of the canvas is clipped off each side); the canvas, the body's top padding, the veil, the credits link, the online readout and the spectator bar all work from them. A menu's veil covers the whole view. Everything the HUD draws in the frame keeps to the crop (`hud.setSquare`, given x0..x1 from it): callouts and the hit counter, the overhead status lines and botch lines, FIGHT and KO (a long banner is scaled to fit), the title card and its START button, the DEMO tag, and the perfect finisher's stamp.
    4. **The controls** at the bottom, the size the player set. They never overlap the view, and a press on the view never starts the stick.
  - **When the screen is too short** for all four, things give way in this order, and a control is never cut off: the view's height, down to three quarters of its width (a 960 crop); the band, to its compact form; the view's height again, down to the whole 16:9 frame (so a fighter is never smaller than he was before the upright view); the controls, down to 60%; and last, the view to whatever is left. The view stays as wide as the screen through all but that last step, which takes a screen far smaller than a phone with its browser bars showing. Measured (`scripts/shots/portrait.mjs --check`, controls at NORMAL; 395x700 and 412x640 are Chrome on Android with its bars showing):

    | Screen | Top buttons | HUD band | View | Crop | Gap | Controls | Fighter, close / walls apart |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | 390x844 | 36 px | 80 px | 390x390 | 720 | 104 px | 100% (72 px buttons) | 116 / 17 px |
    | 412x915 | 36 px | 85 px | 412x412 | 720 | 148 px | 100% | 123 / 18 px |
    | 375x667 | 36 px | 77 px | 375x312 | 865 | 8 px | 100% | 103 / 17 px |
    | 395x700 | 36 px | 81 px | 395x341 | 834 | 8 px | 100% | 111 / 17 px |
    | 412x640 | 36 px | 59 px (compact) | 412x303 | 979 | 8 px | 100% | 102 / 18 px |

    The square's order put the bands first and the controls last, and that's kept: the view gives up height before the band drops its hints, and both before the controls get smaller than the player set them.
  - **Upright is a first-class way to play** (since 2026-11-11). Nothing nudges a player to turn the phone: there's no "turn your phone sideways" note, fullscreen (the pill, F11 and the homepage's FULL SCREEN) leaves the phone the way it's held, and the installed app turns freely (`"orientation": "any"`). Sideways plays exactly as before.
  - **What the crop costs.** Only the width is cut (on a square view; a wider crop costs less), so with the fighters apart the camera has to zoom out sooner (Camera, below), and at the far end they're the size they always were. Zoomed out the square also shows more above and below the room: the floor under it and the night sky over the building. A true square render with art made for it is a separate job.
  - **Checked** (2026-11-10, Playwright with touch emulation, headless Chromium): every step at 667x375, 375x667, 932x430, 430x932 and 640x360, with no safe areas and again with a notch's (47 px sides sideways; 47 top and 34 bottom upright, set through the DevTools safe-area override). In all 50 cases no button, stick or label touches another, the HUD strip or a safe area, nothing leaves the screen, and the stick zone covers the stick.
  - **Saved** in localStorage as `stick-fight-touch` (`{ scale, at }`, `at` being when the player last changed it; every storage call wrapped) and read before the layer is first drawn, so the controls don't jump. Signed in, it also goes to the account (Multiplayer, Profiles and sign-in).

### The look

Neon arcade: Bungee and Teko, cyan, pink, amber and violet on a deep purple night, square edges. Never a terminal look.

**Readable text never glows. Only decorative text may.** Glow smears small letters into the busy room behind them, so anything a player is meant to read is a solid color (the same neon family, a little lighter) inside a tight dark outline.

- **Readable, no glow:** menu items and buttons, labels, names, taglines, stats, move names, inputs and notes, hints, legends, status lines, toasts, room codes, typed fields, settings panels, the site's section headings and body copy.
- **Decorative, glow allowed:** the STICK FIGHT wordmarks, the big sign headings (SELECT FIGHTER, PAUSED, GAME OVER, a site page's one big title), arcade callouts and numerals (the combo and round banners, the timer, the continue countdown, the online set score), the digits and icons on the touch buttons, signs in the arena.
- **When it's unclear, it's readable.** A heading that carries something to read (a room code, a player's or crew's name) is plain even at heading size.
- A box's neon border or a button's glowing outline is decoration and stays. The rule is about the letters. Fighters in the arena never glow at all.

How to write it:

- **Canvas (the HUD):** `plainText()` in `src/combat/hud.js` for readable text, `neonText()` for decorative.
- **The DOM:** `PLAIN` in `src/plaintext.js` is the one `text-shadow` for readable text: a firm dark outline with a short dark falloff, never a color.
- **The menus (`src/flow/ui.js`):** `#flow` sets `text-shadow: var(--plain)` (that outline) and everything inherits it, so a new label is plain without doing anything. Only `.fl-h1`, `.fl-h2`, `.fl-count` and `.fl-sn` put a glow back; add `.fl-read` to a heading that carries a code or a name. The move list, the online panel, the SETTINGS panel, the controls legend and the touch labels do the same in their own stylesheets.
- **The site (`site/site.css`):** the backgrounds are flat and dark, so readable text is just a solid color with no shadow. Glow is on `.s-logo`, `.s-title`, `.s-h1` and `.s-pillar-n` only.
- Dark letters on a lit chip or a focused button take `text-shadow: none`.
- Never a colored `text-shadow`, a `drop-shadow` filter or a canvas `shadowBlur` on text that's read.

### HUD

The fight HUD is its own 2D canvas over the game (`src/combat/hud.js`), in the arena's neon (Bungee and Teko, no terminal look; glow on its decorative text only, see The look). Nothing on it may cover a live fighter (owner requirement 5): every block is laid out against the fighters' screen boxes and steps aside or fades.

- **Held upright** the strip (bars, names, pips, timer, hints, score) is drawn on a band above the view by this same code, and everything else keeps to the view's crop (Controls, Held upright: the view).
- **The bars.** Two slanted health bars with a thin stamina bar under each (it flashes pink with LOW under 35, and shows FAINT while fainted), round pips at the inner end (one per round win the match needs), and the ringed timer with ROUND under it. The timer is a 33px ring with a 38px number (`TIMER` in `src/combat/hud.js`); its ring drains with the share of the round left, and with no time limit it shows ∞ on a full ring. In the last 10 seconds the ring and number turn pink and pulse once a second: the timer swells about 28% and flares the moment the number ticks over, then settles before the next one, on the sim's clock, so it holds in a pause or a hitstop. In an online fight the room's running tally shows small under ROUND, from your side on both screens: `YOU 3–2 RIVAL` (plus draws when there are any), from `flow.hud().tally`; it fades if a fighter is under it.
