- **The sim announces the outcome:** `sim.result = { over, winner, reason, final }`, with `reason` one of `'ko'`, `'time'` or `'heroDown'`. Only `roundKO()` (from `onKO`) and the clock set it, and the match end adds `final: true`. A new round starts it back at not over. It's part of the snapshot, so rollback and resync carry it. The flow and `main.js` read it (Arcade flow).

Two things stay on the hit's step, as they always have. The FINISH callout is logged between the hit's own log entries, and the log is part of the rollback state, so it's still sent from `koBody()`, which only asks `rules().endsRound()` whether this KO ends the round. And a trade that KOs both fighters still gives the round to the later hit, because KOs resolve in the order they happened rather than by unit index.

**1v1 didn't change by a bit.** Each of these was run on main and on this build:

- `npm run netsim:proof -- --hashes FILE` writes every reference step's state hash, minus `result` (main doesn't have it). Copy `scripts/netsim/proof.mjs` and `client.mjs` into a main worktree and run it there too. All 45,000 hashes matched, and the proof's own output matched line for line (0 of 20,755 restored steps differ, 0 of 7,200 resync steps).
- `node scripts/ko/fights.mjs OUT [--root DIR]` runs 36 CPU-vs-CPU fights on short bars (12 pairings, 3 seeds, 6,000 steps each) and hashes every step. That's 216 round ends: 213 plain-hit KOs (166 of them FINISH) and 3 juggle KOs. All 216,000 hashes matched.
- `node scripts/ko/scenes.mjs record OUT [--root DIR]`, then `diff A B`, runs every 1v1 `?scene=` test timeline phase by phase (`{ only: k }`, 9 s each). It compares the whole sim log in order, every unit's end health, and a per-frame digest of both bodies, the round state and the callouts. All 296 phases from 48 scenes were identical.
- `scripts/shots/camtrace.mjs` against main's build: every 1v1 scene's camera was identical at all three sizes.

**Horde did change, a little.** `node scripts/ko/horde.mjs [--root DIR]` runs each horde scene for a minute (main / now): `horde` (a hero who presses nothing) 3 downs / 3 downs. `horde-ai` 33 goons and 4 floors / 30 and 4. `horde-floors-ai` 28 and 4 / 26 and 4. No run ended early, and no warnings were logged. The runs match unit for unit until the first FLOOR CLEAR. After that, the last goon leaves the floor standing instead of in whatever state the hit left it in, and while every goon is off the floor the CPU hero is handed that hidden goon as his opponent, so the two runs drift apart. A goon KO also sends the same `hit` event a 1v1 KO does: strength by damage, where the old horde path sent 1, so the camera kicks a little less, and the horde camera parts ways from main at about frame 70. `npm run test:ko` (`scripts/ko/guard.mjs`) is the regression guard. A source check confirms only `roundKO()` and the clock set `sim.phase = 'ko'`, only they and the match end write `sim.result`, and only `goonKO()` calls `goonDown()`. Then, in a horde run, it drops a goon to 0 health with no KO reported (on his feet, in the air, on the floor, in hitstun), which is what a brand-new hit branch that forgot `koBody()` would do. Each time the run keeps going, the goon counts once, and the next one comes in, and the hero at 0 still ends the run with `heroDown`. In 1v1 the same drop is a KO for the other side, and TIME gives `'time'`.

### States and the flags they own (2026-11-12)

A unit carries a lot of short-lived flags beside its `state`: a strike's `glide`, a launcher's `followThrough` and `chase`, a grab's `grabbedBy` and `grabTo`, a knockdown's `splatPending` and `wallBounce`, the ground hit's `otgHit`, and more. Each one means something in a state or two and nothing anywhere else. Before this, about 40 places set `u.state` directly and cleared the flags they remembered to, and `setState()` only cleaned up `phase`, `move` and `crouch`, so a flag set in one state could still be set three states later and get read there. `goonKO()` clearing `grabbedBy`, `grabTo`, `glide`, `chase` and `followThrough` by hand was the sign of it.

**One door.** Every change of `u.state` in `src/combat/sim.js` goes through `enter(u, to)`, and `setState()` is `enter()` plus the clip and the crouch rule. `grep -nE "\.state = '" src/combat/sim.js` shows only `spawn()`, which builds a fresh unit for the round, with a comment saying so. `makeUnit()` builds one as an object literal, and the snapshot restore (`src/combat/snapshot.js`) puts a saved state back rather than changing it. The switch on `u.state` in `updateUnit()` stays as it was. A test scene that poses a body (`horde-ko-test` in `src/main.js`) uses the same door, `sim.enter`.

**The table.** `STATE_OWNS` in `src/combat/sim.js`, next to `enter()`, lists the transient flags each state owns. A flag lives in the states that list it. When a unit leaves a state for one that doesn't list the flag, `enter()` puts it back to its rest value (`FLAG_REST`). A flag set just before the state changes (`applyHit` sets `splatPending`, then picks the knockdown) carries into a state that owns it.

| State | Owns | Why |
|---|---|---|
| `attack` | `move`, `phase`, `launchCancelAt`, `otgConfirm`, `lowStance`, `wakeUp`, `grabOK`, plus the ride | the strike out now, its cancel windows, a wake-up strike's lying part, a throw's lock-on |
| `idle`, `walk`, `run` | the ride: `glide`, `followThrough`, `chase` | a launcher's follow-through glides the attacker to where the body lands, and a launch cancelled with a direction keeps riding into a walk until the glide runs out |
| `grabbed` | `grabbedBy`, `grabTo`, `grabT`, `grabNoBreak` | who holds him, where he's pulled to, how long, whether it can be broken |
| `knockdown` | `splatPending`, `wallBounce`, `groundBounce`, `juggle`, `breakOK` | trick shots waiting for the wall or the floor, whether it can be juggled, whether it can go over a railing |
| `splat` | `splatPending`, `wallBounce`, `groundBounce`, `breakOK` | a splat slides back into the knockdown it came from |
| `hitstun` | `stun`, `breakOK` | the floor break is decided on the hit, before it's known whether he reels or falls |
| `blockstun` | `stun` | |
| `ko` | `breakOK` | a KO'd body can still go over a railing |
| `down` | `otgHit`, `downPin` | the knockdown's one ground hit, and the stomp's pin |
| `roll`, `getup` | `rolled` (and `rollCross` in `roll`) | one roll per knockdown, read until the getup ends |
| `vault`, `landing`, `dash`, `perfect` | `vaultPhase`, `landLag`, `dashDir`, `pfStage` | |

Left out on purpose, because something reads them after the state has changed: `downTimer` (the wake-up strike's log entry reads it after `startAttack`, and the log is rollback state), `splatSide` (the hit that knocks a body off the wall reads it after the knockdown starts), `shockT` (ELECTRICIAN's CPU reads the rival's on the next step; `tick()` already ends it at his next update), `botchKind` (a botch in the air sets it after the knockdown starts; `tick()` ends it too), `rootT` (a status that runs past the stun on purpose; `floored()` ends it) and `crouch` (`setState()`'s own rule keeps it through blockstun, and a strike sets it from its frame data). Flags that are set on entry and read only in their own state (`splatT`, `rollT`, `getupSteps`) need no entry. A new transient flag goes in the table with its rest value, or in that list with its reason.

The table is plain data the sim keeps, worked out per pair of states the first time a unit makes that change. Nothing is stored on a unit, so snapshots and rollback stay plain values.

**What changed, and why it's a fix.** `node scripts/netsim/behave.mjs OUT [--root DIR] [--only RUN] [--dump STEP]` plays 108 CPU-vs-CPU runs (every character, versus and horde, three seeds, 6,000 steps each) and hashes every step's whole state without the flags `enter()` clears, so a flag that only lingered unread can't show up and anything it changed can. Run one process per run (`--only`), since the learned reach table carries from one run to the next. Against main, 101 of the 108 runs matched on every step. Each of the 7 that parted from main did so at a step where a stale flag was read:

- **A glide that fired in hitstun or blockstun** (horde CEO v MANAGER seed 11 at step 584, horde MANAGER v SALES REP seed 22 at 5887, versus MANAGER v RECEPTIONIST seed 11 at 355, versus CHEF v SECURITY seed 33 at 4870). A travelling strike's glide starts a few frames in (`glide.from`). Hit or grabbed before then, he kept it, because `applyHit` and `grab()` never cleared the defender's glide, and when it started it slid him toward the attacker at 144 to 184 px/s while he reeled, overriding the planted-feet rule in `physics()`. In CHEF v SECURITY it slid him out of a throw break.
- **A launch's follow-through still set long after** (horde SECURITY v BARISTA seed 11 at step 4763, horde HR REP v WELLNESS seed 22 at 2576, horde JANITOR v WELLNESS seed 33 at 3272). `followThrough` and `chase` from a launch thousands of steps earlier survived the hero being hit, knocked down, rolling and getting up. Later a stop in idle eased out at the chase's friction (0.975 a step instead of a stop), and in SECURITY v BARISTA a strike out of a run read it as "a cancel keeps its momentum" and slid at run speed (−414 px/s) instead of planting.

`npm run netsim:proof` still finds no difference after any rollback or resync (0 of 20,146 restored steps, 0 of 7,200 resync steps). Its bouts 1, 2, 3 and 5 played exactly as on main. Bout 4 (WINDOW WASHER v INTERN) parted at step 3322 for the same reason: unit 0's launcher and unit 1's hit traded on one step, the launch gave unit 0 its follow-through glide (−940 px/s) and the hit then put him in hitstun, and on main he slid about 39 px toward the body while he reeled. So the bout's line now ends in round 2 at step 4,564 instead of round 1 at 2,435, and the proof's rollback counts move with it.

**Per-step invariants.** `src/combat/invariants.js` checks the sim after a step and returns the first thing that's wrong, or null. `npm run netsim:invariants` (`scripts/netsim/invariants.mjs`) runs it after every tick of 108 CPU-vs-CPU runs: every character in versus (against a different character for each seed, on short bars so rounds and matches turn over) and in horde (as the hero against CPU goons), seeds 11, 22 and 33, the CPUs at 0.9, 0.5 and 1, 6,000 steps each, 648,000 steps in all. That's about 25 seconds, so CI runs it before every deploy. The first violation stops it and prints the run, the seed, the step, the unit, the last `sim.log` entries and the command that repeats it. `--steps` and `--seeds` change the run, and `--durations` prints the longest stretch spent in each state. On the dev server, `?invariants` loads the checker into any scene through `sim.afterTick` and logs the first violation to the console and `window.__invariant`. `npm run test:invariants:scenes` (`scripts/netsim/invariants-scenes.mjs`) does that for every scene in headless Chromium, 20 s each (58 scenes, about 139,000 steps, about 30 s). Nothing in `src/combat/sim.js` imports the checker, so without the flag a page never loads it (the build keeps it in its own 2.5 KB file), and the sim's only cost is a null check a step. It's off in the data too: the stuck counts live in a `WeakMap` of the checker's, never on a unit. The rules:

- **Nobody stands at 0, nobody lies KO'd with health left.** `resolveKOs()` has run by the end of every step, so a visible unit at 0 health is in `ko`, and a `ko` unit is at 0. Health is never over the bar, and position, speed and health are finite numbers.
- **Grabs.** `grabbedBy` and `grabTo` are set only while `grabbed`, and the holder is on the floor (not hidden) and either holding him (in his throw, frozen for the hold) or knocked out of it during this hold. The last case is horde's, where a second goon hits the hero mid-throw and the victim drops when `grabT` runs out (`landThrow`).
- **Glide.** A strike's glide belongs to the strike. The only glide outside an attack is the launcher's follow-through (no `from`) riding into idle, walk or run after a launch cancel, which is designed (see the table). That ride is why the rule isn't simply "no glide outside an attack".
- **Every flag in `STATE_OWNS` is at rest outside the states that own it.** This is the general form of the two above, and the one that catches a flag written in the wrong state (the table only clears flags when a unit leaves the state that owns them). It also covers `move` and `phase` outside an attack, and an attack is always a known move in a known phase.
- **A hidden unit** (a goon off the floor, a KO'd body in the pile or thrown at the camera) is `idle` or `ko`.
- **Nobody's stuck.** Each non-free state has a limit, several times the longest seen in CPU play (`--durations`), with the reason next to it in `STUCK_STEPS`: for example 600 steps of hitstun (stun is under a second; a combo restarts the state), 900 of knockdown (`unwedge()` sets down any ragdoll still up after 600), 600 of `down` (`DOWN_MAX` is 120), 1,800 of `ko` (the KO hold, a perfect round and the WINS card). Idle, walk, run, block and crouch are the player's choice and have none.
- **The outcome.** `sim.result.over` is true exactly while the KO card or the WINS card is up. A horde run only ever ends with `reason: 'heroDown'`, and a 1v1 round with `'ko'` or `'time'`.

**What it found.** The first full run stopped twice on bugs the table can't catch, because the flag was written in the wrong state, not left over from one:

- **A trade's second hit wrote a launch's follow-through onto a reeling attacker** (versus HELPDESK v COURIER seed 11, step 1085). In `resolveHits` a trade applies both hits in turn. HELPDESK's jump jab put COURIER in hitstun, then COURIER's jab launched the airborne HELPDESK, and `applyHit` gave COURIER the launcher's ride: a −940 px/s glide, `followThrough`, `chase` and `launchCancelAt`, all in hitstun. Now, after both hits, `restFlags()` puts each unit's flags back to what its state owns.
- **A KO's follow-through went to a hero the same trade knocked down** (horde RECEPTIONIST v BARISTA seed 33, step 461). The hero's jump cross KO'd a goon while the goon's air roundhouse knocked him down. `goonKO()` runs after the hits, and it sent the knocked-down hero "straight on to the next goon" (60 px/s and a `launchCancelAt`). Now `goonKO()` and `koBody()` give the ride only to an attacker still in his attack.

The scene sweep found one more, which CPU play never reached: **a new round kept the old round's flags.** `spawn()` builds a fresh unit for each round, but it didn't reset `launchCancelAt`, `juggle`, `grabOK`, `pfStage` and the rest, so a fighter caught mid-strike when a round ended (or a test scene switched) started the next round idle with them set. Now `spawn()` puts every flag in `FLAG_REST` back to rest. `horde-ko-test`'s helper in `src/main.js`, which poses the hero and the goons, now goes through the door too (`sim.enter`) instead of writing `u.state`.

With those fixes, `behave.mjs` against main has 98 of 108 runs matching on every step. The 3 more that part from main all part at a trade: horde LAWYER v HELPDESK seed 22 at step 3552 (on main `goonKO` gave the reeling hero 60 px/s and a `launchCancelAt`), versus RECEPTIONIST v ACCOUNTANT seed 33 at 1306 (a double KO: on main the second `koBody` pushed the body the first hit had already KO'd at −460 px/s), and versus RECEPTIONIST v COURIER seed 22 at 419 (on main the KO gave the attacker, already in hitstun, −400 px/s). `netsim:proof` prints the same lines as after the table alone. And every 1v1 test scene plays exactly as on main: `node scripts/ko/scenes.mjs record OUT --omit stun` (new flag: the table resets `stun` when a unit leaves hitstun, which changed the frame digest without changing the fight) on main and on this build, then `diff`, gives all 296 phases of the 48 scenes identical, with the same log, health, bodies, joints and callouts on every frame.

**Breaking it on purpose** (each on a scratch copy, then `npm run netsim:invariants`):

- `enter()` without the table (`leaving()` returns nothing): fails at versus TEMP v INTERN seed 11, step 349, `stun = 24 outside hitstun/blockstun` on INTERN in knockdown after a NEON TORNADO.
- The trade's `restFlags()` commented out: fails at versus HELPDESK v COURIER seed 11, step 1085, `glide {"vx":-940,"until":1096} in hitstun`.
- `goonKO()`'s `att.glide = null` commented out: still green, and that's expected. The attacker is still in his attack there, where a glide is his, and once he leaves it `enter()` clears the glide anyway. The table now covers what that line was covering by hand.

### Floors

- **The building** is a cutaway of two office floors (3 and 2 on the elevator's indicators), 2560px wide, twice the first building, with a straight flight of stairs near each end. It's symmetric for the fight: the walls are at x 125 (the elevator core) and 2435 (the outer wall, the street beyond it), each floor's spawns sit 220px either side of the middle (1060 and 1500), and each half has a stairwell 325px in from its wall (x 450 to 790 and 1770 to 2110), so neither side starts with a hole behind it. Each floor's floor is a wet plane running from the back wall (the line fighters stand on) forward to the cut edge, then the concrete slab cut through, and the slab's underside is the ceiling of the floor below. A stairwell is a hole through the main floor's plane and slab; its guard rail is fixed to the far lip, with its end posts on the solid floor either side, and the flight comes up through it. Beyond each stairwell is a corner of solid floor before the wall (the first building had 290px past its stairwell; this one has 325), so the wall game is still there: a water cooler stands in the elevator's corner and a fire hose cabinet hangs in the other. Between the stairwells: a set of double doors under a green EXIT sign on each half, the vending machine and the copier facing each other, a clock and a pinboard, two windows either side of a pillar (the right one's smashed pane lets the rain in), four ceiling tubes and ten desks on the floor above. Downstairs the fight uses the room between the two stair feet (x 875 to 1685): the lit doorway, the TV and the status board, a corridor to a far door, a green-windowed door, three pendant lamps, two sconces and an exit arrow. Under each flight is its stringer wall with a storage door in it, and beyond each flight's high end the stair shaft, with a window to the street and lockers. Everything stands on a floor, hangs from a ceiling or is mounted on a wall, each flight bears on the slab at its head and the floor at its foot, the mirror stops at the walls and at both holes, and the only thing in front of the cut is the street lamp outside, whose pole runs down to the street (out past the outer wall at every zoom). Layout constants and the drawing are in `src/arena/building.js`; the skyline outside covers the wider parallax range (the far layer is redrawn in 64px steps as the camera crosses the building, `fx.far.base`), the rain still comes only through the smashed pane, and there are 37 static lights (the compositor keeps the strongest on screen within each tier's cap: at most 28 of 32 at full, 16 of 16 on the phone tier, the same as before).
- **The stairs** go both ways. Each flight is 17 steps of 25px run by 20px rise (slope 0.8), from its **head** on the main floor (x 450 and 2110) down toward the middle to its **foot** on the lower floor (x 875 and 1685). A fighter on a flight stands on the line through the treads' middles, and the rig's pose is fitted to the steps (`slopePose()` in `src/combat/sim.js`): each foot near the floor goes onto the tread under it, its knee half as far, and a body lying down is laid along the slope on the treads' front edges (see Bodies on the floor below). Joints are the hurtboxes and hitboxes, so that fitting is the geometry the fight uses.
  - **Up** from the lower floor: walk past a foot. The lower floor is the room between the feet, and past a foot the ground is the flight going up; at the top you walk off the head onto the main floor.
  - **Down** from the main floor: at a stair head, hold **down and toward the flight** (down-forward toward the middle). Within 24px short of the head to 40px down the flight that steps you onto it, and for the first 60px down holding it keeps walking instead of crouching. A crouch at the head with no direction, or a crouch-walk toward the wall, stays upstairs. Walking along the main floor over the hole (the walking strip behind the rail) never takes you down; that's the only branch, because a flight's foot meets the lower floor's own end.
  - **Fighting on the steps:** stand, walk, run, guard, strike, jump, get hit, knocked down and lie there, get up, roll and wake up all work on a flight. The higher fighter has the height because his body is higher: a jab from above meets the head, a standing guard holds, a string carries the lower man down the steps. Lows follow the treads (a planted foot sits on the tread under it), so a sweep up the steps still takes the feet. Throws pull the victim along the slope; knockdowns and KO'd bodies lie along it; the stomp and the ground hits work on a body lying there. Two fighters can touch (hits, pushboxes, stomps, throws, the guard's threat) when they're on the same floor, on the same flight within 170px of height, on a flight and the floor at its foot within 170px, or on a flight within 70px of its head and the main floor (`sameLevel()`); anyone further down a flight is in the hole, under the floor the other one walks on. There are no walls on a flight (the walls are the building's ends), and the lying-body rules (stand over a body, never come out its far side) work along x as before.
  - *CPU.* When its opponent is on another level it takes the stairs (`src/combat/stairNav.js`): from the lower floor to the foot of the flight that makes the shorter trip and up it, from the main floor to that flight's head and down with down + toward, along a flight toward his height. Once the two can touch, the normal CPU takes over. It hunts the rail behind its opponent (the stairwell nearest him) and never backs into the one nearest itself.
  - Sound: a step on a tread is a harder, higher knock with a short ring (`sfx.footstep({ stair })`). The CONTROLS screen's note and the F1 strip say how to go up and down.
- **Floor break:** an ender, a throw, a launcher or a heavy knockback that carries someone over either railing sends him tumbling down that flight: a FLOOR BREAK callout and 10 extra damage when he lands, at the foot or on the steps. The attacker vaults the rail after him (from the side of the opening he's on), and the vault drops him onto the flight under it; the fight goes on from there. Once per round, as before, and the next round starts upstairs. The trip isn't one way any more: either of them can walk back up.
- The tumble is a ragdoll on the stair treads (one way, from above, and solid from the side: a joint stops at a riser), sent the way its flight goes down, and through the flight its walls are the opening's head-side edge and the treads' far end, so it can't drop past the head into the shaft. It now and then jams: in the opening above the slab (a straight push down), or stalled on a tread (a nudge toward the next tread down). A fall that stops making headway for 0.9s, or runs past 3s, is set down flat where it is, on the steps if it's over them (and any ragdoll still up after 5s is set down on its floor). Where a ragdoll comes to rest decides where it lies (`restOnSurface()`): on a flight over the steps, on the main floor past a head, otherwise on the floor below.
- **Bodies on the floor** (2026-11-09; the owner: "it's too easy to phase through the floor depending on how the player gets hit into it"). Bodies hit the floor and stop there: no drawn joint's edge (its position plus the head's radius, the limb's half width at that joint, the torso's) goes below the surface under it, on either floor, the stairwell lips or a flight's treads. Fixed where each dip came from:
  - *The ground snap and the point simulation* used one radius for every joint (half a limb's width), and the neck and chest are drawn wider, so a body lying on its back sank 1.5 to 3 px. Each joint now touches the floor at its drawn radius (`jointRadii()` in `src/fighter/ground.js`, never less than the old half limb width, so a standing pose stands exactly where it did).
  - *Stairs.* A body lying on a flight was laid along the walking line through the treads' middles and sank half a rise (10 px) into the front of every step, more with its head; a ragdoll on the steps slid on the same line. Both now use the line through the treads' front edges, the nosings, which is what a body on steps rests on (`nosingY()` in `src/combat/sim.js`, the flights' `solid` lines in `src/arena/building.js`). After the slope fit, anything still under a tread is fitted back on top of the real tread tops (`fitToGround()`): a long stride's rear foot up the steps is set on its tread with its knee half as far, a limb turns up off a step about its near joint, and a body lying at a flight's foot with its head toward the steps is propped up against them. That runs in the air too (a keyframed knockdown or a jump coming down onto the steps).
  - *The tumble down a stairwell.* The treads stopped a joint only when it came down onto one from above, and a bounce rewrote the point's last position, so a later pass of the solver thought it had come from below and let the bones pull it under: heads and knees ended up 50 px inside the flight. The one-way test now uses where the joint was at the start of the step, and a joint that comes at a step from the side stops at its riser (a flight is solid down to the floor under it, the slab 80 px thick).
  - *The vault* faced away from the opening when the man started inside his rail spot (he ran back out to it), so the clip dropped him through the solid floor beside the hole; it now always faces the opening, and its legs stop on the treads as it comes down.
  - *Cloth* (`src/fighter/cloth.js`) hung straight through the floor under a body lying down (a lanyard 20 px, an apron's hem 40). Every point, the anchor included, now keeps its drawn thickness (`thick`, `endR`) above the floor and slides along it.
  - *The pile* (`addBody()`): the lying pose's head and feet drew 1 to 4 px into the floor with their rim, and the settle could start from a pose under it. Both are lifted onto the floor, so the tween between them never passes under it.
  - *The floor guard* (`floorFor()` in `src/main.js`), the last word before a body is drawn, for whatever the drawing layers add after the sim (the turnaround squash on a flight mirrors a pose fitted to the steps, the snap fade, the air catch-up, the life layer's springs and hit wave): `fitToGround()` against the surface the sim uses for that body, from the hips out. The hips go on the floor with the body; a spine segment, upper arm, thigh or shin whose far end would go under turns up about its near joint until it clears (the limb bends or slides along the floor, its bones keep their length); a planted foot is set on the surface under it, never slid; whatever still can't clear lifts the body as a whole. It changes nothing when nothing is under the floor, which is nearly every frame, so a hit still draws touching. The life layer runs it before the cloth hangs off the body, and the horde's flying goons go through it too.
  - *Measured* with the floor logger (`node scripts/floorlog/run.mjs`, page side `scripts/floorlog/probe.js`): every scripted test scene for one cycle, horde-floors-ai and horde-ai, and 36 CPU bouts (each fighter against two others, 90 s each), 234,359 frames at 60fps, each body measured per frame at three stages (the sim's joints, after the draw smoothing, as drawn) plus its cloth, the horde's flying goons and the pile, with the same logger on the build before and after. Unit-frames with a drawn edge more than 1 px under its floor: before 21,661 (11,045 over 3 px, 6,138 over 10, the worst 55 px: a head inside a flight), after 0 in every frame; by surface before: main floor 5,299 (433 over 3 px), on a flight 10,416, the lower floor beside a flight 3,254, falling down a stairwell 2,692. The sim's own joints (the hitboxes): before 18,739, after 0. Cloth: before 13,768 (worst 41 px), after 0. The pile: before 123,450 lying and 3,451 settling (mostly 1 to 4 px of rim), after 0. The worst clips before: `down/knockdown`, the ragdoll (`knockdown/airborne`, `ko/airborne`), `getup`, and on the stairs `cross`, `idle`, `walk` and the vault. `node scripts/floorlog/strip.mjs` draws frame strips with the surface under the body as a line (the task has launch, sweep, throw, ground bounce, horde ragdoll and stair strips, before and after). The fights play out as before: 36 CPU bouts gave 5,046 hits against 5,056, 68 rounds against 65 and 70 floor breaks against 75; a tumble down the steps now clatters on the treads instead of slipping through them, so it lands about half a second later and knocks more often.
- **Never stranded on two floors:** the stairs go both ways, so the two can always reach each other; the CPU does it on its own. The attacker still vaults down after a floor break, from his feet. As a last resort against a stand-off, when the two have been on different floors for 6s with neither on the stairs, falling or vaulting, whoever is upstairs vaults down the nearer stairwell (`regroup()` in `src/combat/sim.js`; not in horde, where floors are the point).
- **Horde, floor by floor:** each floor sends a wave of 5 to 8 goons through its doors (two at a time from the third): upstairs both sets of double doors and the elevator, downstairs the lit doorway, the far door down the corridor and the green door. Clear it for FLOOR CLEAR, and the hero vaults down the nearer stairwell to the next floor (walking down the stairs gets him there too); after the lowest floor the run loops back to the top, forever: the wave grows by one a floor up to 8, and the run ends only when the hero goes down (Arcade flow, Horde). Goons chase him up and down the stairs. The bodies lie on the floor where they fell, except that none rests over either opening: one that would lie across a hole slides off to the nearer lip as it settles (`arena.addBody`); a fighter KO'd on a flight lies along the steps. They don't collect forever: each lies there 10 seconds, then fades out over 1.2 and is gone, and past 8 bodies the oldest start fading early (`BODY_LINGER`, `BODY_FADE`, `MAX_BODIES` in `src/arena/index.js`). The arena's update runs inside the sim step and the snapshot keeps each body's age, so both machines fade the same bodies. The HUD tracks the floor, the wave and the goons downed.
- **Camera:** it frames the pair anywhere in the building (the main floor's walls bound it, `sim.viewBounds`), so a fight spread over a flight and both floors stays in frame; two floors apart it pulls back as before.
- **The test scenes** were written for the first building, so `timeline()` moves every phase's spots over by `SCENE_SHIFT` (500px: the old default pair at 560 and 650 lands just left of the middle), and the wall helpers take it back off so a spot by a wall is still by that wall; the stair and floor-break phases are written against the new building (`abs`). Run against the old build phase by phase (each test timeline, events and health at the end), 200 of the 218 old phases come out identical. Of the rest, 12 floor-broke by accident in the old building (its stairwell was 110px past the default spots: throw-test's throw, three string enders, PERFORMANCE REVIEW, WRITE-OFF, LATTE ART twice, CLOSE THE DEAL, ESCORT, the guard-test throw, the vault in air-recovery-test) and now show the move itself with no floor break; floor-break-test's three old phases break the floor the same way with a cleaner fall (no jam, no unwedge) and fewer chase hits, plus two new phases into the left stairwell; receptionist-test's toss forward out of the left corner now goes over that corner's rail (the left wall has a stairwell in front of it now, as the old right corner did).

`?scene=stair-test`: up the left flight from the lower floor, up the right one running, down at a head with down + toward, a crouch at the head staying upstairs, the higher man's 1,2,4 knocking the lower one down the steps, a guard holding on the steps, a sweep up the steps, a throw on the steps and the stomp on the body lying along them, a jump landing on the tread under him, and a floor break over the left rail followed by the climb back up.

**Measurements** (the stairs, against the build before them). CPU vs CPU at 0.7, 24 matches a pairing with the characters swapping sides every match, rounds won by the left spawn – the right: TEMP v TEMP 36–23, TEMP v COURIER 39–21, SECURITY v HELPDESK 28–33, MANAGER v INTERN 29–32, JANITOR v WINDOW WASHER 23–31; 155–140 in all (52.5%, inside the noise of 295 rounds), matches 64–56. Floor breaks by side: 36–42. The same test on the old building: rounds 144–153, but floor breaks 166–13, because only the left spawn had the hole behind its opponent. Rounds run about as long as before (17 to 21 seconds of fighting). The CPUs spend 4 to 7% of their time on a flight, and someone walks back up to the main floor about once every two matches. Frame cost at the phone tier (headless, the same camera moves) is no worse than the old building's.

URL `?scene=NAME` picks a scene: attract (the demo, which plain `play/index.html` opens on), play, dummy, ai-fight, ai-fight-2, ai-fight-3, scripted-combo, combo-test, string-test, ko, horde, horde-ai, horde-floors-ai, horde-ko-test (every way a goon goes down, then the hero; Arcade flow, Horde), plus the test timelines dash-test (the forward dash, the dash held into the run, a back dash out of a jab's range and the dash back in that punishes it, a back dash caught by a lunge cross, a held back that guards and doesn't dash, each dash attack hitting and blocked and punished, RISE AND SHINE as a 2-hit combo and botching with down still held, the rising jab and kick, the rise running out, COURIER's own →→3 and the shared →→1, mashing ← and mashing buttons with ←, and a double tap → that doesn't parry), parry-test (parries of a high, a mid, a low with down-forward and a jump-in, each punished; a tap too early, a held forward, a standing tap against a low, a throw and mashed forward all getting hit), air-recovery-test (air recovery too early, in the jab window, late, mashed, and against a juggle that keeps going; the second air jab; TANGLED; an attack off the vault), stomp-test (the stomp landing and pinning, a greedy stomp punished by a wake kick, whiffing on a roll, the wake kick after a stomp guarded and not, one ground hit per knockdown: a second stomp only grazes), ground-test (the low jab and the low kick on a body on the floor, each landing once then grazing; the stomp then a low kick grazing; the sweep falling short; the axe kick grazing), getup-test (lying until the clock stands him up, up, rolls away and toward with him over the body, from across the room, into the wall and with his back to the wall, the Wake Kick and Wake Sweep landing, then each blocked and punished on the rise), mixup-test, stamina-test, faint-test (fainted with no input for the full 1.5s; alternating at the best rate, a press every 100ms, out in 0.88s; a calmer beat out in 1s; holding a direction and mashing one direction, rooted and no gain; frantic alternation every 42ms, slower than the steady beat; shaking under two jabs that still knock him back; `node gauntlet/measure.mjs faint` prints each phase's faint length), refund-test (NEON TORNADO, two juggle jabs and the stomp from a full bar with a refund on every hit and no faint; the same string whiffed from out of range; a cross at 12 stamina that hits and leaves him on its refund, the same cross blocked and whiffed, both fainting), string-ender-test, wall-test, juggle-test, trade-test (a jab and a snap kick landing together, then a counter hit), clash-test (jab against jab, cross against cross, snap kick against snap kick, roundhouse against roundhouse and low kick against low kick all clashing, a jab against a cross thrown earlier clashing, a second jab one step late counter hit instead, a jab batted aside by COMET CROSS breaking through, a jab and a snap kick still trading, a tapped parry still parrying, a jab against a throw unchanged), throw-test, floor-break-test (either stairwell), stair-test (walking and fighting on the stairs, the climb back after a floor break), jump-in-test, anti-air-test, guard-test (holding back walks back; the guard snaps on for a high or mid that can reach, not for a low or a throw; then a blocked jab, a sweep blocked at down-back and a blocked ender, for the block effects by weight), botch-test (one mistimed press per botch family, a spin-out punished from behind, a botch fainting a tired fighter), accident-test (each happy accident at its window, then the same press a step early and a step late botching), and the character tests courier-test, security-test, intern-test, windowwasher-test, helpdesk-test, manager-test, accountant-test (the parry catching a high and a mid, losing to a low and a throw, a whiffed stance punished) receptionist-test (Please Hold from range, blocked, on a croucher and whiffing up close; Transfer in the open, out of the corner, unbreakable and with no room behind her; HANG UP), lawyer-test (Objection! catching a throw and ESCORT, losing to a jab and punished on a whiff; Cross-Examination into the throw, into the mid on a ducker and into the cross on hit; CLOSING ARGUMENT), ceo-test (Golden Parachute through a cross and a throw, its landing punished; Hostile Takeover twice into the wall and after an uppercut, punished on block; STOCK SPLIT) and janitor-test (the last also shows the double jump, a double-jump cross-up and the wall jump; each unique move landing as designed, the character as P1 against a TEMP), hrrep-test (Write-Up's WRITTEN UP regen next to the same jabs without it, Training sending him back, EXIT INTERVIEW), salesrep-test (Cold Call, both feints through a standing guard, the same press after the window botching, Close the Deal, ALWAYS BE CLOSING) and barista-test (a WIRED jab next to the same jab not wired, Pour Over hit and blocked, LATTE ART, WIRED LATTE ART), chef-test (Cleaver on a crouching guard, armoring a jab, blocked standing and punished; Flambé launching into jabs; EIGHTY-SIX into the wall), wellness-test (Tree Pose held into the crane kick, the root sweep under a standing guard, the crane kick on a crouching guard, 2 stepping out, a tap holding a beat and stepping out by itself, a jab counter-hitting the stance's startup; SUN SALUTATION) and electrician-test (Live Wire's shock with 2, 3 landing in the extra stun, a second Live Wire in the same combo that only stuns, Live Wire blocked; Ground Fault rooting a back-walker and the rooted body still guarding; SHORT CIRCUIT) (`__gauntlet.setScene(name, { only: k })` runs one phase of a timeline). Every scene is TEMP vs TEMP unless `?p1=ID&p2=ID` (or `setScene(name, { chars: [a, b] })`) says otherwise; the attract demo draws a random pair from the roster. Add `&mirror=1` to see any scene the way the online guest sees a match (Multiplayer, Mirrored view); the capture tool takes it in the page argument, e.g. `node gauntlet/capture.mjs "play/index.html?mirror=1" play out/`. Old links to the homepage with `?scene=` (or `?room=`, `?crew=`, `?p1=`...) are forwarded to `play/` (see Site).

## Characters

Eight office workers plus TEMP, the baseline, each in its own file in `src/characters/`. Every character keeps the shared core: the four limb buttons and their direction variants, the crouch set, the air attacks, throws (1+3, 2+4), the getup options and the wake-up strikes, and the shared strings (it may drop some). On top of that each has at least two unique moves and one unique string or ender, its own stats and body, and CPU hints.

| Character | File | Status | Plays like | Unique moves |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| TEMP | `temp.js` | built | the baseline: the original fighter, exactly | none (every shared move and string) |
| INTERN | `intern.js` | built | scrappy all-rounder, the ladder's stage 1 | Coffee Run (→→ 1), Paper Cut (↓1, ↓1, 2), REPLY ALL (1, 2, 2) |
| HELPDESK | `helpdesk.js` | built | defense and counters, better stamina regen | Ticket Closed (← 1, parry stance), Reboot (← 2, palm push, wall carry), CTRL ALT DEL (1, 3, 2) |
| COURIER | `courier.js` | built | speed rushdown, lower health | Express Slide (→→ 3), Drop-Off Knee (→→ 4), SAME DAY (1, 1, 3) |
| SECURITY | `security.js` | built | big grappler, more health, slow | Escort (→ 1+3), Shoulder Check (← 2), LOCKDOWN (1, 2, 1) |
| JANITOR | `janitor.js` | built | long reach, a little slow | Wet Floor (→ 3, long low sweep), Mop Spin (← 3, hits both sides), AISLE 3 (3, 3, 3) |
| THE MANAGER | `manager.js` | built | heavy power, slow uniques, more health, the boss | PERFORMANCE REVIEW (1, 2, 2: four-hit rush ender), Downsizing (← 3, overhead axe kick), TOP-DOWN (3, 2, 3) |
| ACCOUNTANT | `accountant.js` | built | technical mixups, plus frames | Fine Print (↓ 3, low, plus on block), Balance Sheet (↓3, 4, overhead), WRITE-OFF (↓3, 3, 2) |
| WINDOW WASHER | `windowWasher.js` | built | aerial: double jump, wall jump | Squeegee Dive (↓3 in the air), Updraft (← 3), STREAK FREE (1, 3, 4) |
| RECEPTIONIST | `receptionist.js` | built | spacing and reach: one very long, very open poke | Please Hold (→ 1, phone-cord lash), Transfer (← 1+3, throw over her head: sides swap), HANG UP (3, 1, 1) |
| LAWYER | `lawyer.js` | built | throws and the answer to throws | Objection! (← 1, throw counter), Cross-Examination (→ 1, plus-on-block tick), CLOSING ARGUMENT (2, 3, 2) |
| THE CEO | `ceo.js` | built | balanced powerhouse, small stamina bar, secret boss | Golden Parachute (← 1, invulnerable hop back), Hostile Takeover (↘ 2, launcher; in a juggle, the ender), STOCK SPLIT (2, 1, 2) |
| HR REP | `hrRep.js` | built | defensive, procedural: debuffs and spacing | Write-Up (← 1, WRITTEN UP: half regen 3s), Mandatory Training (← 2, long shove), EXIT INTERVIEW (2, 1, 2) |
| SALES REP | `salesRep.js` | built | pressure and feints | Cold Call (→→ 1, feints into ←3 or a throw), The Pitch (← 3), Close the Deal (→ 1+3), ALWAYS BE CLOSING (3, 1, 2) |
| BARISTA | `barista.js` | built | speed: a self buff and a flurry | Espresso Shot (← 1, WIRED: faster startups 2.5s), Pour Over (← 2, four hits), LATTE ART (1, 2, 1) |
| CHEF | `chef.js` | built | heavy and slow, armored overhead | Cleaver (← 2, armored overhead chop), Flambé (← 1, short launcher in a big flash), EIGHTY-SIX (1, 1, 4) |
| WELLNESS COACH | `wellness.js` | built | flexibility and a stance | Tree Pose (← 1 held, a stance: 4 Crane Kick, 3 Root Sweep, 2 out), SUN SALUTATION (1, 3, 4) |
| ELECTRICIAN | `electrician.js` | built | control: a shock and a root | Live Wire (→ 1, SHOCKED: extra stun), Ground Fault (↘ 3, ROOTED: no walking), SHORT CIRCUIT (2, 1, 2) |

A phase 2 file is `export default null` until it's built. `index.js` registers every file that isn't null, the select screen shows only registered characters, and the arcade ladder fights a TEMP under that stage's name and color in the meantime.

**COURIER** ("delivers in thirty seconds or less"). Pink with a cyan messenger cap; a slightly smaller rig (scale 0.96) with longer legs, thinner limbs and a 5 degree forward lean. Health 90, walk and run 1.2x, back-walk 0.95 of that, jump 1.06x, dash 1.2x, weight 0.88 (flies farther), stamina regen 1.15x, damage 0.97x. Keeps every shared string.
- **Express Slide**, →→ 3 (dash forward, then 3): a feet-first slide along the floor. Low (beats a standing guard), under highs (`tc`), knocks down. Travels 520 px/s (x dash) from 60 ms for 230 ms; 10 damage, 22 stamina, unsafe on block (10 frames of blockstun against a long recovery). It ducks a jab thrown into it and counter-hits.
- **Drop-Off Knee**, →→ 4: a running flying knee. Mid, launches into a juggle (kb 130 / 450), lows pass under it from the press until recovery (`lowInvuln`), travels 420 px/s for 190 ms; 13 damage, 24 stamina. Jabs juggle after it (a 4-hit combo in courier-test).
- **SAME DAY**, 1, 1, 3: jab, jab, then the slide as an ender (12 damage, low knockdown, the SAME DAY callout).
- CPU: style rushdown; slides on a whiff, on a guard and from range, knees a standing opponent from a dash.

**SECURITY** ("will escort you out. through the railing"). Green with mirrored shades and an earpiece on a coiled wire; a bigger, stocky rig (scale 1.12, thick torso, short arms and legs, so his reach is about TEMP's and there's more of him to hit), a 4 degree lean and a wider stance. Health 106, walk 0.84x, back-walk 0.9 of that, run 0.88x, jump 0.92x, weight 1.2 (barely moves when hit), stamina 1.05x, regen 0.95x, damage 0.92x. Drops SKY SPIKE.
- **Escort**, → 1+3 (or → 2+4): a command grab. Range 92 px at the press (a throw's is 100), can't be broken, can't be blocked, whiffs on a croucher like any throw, slower to come out, 30 stamina. Holds 16 frames, then a two-handed shove: 10 damage, kb 780, wall splat near a wall, and over the stairwell railing when he's thrown toward it (a floor break).
- **Shoulder Check**, ← 2: an armored charge behind the shoulder, 40 px of travel. Absorbs one hit from the press until recovery (`armor`, taking half its damage, an ARMOR callout), knocks down, bounces off a nearby wall into a juggle (`wallBounce`); 12 damage, 26 stamina.
- **LOCKDOWN**, 1, 2, 1: jab, cross, then a two-fisted hammer from overhead (an overhead: a mid that hits crouchers), 16 damage, knockdown.
- CPU: style grappler; checks through a strike he sees coming, escorts a guard or a standing opponent up close.

**INTERN** ("unpaid, and swinging like it"). Amber with a pink lanyard and ID badge (the badge hangs straight down whatever he does); a touch smaller all over (scale 0.95) with a slightly big head, so his reach is a little short of TEMP's. Health 96, walk 1.04x, run and dash 1.05x, jump 1.02x, weight 0.95, stamina regen 1.15x, damage 0.97x. Drops LOW RUSH (Paper Cut takes d+1). CPU: the plain scrapper (no style), so at stage 1's difficulty 0.2 he forgets to block most of the time.
- **Coffee Run**, →→ 1: a dash-in elbow, the body sliding in behind it (`travel` 420 px/s x dash from 40 ms for 160 ms) with the rear hand up at the chin. Mid, 11 damage, 30 frames of hitstun (a jab or cross links after it), 19 stamina. Only 8 frames of blockstun against a long recovery, so blocked it eats a punish (intern-test shows the cross).
- **Paper Cut**, ↓1, ↓1, then 2: quick low flicks from the crouch (lead hand, then rear hand; 4 damage, 12 stamina each, faster than the low jab), then a rising backfist out of the crouch: mid, 9 damage, knockdown, the PAPER CUT callout. ↓1, ↓1, ↓3 finishes with the low kick instead. The lows go under a standing guard.
- **REPLY ALL**, 1, 2, 2: jab, cross, then a wide haymaker hook he throws everything into and has to stumble out of. Mid ender, 15 damage, knockdown.
- CPU: elbows in after a whiff and from just outside his punches; opens the paper cuts on a standing guard.

**WINDOW WASHER** ("does floor 37 from outside"). Electric blue with a hi-vis climbing harness (shoulder straps, belt, a safety line and carabiner off the back, a squeegee holstered on the hip); TEMP's arms on longer legs (32.5/31.5) and a slimmer frame. Health 94, walk 1.05x, back-walk 0.95 of that, run 1.06x, jump 1.15x, dash 1.05x, weight 0.88 (flies farther), regen 1.05x, damage 0.97x. Keeps every shared string.
- **Double jump** (`mechanics.airJumps: 1`): a fresh jump press in the air jumps again (0.88 of a jump, in any held direction, 8 stamina like a jump) and refreshes the air attack. **Wall jump** (`mechanics.wallJump`): a jump press within 34 px of a wall kicks off it, away from it and over anyone standing there; at most twice a jump. Both play `wwAirJump`: the legs snap down as if off the glass, a tuck with the arms thrown up, the fall and the jump's own landing. The walls are each floor's own band, so nothing leaves the arena or crosses the stairwell; the railing isn't a wall (it's a waist-high rail over a hole, there's nothing to kick off), and a floor break's follow-down vault waits for him to land like it does for any jump. Crossing over him on the second jump or off a wall is a real side change.
- **Squeegee Dive**, ↓3 in the air (off the first jump or the second): the lead leg spears down and forward with the body laid back over it. A dive (the jump is cut short), an overhead (beats a crouch, a standing guard holds), 12 damage, 40 frames of hitstun so a ground string follows on landing (2, 3 off the double jump in windowwasher-test), 16 stamina.
- **Updraft**, ← 3: a rising scissor kick, the body laid back in the air and the lead foot swinging up through the space in front of him. Live the whole arc, so it meets jump-ins; lows pass under it (`lowInvuln`); mid, 12 damage, launches into a juggle (kb 110 / 470); 24 stamina, a long landing to punish on block.
- **STREAK FREE**, 1, 3, 4: jab, snap kick, then a hop into a two-footed dropkick. Mid ender, 15 damage, knockdown, wall splat near a wall.
- CPU: style aerial. Jumps in watching for the anti-air: if you start one (or duck under it waiting) the second jump carries him over you and down behind; otherwise the dive comes down on you. Jumps into the wall and kicks off it over you when he's cornered, crosses up from close range, and swings Updraft at a jump-in or a crouching strike.

**HELPDESK** ("have you tried blocking?"). Cyan with a call-center headset (a band over the crown, an ear cup, and a mic boom round to the mouth with a lit tip); TEMP's proportions, a slightly wider torso and a degree of backward lean. Health 100, walk 0.97x, back-walk 1.06 of that, run 0.95x, weight 1.02, stamina 1.05x, regen 1.15x, damage 0.92x. Keeps every shared string.
- **Ticket Closed**, ← 1: a counter stance (the `parry` hook). The weight settles back, the lead hand comes out open at head height and the rear hand drops to cover the body. Its startup and its 190 ms hold are the catch window: a high or mid strike landing in it is caught (TICKET CLOSED), the striker reels for 46 frames, and the stance turns into the reversal, a trap-and-elbow behind a short glide (10 damage, knockdown, `travel` 520 px/s from 30 ms so a strike caught at the tip of its reach is still answered; the glide stops at the body). Lows hit it (a counter hit in its startup), throws take it, air attacks aren't caught, and a whiff drops the hands into a 390 ms recovery the CPU punishes. 18 stamina; the reversal costs nothing more.
- **Reboot**, ← 2: both palms pull back to the ribs, a long step, and both arms shoot out at chest height. Mid, 11 damage, kb 700, knockdown, wall splat when the body reaches a wall (`splat`, the same trick shot as Comet Cross) and he follows it across the floor; a short glide in (`travel` 240 px/s for 110 ms), reach about 150 px root to root. 24 stamina, 12 frames of blockstun.
- **CTRL ALT DEL**, 1, 3, 2: jab, snap kick, then a rising double palm under the chin. Mid ender, 13 damage, launches into a juggle (kb 140 / 470); a jab juggles after it in helpdesk-test.
- CPU: style turtle. Reads a high or mid he sees starting inside its reach and stances it (40% of the time at full difficulty, less below), reads strings (when the striker's last hit landed and the press that would continue his string is a high or mid, the stance is pressed, buffered out of blockstun), Reboots a whiff and anyone near the wall.

**JANITOR** ("works the lower floor. mops with jump-ins"). Violet with a yellow bucket hat (a low crown and a brim drooping both sides); a lanky rig: scale 1.02, long arms (26/25) and legs (33.5/32.5), a narrow torso and thin limbs (10 wide, TEMP's are 12), a 3 degree lean. The reach is the long limbs; keeping them thin keeps the hitbox and hurtbox small, so he doesn't win the poke game outright the way SECURITY's first build did. Health 100, walk 0.9x, back-walk 0.95 of that, run 0.92x, jump 1.04x, dash 0.95x, weight 0.98, damage 0.95x. Keeps every shared string.
- **Wet Floor**, → 3: he drops low over the rear leg and slides the lead leg out flat at shin height, a hand reaching for the floor, the body gliding in behind it (`travel` 320 px/s from 120 ms for 150 ms). Low (a standing guard doesn't hold it), 10 damage, knockdown. Reaches about 170 px root to root on a standing body (a sweep reaches about 115), comes out in about 125 ms up close and 150 to 200 ms at the tip of its range. 22 stamina; 8 frames of blockstun against a long recovery, so blocked it eats a punish (janitor-test shows the cross).
- **Mop Spin**, ← 3: a full turn on the rear foot with the lead leg out flat at hip height and the arms flung wide. The leg passes behind him first (about 100 ms after the press) and comes round in front (about 230 ms), one hit either side (the `bothSides` hook below). Mid, 12 damage, knockdown, 24 stamina. It catches a run-jump cross-up over his crouch and sends the jumper away on that side; it hits a jumper in front too.
- **AISLE 3**, 3, 3, 3: two snap kicks, then the lead knee chambers high and a long side kick drives out at chest height. Mid ender, 15 damage, kb 700, wall splat near a wall.
- CPU: style aerial (the ladder's stage 5 asks for it too). Spins when someone is coming down on him or over him, and at a strike or grab starting up close; Wet Floors a whiff, a guard, and a standing opponent at long range.

**Measurements** (phase 2, HELPDESK and JANITOR). CPU vs CPU at difficulty 0.7, 80 matches a pairing (two blocks of 40 under different seeds, sides swapped every other match), rounds won: HELPDESK 106, TEMP 93 (53%); HELPDESK 113, COURIER 85 (57%); HELPDESK 84, SECURITY 110 (43%); JANITOR 109, TEMP 89 (55%); JANITOR 114, COURIER 89 (56%); JANITOR 106, SECURITY 106 (50%); HELPDESK 109, JANITOR 81 (57%). Twenty-match blocks swing about 10 points either way, which is why these are 80. 1,393 of 1,400 rounds ended by KO (the other 7 on time), on average 18 to 25 seconds in. In those 560 matches HELPDESK pressed Ticket Closed 692 times and caught 478 strikes (almost all crosses; every reversal but a few long lunges landed), threw 1,032 Reboots (405 hit) and 144 CTRL ALT DELs; JANITOR threw 1,241 Wet Floors (575 hit), 367 Mop Spins (52 hit; it's mostly an anti-air and cross-up answer) and 7 AISLE 3s (the CPU rarely opens on 3). Mashing any one button or command on a standing dummy at three speeds never makes more than 5 hits in a combo with either; their own commands top out at 4 (← 1, ← 2, → 3, ← 3; AISLE 3's 3, 3, 3 makes 5). The shared d+2 launcher reaches 6 for both on that harness, the same as TEMP's 6 to 7. Contact: every Ticket Closed reversal hit in CPU play seated at 1 to 1.5 px; Wet Floor seats at 0 to 2 px 80% of the time, its outliers being knockdowns measured after the fall starts, as with the shared sweep (67%). Save/restore: eight recorded CPU bouts at difficulty 0.9 (HELPDESK vs JANITOR twice, TEMP, COURIER and SECURITY; JANITOR vs TEMP, SECURITY and COURIER; 9,000 steps each, with inputs, the rng state after each CPU call and `aaJump`, `grabId`, `rollId` recorded) replayed identically to the recording, then took 3,296 rollbacks of 1 to 16 steps (save, run, restore, run, restore, run) whose save points were half inside a unique move or a parry: all 42,207 restored steps and every plain step matched the reference's deep hash of `saveState()`. The helpdesk-test and janitor-test phases, replayed with a save point on every step, matched on all 23,116 restored steps. Taking a frame off the parried striker's stun, dropping the reversal's glide or flipping `home` on restore made it differ at once.

**THE MANAGER** ("your performance review is now"). Red with a gold necktie (knot at the collar, the blade down the shirt front); a bigger, square rig (scale 1.06, a broad torso, arms and legs a touch short of TEMP's so the size doesn't turn into pure reach), a 3 degree lean and a wider stance. Health 104, walk 0.88x, back-walk 0.88 of that, run 0.9x, jump 0.9x, dash 0.92x, weight 1.15, regen 0.95x, damage 1.04x. His uniques are slow and hit hard; the shared moves keep the shared timing. Drops TRIPLE JAB and ONE-TWO KICK, so his 1, 2 goes into the review.
- **PERFORMANCE REVIEW**, 1, 2, 2: jab, cross, then the ender, the first character move on the multi-hit hook (`hits: 4`): three rear hooks (body, head, body), the fist back at the chin between them, then a wind-up and a stepping overhand. The hooks are light (`rush`: 4 damage, 30 frames of hitstun, no knockdown, no callout) and the overhand is the ender (10 damage, kb 520 / 300, knockdown, the callout). A short glide in (`travel` 300 px/s for 150 ms) so the first hook reaches. Every hit counts in the combo and decays like any other: the whole string is a 6-hit combo for about 30 damage on TEMP (combo decay and the soft cap do the rest), and blocked standing all four hooks are blocked. 26 stamina (cheaper than an ender's 30: four hits from a heavy man, but he has to have the bar for the whole string).
- **Downsizing**, ← 3: a lead-leg axe kick. He rocks back, the leg rises high in front of his face and hangs there a beat (readable: contact about 20 frames after the press), then the heel chops down with the arms flung back, and he lands on it 30 px ahead (a small `travel` through the chop). An overhead (`height: 'overhead'`): it hits a crouching guard, a standing guard holds it. 16 damage, knockdown, 26 stamina, 12 frames of blockstun against a long recovery, so blocked it's a free punish.
- **TOP-DOWN**, 3, 2, 3: snap kick, cross, then the axe kick as an overhead ender (15 damage, knockdown).
- CPU: plain scrapper by default; the ladder's boss plays him as the showcase. Axe-kicks a crouching guard and a body getting up, now and then a standing one; opens with the jab when he has the stamina for the whole 1, 2, 2. How often the review comes out is capped by the CPU's stamina rule (it only starts an ender when the rest of the string fits in the bar, and the showcase spends a lot): about one review every two boss matches, every jab-cross chain he does land with the bar for it ends in one.

**ACCOUNTANT** ("read the fine print. then the overhead"). Pale yellow with round reading glasses (cyan lenses, a dark rim, the arm back to the ear); TEMP's proportions, a slightly slimmer torso and thinner limbs (11.2 wide), a 2 degree lean. Everything average: health 100, weight 0.98, regen 1.05x, damage 1.04x. What he has is frame advantage. Drops GROUND RUSH (its d+3 opener is Fine Print here); LOW RUSH still ends in the shared low kick.
- **Fine Print**, ↓ 3: a toe poke along the floor from the crouch, the foot snapping straight back. Low, 5 damage, 14 stamina, contact about 3 frames after the press (a frame faster than the jab), 26 frames of hitstun. 15 frames of blockstun against a short recovery: **+2.5 frames on block** (accountant-test measures it: he's free 5 steps before the guard is). It opens both of his strings.
- **Balance Sheet**, ↓3, 4: up out of the crouch onto the toes, both fists over the head like a rubber stamp, down on top of him (a short `travel` in). An overhead: 12 damage, 32 frames of hitstun. **The mixup**: after a blocked Fine Print, Balance Sheet lands 3 steps after the blockstun ends and the second low (↓3, 3, a shuffle-step low kick, 6 damage) 6 steps after, so the guard has to pick a height as it comes out of blockstun: stay down and the overhead hits, stand and the low hits. A jab pressed in that gap runs into it. On hit both combo.
- **WRITE-OFF**, ↓3, 3, 2: the two lows, then he drives up off the floor behind a rear palm to the chin. Mid ender, 13 damage, knockdown (25 damage for the string on TEMP).
- CPU: plain scrapper by default (the ladder plays him as a turtle when he fills a stage). Pokes a whiff with Fine Print, pokes a standing guard and a crouching one (to get the plus frames and the guess), and carries the string on into the low or the overhead on hit or block (the CPU names Fine Print directly; the sim starts his string from it, see Hooks).

**Measurements** (phase 2, THE MANAGER and ACCOUNTANT). CPU vs CPU at difficulty 0.7, 20 matches a pairing with sides swapped every other match, rounds won: MANAGER 38, TEMP 10 (2 drawn); MANAGER 23, COURIER 26; MANAGER 33, SECURITY 17; MANAGER 26, ACCOUNTANT 26; ACCOUNTANT 28, TEMP 21; ACCOUNTANT 30, COURIER 20; ACCOUNTANT 23, SECURITY 25. ACCOUNTANT sits at 48 to 60% against everyone. THE MANAGER is 60% over all four, but the single pairings swing hard between near-identical builds: across the tuning runs his TEMP matchup ran 63 to 79% and his SECURITY one 29 to 66% (a 1% change in scale moved SECURITY from 29 to 66), so TEMP is his good matchup and the others are about even. 346 of 349 rounds ended by KO, on average 19 to 26 seconds in (the longest 39). In those 140 matches: 42 PERFORMANCE REVIEWs (154 hits landed, about 3.7 a review), 50 Downsizings, 324 Fine Prints, 125 second lows, 73 Balance Sheets and 95 WRITE-OFFs. As the boss (showcase, difficulty 1.0, against a 0.85 TEMP, 10 matches) he took 20 of 22 rounds with 7 to 12 uniques. Mashing any one button or command on a standing dummy at three speeds never makes more than 5 hits in a combo for either (d+2's launch and juggle reach 5, everything else 4 or fewer; PERFORMANCE REVIEW's six hits need three different presses). A save/restore replay of 8 recorded CPU bouts at difficulty 0.9 on the final build (MANAGER vs ACCOUNTANT three times, MANAGER vs TEMP and SECURITY, ACCOUNTANT vs COURIER, TEMP and SECURITY; 9,000 steps each, seven of them crossing a match end; inputs, the rng state after each CPU call and `aaJump`, `grabId`, `rollId` recorded, the recorders registered as script controllers so `resetMatch` doesn't swap in a fresh CPU): 1,612 rollbacks of 1 to 16 steps, save, run, restore, run, restore, run, with 447 save points inside a unique move and 94 between the review's hooks. All 25,918 restored steps and all 59,041 plain replay steps matched the reference's deep hash of `saveState()` (16 more bouts on earlier builds of the pair matched too). Zeroing `hitKey` or dropping the travel `glide` on restore made it differ within a few steps.

**Measurements** (phase 2, INTERN and WINDOW WASHER). CPU vs CPU at difficulty 0.7, 20 matches a pairing with sides swapped every other match, rounds won (a second block of 20 under other seeds in brackets): INTERN 25, TEMP 25 (24-25); INTERN 26, COURIER 25 (18-30); INTERN 25, SECURITY 21 (16-34); WINDOW WASHER 21, TEMP 30 (27-21); WINDOW WASHER 26, COURIER 22 (27-25); WINDOW WASHER 20, SECURITY 27 (24-28); INTERN 28, WINDOW WASHER 27 (24-24). Over the 40 matches that's INTERN 41 to 49% and WINDOW WASHER 44 to 53% against every opponent. 348 of 348 rounds in the first block ended by KO, on average 18 to 23 seconds in (the longest 35). In that block's 140 matches: 371 Coffee Runs, 156 paper cuts and 25 finishers, 58 REPLY ALLs, 165 Squeegee Dives, 48 STREAK FREEs, 180 double jumps and 50 wall jumps (Updraft comes out only a handful of times: the CPU's anti-air is the shared uppercut, and Updraft fires at jump-ins and crouching strikes when he's deciding). Mashing any one button or command on a standing dummy at three speeds never makes more than 5 hits (Updraft's launch and its juggle reach 5; INTERN never passes 4). A save/restore replay of five recorded CPU bouts at difficulty 0.9 (WINDOW WASHER vs INTERN, COURIER and TEMP, INTERN vs SECURITY and WINDOW WASHER, 6,000 steps each, inputs, the rng state after each CPU call and the CPU's unit fields recorded), with 973 rollbacks of 1 to 16 steps whose save points were half inside a unique move, a double jump or a wall jump: all 8,424 restored steps and every plain replay step matched the reference's deep hash of `saveState()`. Zeroing `airJumpsUsed` on restore made it differ at once.

**Measurements** (after phase 1). TEMP vs TEMP is bit-for-bit the old game: the framework change alone left the per-step state hash of combo-test, string-test, scripted-combo, string-ender-test, jump-in-test, anti-air-test, wall-test, juggle-test, floor-break-test, mixup-test, stamina-test, trade-test, throw-test, ai-fight, ai-fight-2, ko and horde-ai identical to the build before characters, over their whole runs (so max combos and callouts too). The back-walk change that followed (Controls) leaves every scripted scene's combos, callouts and damage the same; CPU-vs-CPU fights play out differently, as they should. CPU vs CPU at difficulty 0.7, 20 matches a pairing with sides swapped every other match, rounds won: TEMP 27, COURIER 25; COURIER 24, SECURITY 27; SECURITY 25, TEMP 23 (at difficulty 1.0, 12 matches each: 12-17, 14-17, 16-13). 151 of 155 rounds ended by KO, on average 18 to 24 seconds in (the longest 36). The unique moves get used constantly: in those 60 matches, 186 slides, 80 knees, 21 SAME DAYs, 195 escorts, 151 shoulder checks and 12 LOCKDOWNs. Mashing any one button or command on a standing dummy, for all three characters at three mash speeds, never makes more than 5 hits in a combo (COURIER's d+2 launcher and →→ 4 reach 5, everything else 4 or fewer).

**Measurements (side swaps).** The owner saw fighters "teleport and switch spots without an animation" in the heat of battle. A logger drove 40 CPU-vs-CPU fights at 0.7 (each of the nine characters against the next and the one four along, three seeds, 90 s each), three attract showcase fights, horde-ai and horde-floors-ai twice each and floor-break-test, about 270,000 fight frames, with a fresh page per fight. Per 60fps frame it recorded the drawn head and hip of every body (after all the draw smoothing), every write to a unit's `x` with its stack (a trapping setter), and side swaps by `bodyX()` (a swap counts once the new side is 10px clear and holds 8 frames). Before → after, side swaps: 366 → 208, and swaps with a drawn jump of the body over 25px in the frame 228 → 27. By cause (with a drawn jump in brackets): a getup ending (the fighter stood up at his old root, a body-length from his hip, past the man over his legs) 150 (150) → 0; a throw (the grab put the victim 70px in front, across the thrower if he had turned to a lying hip) 26 (24) → 8 (2, both a thrown body landing past a man standing over it); `keepOff` easing a man off a lying body 15 (14) → 2 (0); walking over a lying body 56 (5) → 33 (4); separation 4 (4) → 50 (0): a man standing within reach of a rising body's hip on the wrong side of it is now shoved back to his own side, 6px a step, as the getup turns solid (visible, never a snap). The legit ones stay: floor breaks and vaults 58 (3) → 68 (6), launches and juggles 18 (4) → 23 (1), jump cross-ups 13 (6) → 8 (2), rolls 4 (3) → 2 (0), horde respawns at a door 14 → 13 (a new goon, not a move). Drawn-body jumps (hip over 25px in a frame): 3,373 → 1,486, and of what's left 812 are contact frames (a strike seats on the body and the reaction starts, under the impact flash, by design), 269 launched, thrown or ragdoll bodies (raw by design), 93 horde respawns, 38 the vault and the rest the knockdown and roll clips' own fast motion; the getup's 491, the cut-short travelling clips' 400-odd (a cross cancelled into a jab snapped back by its step) and the separation snaps' 386 are gone. Every scripted scene (combo-test, string-test, scripted-combo, string-ender-test, jump-in-test, anti-air-test, wall-test, juggle-test, floor-break-test, guard-test, botch-test, accident-test, throw-test, trade-test, mixup-test and the eight character tests, 127 phases) gives the same max combo and the same callouts as before, except one extra FAINT in floor-break-test's first phase: after the floor break the body no longer sticks to the lower floor's wall, gets up sooner, and the scene's after-script jab chase empties P1's bar. CPU vs CPU at 0.7, 40 matches a pairing, sides swapped, with only the side-swap changes (old stamina): TEMP v COURIER 58-41 / 62-33 (a second block under other seeds 53-47 / 54-42), SECURITY v HELPDESK 48-55 / 51-48, MANAGER v INTERN 61-37 / 62-42, JANITOR v WINDOW WASHER 60-38 / 58-32, ACCOUNTANT v TEMP 62-33 / 64-37, COURIER v SECURITY 33-60 / 24-71 (second block 25-76 before). With the faster regen and the knockdown refill on top (the owner's call, same day): 68-31, 57-44, 69-29, 64-38, 62-45, 23-72, and rounds run about 15% shorter; the refill favors the heavier hitters, COURIER most of all. A random-button masher against the CPU still loses every round (0-12 at 0.3 and at 0.7, about 15 botches a match, best combo 3, the same as before). A save/restore replay of six recorded CPU bouts at 0.9 (SECURITY v COURIER, JANITOR v WINDOW WASHER, MANAGER v ACCOUNTANT, HELPDESK v INTERN, TEMP v SECURITY, COURIER v JANITOR; 9,000 steps each, every one crossing a match end; inputs, the rng state after each CPU call and `aaJump`, `grabId`, `rollId` recorded): 2,754 rollbacks of 1 to 16 steps (save, run, restore, run, restore, run), 345 of them saved during a getup, a grab, a roll or a character move; all 47,376 restored steps and 54,000 plain steps matched the reference's hash of the whole `saveState()`, and adding 40 to a restored getup's step count made it differ at once. `window.__game.stepQuiet(ms)` steps a frame without drawing (the draw smoothing still runs) and `__game.drawn(unit)` gives the drawn head and hip, for measurements like these.

**HR REP** ("this is going in your file"). Light pink with cyan pants, a cyan lanyard and ID card on the chest and a clipboard held flat along the lead forearm (it stops short of the fist, so a swat still touches with the hand); TEMP's proportions, a slightly narrower torso and a degree of backward lean (upright, procedural). Health 104, walk 0.95x, back-walk 1.05 of that, run 0.95x, jump 0.98x, weight 1.04, stamina 1.05x, regen 1.1x, damage 0.97x. Keeps every shared string.
- **Write-Up**, ← 1: the clipboard goes up beside the head, a short step, and it swats down across his face. A high (a croucher ducks it), 7 damage, 26 frames of hitstun, 19 stamina. On a clean hit he's **WRITTEN UP**: half stamina regen for 3 seconds (the `status` hook, a pink tag with a draining bar in the overhead track). hrrep-test measures it: from the same jabs he refills about 26 a second instead of 52.
- **Mandatory Training**, ← 2: both hands come up to the chest, he rises a little (the presentation), then a long step and both arms drive out at chest height behind a glide (`travel` 300 px/s from 150 ms for 130 ms). Slow (contact about 27 steps after the press, a jab's is 13) and long: it lands at 160 px root to root and just misses at 170. Mid, 8 damage, 34 frames of hitstun, and the reaction is `hr_shoved`, a two-step backpedal that carries him 64 px back on his feet (`react`), so HR REP gets his spacing back without a knockdown. 22 stamina, 14 frames of blockstun against a long recovery.
- **EXIT INTERVIEW**, 2, 1, 2: cross, jab, then the rear hand goes up by the ear and a heavy palm comes through his chest. Mid ender, 13 damage, 40 frames of hitstun, shoved back (`hr_shoved`) and WRITTEN UP. It never knocks down, on purpose: a knockdown refills the bar, which would throw the regen cut away.
- CPU: style turtle. Writes up anyone not already on notice from just outside a jab and every whiff in reach; Training on a whiff, on someone walking in, and on a guard, from 100 to 155 px.

**SALES REP** ("have you got a minute? great"). Yellow with red pants, a skinny red necktie and a phone in the rear hand (held across the palm, screen lit, never past the fist); TEMP's size, a slimmer torso and thinner limbs (11.4), a 4 degree forward lean. Health 100, walk 1.08x, back-walk 0.92 of that, run 1.1x, jump 1.02x, dash 1.1x, weight 0.98, regen 1.15x, damage 1.05x. Keeps every shared string.
- **Cold Call**, →→ 1: the lead hand drops to the hip, a push off the rear foot, and the jab goes out long with the body sliding in behind it (`travel` 320 px/s from 130 ms for 120 ms, starting after the feint window, so a feint leaves him where he stood). Mid, 9 damage, 28 frames of hitstun, 18 stamina, about 19 steps to contact, reaches about 130 px with the glide.
- **The feint** (the `feint` hook): in Cold Call's first 90 ms (11 steps), ← 3 turns it into **The Pitch** and a throw (1+3, 2+4, or → 1+3) into **Close the Deal**, at once. A designed cancel: no botch. The feint versions slide in behind the lunge that already started (`sr_pitchFeint`: low, 7 damage, `travel` 400 px/s for 130 ms; `sr_closeFeint`: the grab at 150 px, pulled in to the hold). So against a standing guard Cold Call becomes a low, against any guard a grab, and a croucher eats the mid. The same ← 3 after the window botches (salesrep-test shows both).
- **The Pitch**, ← 3: a low shin kick from a dip over the rear leg. Low, 7 damage, 24 frames of hitstun, 14 stamina.
- **Close the Deal**, → 1+3 (or → 2+4): a handshake grab, about 12 steps to the grab (a throw's is about 28): the lead hand takes his, yanks him in, and the rear shoulder drives him away. Range 92, held 12 frames, breakable, 11 damage, knockdown, 24 stamina.
- **ALWAYS BE CLOSING**, 3, 1, 2: snap kick, jab, then a rising phone-hand uppercut. Mid ender, 13 damage, launches (kb 130 / 470); a jab juggles after it.
- CPU: style rushdown. Runs in with Cold Call on a guard or a croucher and turns it into the low or the grab (a `plan`: the press, five steps, the follow-up named), Cold Calls a whiff and a standing opponent, grabs a guard up close.

**BARISTA** ("order up. the order is pain"). Amber with violet pants, a dark apron down the front with a cream edge and neck strap, and a paper cup in the apron pocket that comes up to the mouth for the shot; TEMP's size, a slim torso, a 3 degree lean. Health 95, walk and run 1.15x, back-walk 0.95 of that, jump 1.04x, dash 1.15x, weight 0.92, regen 1.2x, damage 1.02x. Keeps every shared string.
- **Espresso Shot**, ← 1: the cup comes up, the head goes back for the shot, the cup comes down hard and a shiver runs through him. Never strikes (`noHit`); 20 stamina and about half a second with his hands busy (a counter-hit window). At the active key he's **WIRED** for 2.5 seconds (the `selfStatus` hook, a lime tag): every strike's startup plays 1.35x as fast, so a jab that takes 8 steps to land takes 6 (barista-test), and a string's every press comes out sooner.
- **Pour Over**, ← 2: three quick lead-hand flicks (head, head, body), the fist back at the chin between them, then a coil and a stepping lead palm (`hits: 4`, the multi-hit hook). The flicks are `rush` hits (3 damage, 26 frames of hitstun, no knockdown) and the palm is the move (8 damage, knockdown, kb 440 / 240), behind a short glide in. About 15 damage for the four on TEMP; blocked standing all four are blocked. 24 stamina.
- **LATTE ART**, 1, 2, 1: jab, cross, then a whirl on the lead foot into a backfist at head height. High ender, 13 damage, knockdown, kb 560 / 240.
- CPU: style rushdown. Drinks the shot when there's room (out of reach and not wired yet, or while you're on the floor), Pour Overs a whiff and a standing opponent up close, and runs the shared strings and LATTE ART wired.

**Measurements** (phase 3, HR REP, SALES REP and BARISTA). CPU vs CPU at difficulty 0.7, 24 matches a pairing with sides swapped every other match, on the final build, rounds won (a second block of 24 under other seeds in brackets where it ran): HR REP 28, TEMP 31 (27-34); HR REP 38, COURIER 22 (38-26); HR REP 29, SECURITY 29 (32-27); SALES REP 28, TEMP 31 (29-34); SALES REP 33, COURIER 27 (36-21); SALES REP 24, SECURITY 35 (26-29); BARISTA 32, TEMP 31 (34-22); BARISTA 34, COURIER 26 (31-29); BARISTA 21, SECURITY 42 (an earlier block on the same BARISTA, 26-33); HR REP 25, SALES REP 33; HR REP 29, BARISTA 30; SALES REP 35, BARISTA 26. Over the blocks that's 41 to 61% for every pairing except BARISTA v SECURITY at 39% (47-75 over 48 matches); the blocks swing 10 points or more between seeds (a 2% change in BARISTA's damage moved COURIER from 55 to 77% and HR REP from 49 to 37% in opposite directions), so the stats stop there. Every round ended by KO, on average 15 to 20 seconds in. Scale was the biggest lever, as before: SALES REP and BARISTA started at scale 0.98 and 0.95 and won 19 and 26% against TEMP; at 1.0 (with thicker limbs) they sit near even. In the first block's 288 matches: 113 Write-Ups (41 hit), 80 EXIT INTERVIEWs (77 landed), 46 Mandatory Trainings (18 hit); 190 Cold Calls (59 hit), 35 feints into The Pitch and 21 into Close the Deal, 50 Close the Deals, 30 Pitches, 6 ALWAYS BE CLOSINGs (the CPU rarely opens on 3); 153 Espresso Shots, 147 Pour Overs (265 of their hits landed) and 54 LATTE ARTs. A random-button masher playing each one against a TEMP CPU at 0.7 loses every round (0-6 over three matches each) with 15 to 20 botches a match and a best combo of 2. Save/restore: six recorded CPU bouts at 0.9 (HR REP v SALES REP, SALES REP v BARISTA, BARISTA v HR REP, HR REP v TEMP, SALES REP v SECURITY, BARISTA v COURIER; 6,000 steps each, inputs, the rng state after each CPU call and `grabId`, `rollId`, `aaJump` recorded and replayed on a counter that survives a match reset) replayed identically, then took 1,257 rollbacks of 1 to 16 steps (save, run, restore, run, restore, run), with save points weighted toward the uniques: 125 inside a unique move, 124 with WRITTEN UP or WIRED running, and through the feints. All 32,121 restored steps and every plain step matched the reference's deep hash of `saveState()`; nudging a restored `regenEnd` or `speedMul` made it differ at once.

**RECEPTIONIST** ("please hold. your beating is important to us"). Violet like JANITOR, so her legs are the green pants (`pants: '#5dff8a'`; in a mirror match P2 is pink with yellow pants) and she reads apart at a glance; a slim office headset (a thin band, a small ear pad, a short boom) and the desk phone's handset in her lead hand on a coiled cord that runs down to the base at her hip. TEMP's arms and legs on a slimmer frame (scale 0.98, limbs 11 wide, torso 14, a degree of lean): her reach is the cord, not the limbs. Health 102, walk 1.04x, back-walk 1.08 of that, weight 0.96, regen 1.1x, damage 1.06x (the open cord costs her, the stats pay it back). Keeps every shared string.
- **Please Hold**, → 1: the lead hand winds the handset back by her ear and whips it out on the cord, which hangs at full stretch a beat and gets reeled back in. The cord is the pose's `ext` channel (Hooks), so the hitbox is only its far end, two thin circles out along the forearm: it reaches about 190 px root to tip (a jab reaches about 97, JANITOR's Wet Floor about 170) and touches nothing inside about 100 px, so up close it goes right past him and the recovery is a free punish (receptionist-test shows the cross). Mid, 10 damage, 30 frames of hitstun, 9 of blockstun, 20 stamina; contact about 180 ms after the press, then about 270 ms of reeling in.
- **Transfer**, ← 1+3 (or ← 2+4): a grab that can't be broken (range 96 px at the press, held 14 frames, 26 stamina). She sinks, pulls him in and heaves him up over her head, arching back with her arms following him round, and he comes down on her other side (the `backThrow` hook): a real side change on the point simulation, one continuous arc, never a snap. 10 damage, knockdown. Out of the corner it puts him in it; with her own back within 150 px of a wall there's no room, and it's a plain toss forward.
- **HANG UP**, 3, 1, 1: snap kick, jab, then the handset swung up over her head on a short length of cord and slammed down on him. An overhead ender, 14 damage, knockdown (28 for the string on TEMP).
