- CPU: plain scrapper. Lashes the cord at a whiff and at someone standing or walking in at its far end, Transfers a guard up close, and Transfers out of her own corner when there's room behind her.

**LAWYER** ("objection. also, you're on the floor"). Periwinkle with amber pants (`pants: '#ffb02e'`; mirror: mint with pink pants); round reading glasses and a briefcase in the rear hand that hangs from the handle and swings out along the forearm when the arm swings. TEMP's proportions, limbs 11.6 wide, a 2 degree lean. Average everything (health 100, walk and run 0.98x, regen 1.05x, damage 1.0x): his edge is the close game. Keeps every shared string.
- **Objection!**, ← 1: a throw counter (the `throwCounter` hook, a sibling of `parry` for grabs). The weight rocks back and the lead arm shoots out, finger pointed at him; startup plus a 220 ms hold is the catch window. A throw or command grab landing in it is caught (OBJECTION! and the parry's flash), and he answers with **Sustained**: his pointing hand takes the wrist, the briefcase hand the collar, and he throws him over in front of him (an unbreakable grab, 12 damage, the shared thrown reaction). It catches the plain throw and SECURITY's ESCORT (lawyer-test shows both). Any strike beats it (a jab in its startup is a counter hit), and a whiff drops the arm into a long, open recovery. 14 stamina.
- **Cross-Examination**, → 1: a short, sharp point to the sternum with a small step in. Mid, 5 damage, 26 frames of hitstun, 15 of blockstun against a short recovery: **+2.5 frames on block**, and instead of bouncing off the guard he steps in (`blockPush`), so his throw still reaches: pressed as he recovers it grabs him before he's out of blockstun (his answers are the break and his own Objection!), and a guard that ducks to beat the throw eats a mid (the snap kick). On hit the cross links (15 damage for the pair). 14 stamina.
- **CLOSING ARGUMENT**, 2, 3, 2: cross, snap kick, then the briefcase wound all the way back and swung round at rib height behind a step. Mid ender, 15 damage, knockdown, wall splat (31 for the string on TEMP, by the wall).
- CPU: style grappler. Objects to a grab he sees starting up (a reflex special, looked at every step, so a slow ESCORT gets caught far more than a quick throw), and now and then to someone walking in on him or while he's in blockstun; runs the tick: the point, then on block the throw or a mid, on hit the cross.

**THE CEO** ("the board has decided you're a cost"). Gold with cyan suit trousers (`pants: '#2ee8ff'`; mirror: orange with blue), dark lapels down the chest, a cyan tie and a gold watch on the lead wrist. A little bigger and broader (scale 1.03, torso 16.5, limbs 12) with the arms a hair short (23.4 / 22.4) so the size isn't all reach. A touch better at most things: health 102, walk and run 1.03x, jump 1.02x, dash 1.03x, weight 1.04. The weakness is the bar: **stamina 0.8x**, the smallest in the building, so a full string and a special can faint him. Keeps every shared string. When TEMP plays P2 against him the shared color rule makes TEMP cyan (orange clashes with gold), the same cyan as his trousers; the gold torso still tells them apart.
- **Golden Parachute**, ← 1: knees dip and he hops straight back about 87 px, hands up straightening the tie. For the first 200 ms strikes and throws pass through him (the `invuln` hook; a throw he slips loses its lock), then he lands and the landing is open (ceo-test shows a cross dodged, a throw dodged, and a cross that follows him in catching the landing). It never strikes. 14 stamina.
- **Hostile Takeover**, ↘ 2 (down-forward and 2): both fists clasped low by the rear hip, a long step, and up through his chin together. From the ground a launcher: mid, 12 damage, kb 150 / 540, 26 stamina, 12 frames of blockstun against a long recovery (unsafe). On a body already in the air it's the ender instead (`onJuggle`): 14 damage, kb 820, a splat, the HOSTILE TAKEOVER callout, and he goes across the floor into the wall. ↘ 2, ↘ 2 is the combo (20 damage into a splat on TEMP), and it ends the shared uppercut's launch too (17). After a jab in the juggle a 2 continues a shared string instead (1, 2 is ONE-TWO), so it's launcher then takeover.
- **STOCK SPLIT**, 2, 1, 2: cross, jab, then the rear fist digs into the gut and splits off upward through the chin: two hits on the `hits` hook (5, then 11 with a knockdown), 30 damage for the string on TEMP.
- CPU: plain scrapper. Hops back from a strike he sees coming (a reflex special, a small chance each step of its startup), launches a whiff and now and then a standing opponent up close, and finishes his own juggles with the takeover.
- **Secret boss.** Clear the arcade ladder's boss without using a continue (and not as THE CEO) and THE CEO is waiting upstairs: a seventh stage at difficulty 1.0 in the showcase style (`SECRET_BOSS` and `secretStage()` in `src/flow/ladder.js`, appended to the run's ladder by `nextStage()` in `src/flow/index.js`). The stage-clear card after THE MANAGER still reads THE BUILDING IS YOURS, so it's a surprise; the ending names him as the boss.

**Measurements** (RECEPTIONIST, LAWYER, THE CEO). CPU vs CPU at difficulty 0.7, 48 matches a pairing (two blocks of 24 under different seeds, sides swapped every other match), rounds won: RECEPTIONIST 58, TEMP 62 (48%); RECEPTIONIST 77, COURIER 43 (64%); RECEPTIONIST 43, SECURITY 74 (37%); LAWYER 57, TEMP 63 (48%); LAWYER 66, COURIER 55 (55%); LAWYER 63, SECURITY 56 (53%); THE CEO 63, TEMP 57 (52%); THE CEO 59, COURIER 64 (48%); THE CEO 40, SECURITY 76 (34%); RECEPTIONIST 60, LAWYER 62 (49%); RECEPTIONIST 47, THE CEO 72 (39%); LAWYER 59, THE CEO 63 (48%). The 24-match blocks swing 10 to 20 points between seeds (THE CEO against SECURITY ran 29 to 54% across the tuning runs), so the out-of-band ones are SECURITY's armored shoulder check (it lands about four times in five against both, 96 and 115 hits) and RECEPTIONIST against COURIER. All 1,439 rounds ended by KO, on average 14 to 20 seconds in. Unique moves in those 576 matches: 222 Please Holds (117 hit), 174 Transfers (88 landed), 8 HANG UPs; 357 Cross-Examinations (67 hit, most of the rest blocked: it's the tick), 172 CLOSING ARGUMENTs (162 hit), 25 Objections catching 18 throws and grabs (13 of them against SECURITY; the CPU objects to what it sees starting up, and a plain throw's startup is a few frames); 263 Golden Parachutes (73 strikes and throws let through), 190 Hostile Takeovers (70 hit, 10 as the juggle ender), 172 STOCK SPLITs (322 hits). A random-button masher playing each of the three against a TEMP CPU at 0.7 loses every round (0-6 each over three matches), botching about 17 times a match, best combo 1 to 2. Save/restore: six recorded CPU bouts at difficulty 0.9 (RECEPTIONIST vs LAWYER, THE CEO and SECURITY; LAWYER vs THE CEO and SECURITY; THE CEO vs TEMP; 9,000 steps each, every one crossing a match end; inputs, the rng state after each CPU call and `grabId`, `rollId`, `aaJump` recorded, the recorders registered as script controllers) replayed identically to the recording (54,000 steps), then took 2,517 rollbacks of 1 to 16 steps (save, run, restore, run, restore, run), 1,032 of them saved inside one of the new moves, a grab or a thrown body in flight: all 43,242 restored steps matched the reference's deep hash of the whole `saveState()`. Nudging a fighter's `x` by 0.01 px on one restore made it differ at once. Every test phase (receptionist-test, lawyer-test, ceo-test: 24 phases) plays as named; zero console errors on index.html (through the menus to the fight), ?scene=play, combo-test, attract and the three test scenes.

**CHEF** ("the special today is you"). White like TEMP, so the red kitchen pants (`pants: '#ff3b3b'`) are what tell them apart; a chef's toque with a red band and an apron tied at the waist, in strokes with a dark edge so the white reads on white. A big, square rig (scale 1.08, a broad torso, arms and legs a little short of TEMP's so the size isn't reach, limbs 11 wide), a 3 degree lean and a wider stance. Health 100, walk 0.82x, back-walk 0.88 of that, run 0.86x, jump 0.9x, dash 0.9x, weight 1.12, regen 0.88x, damage 0.9x: heavy and slow, and the heavy hits are the uniques. Drops SKY SPIKE.
- **Cleaver**, ← 2: the rear hand goes up behind the head and he rocks back and hangs there a beat (readable: contact about 17 frames after the press), then a step and the arm chops straight down. Armored from the press until recovery (one hit at half damage, `armor`), an overhead (it hits a crouching guard; a standing guard holds it), 13 damage, knockdown, a short `travel` in through the chop. 26 stamina; 10 frames of blockstun against a long recovery, so blocked it's a free punish (chef-test shows the cross).
- **Flambé**, ← 1: both hands drop to the hip, he sinks, and both arms flick up in front of him as he drives up onto his toes behind a short glide (`travel` 260 px/s for 120 ms). Mid, 11 damage, launches into a juggle (kb 110 / 480; jabs juggle after it), a big orange flash (`flashR` 92, `flashColor`). 24 stamina, 12 frames of blockstun. Short: it's an up-close move.
- **EIGHTY-SIX**, 1, 1, 4: jab, jab, then the rear knee comes up and the foot drives straight out like a boot through the kitchen door. Mid ender, 15 damage, kb 760, wall splat near a wall (the EIGHTY-SIXED callout).
- CPU: plain scrapper. Chops a croucher, a strike he sees starting (through it, on the armor) and a body getting up; flicks a whiff and a standing body up close; opens with the jab when he has the bar for 1, 1, 4.

**WELLNESS COACH** ("breathe in. now breathe through the floor"). Green like SECURITY, so the pink pants (`pants: '#ff2bd6'`) tell them apart; a cyan headband with its tails flying and a rolled yoga mat slung across her back. A slim rig on long legs (33.5/32.5, scale 0.98) with thin limbs (10.8 wide) and a degree of backward lean: the legs give the reach, the thin limbs keep the hitbox and hurtbox small (the balance note). Health 104, walk 1.02x, back-walk 1.05 of that, run 1.02x, jump 1.08x, weight 0.98, regen 1.2x, damage 1.03x. Keeps every shared string.
- **Tree Pose**, ← 1, held (the `stance` hook): she stands tall on the rear leg with the lead foot tucked to the knee and the hands together over her head. It holds while 1 is held (up to about 1.1 s, then the foot comes down), or for 380 ms on a tap, and then she steps out (Exhale). Out of it, whatever the stick says: **4 Crane Kick**, the tucked leg unfolds straight up to his chin while she lays back under it (mid, so it hits a crouching guard; highs pass over her, `tc`; 12 damage, launches, kb 120 / 470; 20 stamina); **3 Root Sweep**, the foot plants ahead and the rear leg sweeps the floor (low, so it goes under a standing guard; 10 damage, knockdown, a short glide in; 18 stamina); **2 Exhale**, the hands float down and she's back in her guard. The stance costs 10, never strikes, can't guard, and a hit in its first 100 ms is a counter, so the read is the whole move: crouch and the crane kick hits, stand and the sweep does.
- **SUN SALUTATION**, 1, 3, 4: jab, snap kick, then she lays back into a backbend with the hands reaching for the floor behind her, the rear leg sweeps up over the top in a split and the heel comes down on him. Overhead ender, low-profile (`tc`), 15 damage, knockdown.
- CPU: plain scrapper. Goes into the tree at the edge of her reach and reads him (a crouch gets the crane kick, a standing guard the sweep, a coin flip otherwise), trees a whiff into the crane kick, and opens with the jab for 1, 3, 4.

**ELECTRICIAN** ("did you try turning yourself off and on"). Blue with yellow work pants (`pants: '#ffe066'`) and a matching hard hat with a ridge and brim; a tool belt with a pouch and a red tester. TEMP's frame a touch bigger (scale 1.02, a thicker torso, arms and legs a little short of TEMP's), a 2 degree lean. Health 98, walk 0.96x, run 0.95x, jump 0.98x, weight 1.04, regen 1.05x, damage 0.95x. Keeps every shared string.
- **Live Wire**, → 1: the lead hand comes back to the chest with two fingers out and snaps out at chest height behind a half step. Mid, 7 damage, 22 frames of hitstun plus the `shock`: 26 frames more (scaled by the combo's decay, once per combo), with a jitter on the reaction layer, yellow arcs crackling off him and SHOCKED over his head. On its own that's 48 frames of stun, room to walk in and start a string (2, 3 lands in electrician-test); a second Live Wire in the same combo only stuns. 20 stamina, 8 frames of blockstun.
- **Ground Fault**, ↘ 3: down into a half crouch and the lead foot stamps onto his front foot. Low, 6 damage, 20 frames of hitstun, then the `root`: 30 frames (half a second) when he can't walk, run or jump, though he still guards and crouches (ROOTED, a yellow ring at each foot). 18 stamina.
- **SHORT CIRCUIT**, 2, 1, 2: cross, jab, then both palms pulled to the ribs and driven out together behind a long step. Mid ender, 12 damage, kb 560, knockdown, a yellow flash.
- CPU: plain scrapper. Pokes a standing body with Live Wire and, once he's buzzing, walks in and opens a string; Live Wires a whiff; stamps a standing guard and a body walking in.

**Measurements** (phase 3, CHEF, WELLNESS COACH and ELECTRICIAN). CPU vs CPU at difficulty 0.7, two blocks of 24 matches a pairing under different seeds (48 in all), sides swapped every other match, rounds won: CHEF 51, TEMP 64 (44%); CHEF 64, COURIER 55 (54%); CHEF 59, SECURITY 61 (49%); CHEF 68, WELLNESS COACH 48 (59%); CHEF 57, ELECTRICIAN 65 (47%); WELLNESS COACH 59, TEMP 65 (48%); WELLNESS COACH 71, COURIER 48 (60%); WELLNESS COACH 61, SECURITY 58 (51%); WELLNESS COACH 74, ELECTRICIAN 51 (59%); ELECTRICIAN 59, TEMP 55 (52%); ELECTRICIAN 65, COURIER 56 (54%); ELECTRICIAN 63, SECURITY 66 (49%). A single 24-match block swings 10 points or more either way (the first builds ran from 31 to 74%), which is why these are 48. Every one of the 1,443 rounds ended by KO, on average 15 to 20 seconds in, with 1.1 to 1.8 botches a match. Unique moves pressed / landed across those 576 matches: Cleaver 326 / 202, Flambé 322 / 59 (short: the CPU throws it a little long), EIGHTY-SIX 212 / 203; Tree Pose 177 (Crane Kick 48 / 28, Root Sweep 68 / 27, Exhale 36), SUN SALUTATION 152 / 140; Live Wire 263 / 126, Ground Fault 167 / 109, SHORT CIRCUIT 186 / 181. A random-button masher playing each of them against a 0.7 TEMP loses every round (0-6 over three matches each, about 19 botches a match, best combo 1 to 2). Save/restore: six recorded CPU bouts at 0.9 (CHEF v ELECTRICIAN, WELLNESS COACH v ELECTRICIAN, WELLNESS COACH v CHEF, CHEF v TEMP, WELLNESS COACH v SECURITY, ELECTRICIAN v COURIER) plus two of a scripted WELLNESS COACH working the stance (every button out of it, held and tapped) against a TEMP CPU, 9,000 steps each, each crossing a match end; inputs, the rng state after each CPU call and `grabId`, `rollId`, `aaJump` recorded: all 72,000 plain replay steps matched the recording's hash of the whole `saveState()`, and 4,088 rollbacks of 1 to 16 steps (save, run, restore, run, restore, run; 798 saved inside a unique move, a stance, a shock or a root) matched on all 42,026 restored steps. Adding 7 to a restored `shockT` or `rootT`, or moving a stance's `attackStart`, made it differ within the window 116 times out of 120 (the other 4 were a stance held through the window, where `attackStart` isn't read). That replay found a snapshot bug from before: `saveSim` into a reused snapshot kept reach entries the sim had learned after that point (`s.reach` was only ever added to), so a restore could bring back a reach learned later; it now drops them. Zero console errors on index.html (into a fight through the menus), ?scene=play, combo-test, attract and the three test scenes.

### Adding a character

Replace your character's file in `src/characters/` with a default export like `courier.js` and `security.js`. A brand new id also goes in `worker/roster.ts`, so its picks and matches get counted (the build fails until it's there). Nothing else needs editing unless you need a mechanic no hook covers. Then add a generic hook in `src/combat/sim.js`, read from frame data or `mechanics`, and document it here. The definition:

- `id` (the file's name: `intern`, `helpdesk`, `janitor`, `manager`, `accountant`, `windowWasher`), `name` (caps, on the HUD and the select screen), `tagline`.
- `color` (the body's neon), `altColor` (P2 in a mirror match), `accent` (the accessory and highlights). Neon arcade only: no monospace, no terminal look.
- `stats`, each relative to TEMP (all default 1 except health): `health` (out of 100), `walk`, `backWalk` (a share of the walk), `run`, `jump`, `dash` (multiplies every `travel`), `weight` (knockback taken: x by 1/weight, y by 1/sqrt(weight)), `stamina` (the bar's size), `regen`, `damage` (every hit he lands).
- `body`, for the rig (`createFighter({ body })`, `rig.setBody`): `scale`, `headR`, `upperArm`, `lowerArm`, `upperLeg`, `lowerLeg`, `lowerTorso`, `upperTorso`, `limbW`, `limbTip`, `torsoW`, `hipW` (defaults in `RIG`, `src/fighter/index.js`), `lean` (degrees of extra forward pitch), `stance` (degrees each leg spreads), and `accessory(ctx, joints, { scale, facing, color, accent, headR })`: a few strokes drawn over the head on the figures layer, so it's lit like the body. Hit and hurt radii and the contact surface scale with `scale` and the limb and torso widths, and a walk or run cycle plays at the rate that keeps feet planted at the character's speed. Watch the reach: a longer arm or a thicker fist wins the poke game outright (SECURITY's first build won three rounds in four on jabs and crosses alone).
- `cloth` (optional): a list of things that hang and swing (a tie, a lanyard and badge, an apron's hem, a ponytail, a tool on a lead), each a short chain the life layer swings (see Layout, The life layer, Cloth). An item is `{ draw, axis, at, off, dir, len, seg }` plus optional tuning, lengths in px at scale 1:
  - `axis: [a, b]` two joints for the body frame (down runs from `a` to `b`, forward is the facing side; `['head', 'neck']` for something on the head), `at` how far from `a` to `b` the anchor sits and `off: [forward, down]` px from there;
  - `dir: [forward, down]` its rest direction in that frame (an array of pairs gives each segment its own; a function `(J, facing) => { x, y }` computes it), `hang` 0..1 leaning it toward plain world down, `len` its length and `seg` its segment count (2 to 5 is right; `len` may be an array of segment lengths);
  - `hz` how firmly it holds its rest shape (2 is a tie), `damping` how fast a swing dies (0.2 loose, 0.45 heavy cloth), `air` drag in a run, `gravity`, `limit` the most a segment swings off its rest (radians), `side: 'front'` or `'back'` with `clear` px to keep it off the body line, `push` thigh joints that shove it aside, `end` a joint to pin the last point to (a cord between the hip and the hand), `kick` how hard a hit flings it, and `layer: 'back'` to draw it behind the far arm, the body and the head (the default draws it over the chest after the head and the accessory);
  - `draw(ctx, P, { scale, facing, color, accent, R, headR, pose, J })` gets the chain's points, anchor first. Without the life layer `P` is the rest shape. `src/fighter/cloth.js` has helpers for the strokes (`chainPath`, `ribbonPath`, `chainAt`, `chainNormal`, `coilPath`).
  A rigid part stays in `accessory`; only what hangs goes in `cloth`. A tie, for example, is one line: `cloth: [{ draw: tie, axis: ['neck', 'chest'], off: [3.6, 4], dir: [0.12, 1], len: 20.5, seg: 3, hz: 1.8, damping: 0.25, side: 'front', clear: 3.6 }]`. Drawn only, so it costs the sim nothing; flat color, never a glow.
- `life` (optional): how it moves, drawn only: `{ spring, damp, eye, breath, breathAmp, sway, swayHz, bounce, bounceHz, habits, laugh, ko }`, every field optional (see Layout, The life layer, Signatures). Give a new character at least its own numbers, so nobody moves identically.
- `dance` (optional, every character has one): `{ keys, blend }`, a looping clip built with the same `K`/`F` kit as the moves. The registry adds it as `<id>_dance` (a clip only: no frame data, so nothing can press it). When a match is won, the winner breaks into it the moment it's standing free on the ground in the sim's `match` phase (the `celebrate` state in `src/combat/sim.js`: no input, no movement) and loops it through the WINS card; never in horde. It's sim state like any clip, so both machines in an online match play it the same (`NET_VERSION` 14). Each fighter page shows it as a CELEBRATION card. The 18: TEMP's stunned double fist pump and brow wipe, INTERN's star jump and spin, HELPDESK's robot and reboot slump, COURIER's running man, SECURITY's folded arms and double flex, JANITOR's mop shuffle, THE MANAGER's tie, golf clap and point, ACCOUNTANT's finger count and tidy fist pump, WINDOW WASHER's wax-on circles, RECEPTIONIST's phone at the ear and hip sway, LAWYER's point and lapel strut, THE CEO's make it rain, HR REP's clipboard scribble and two thumbs up, SALES REP's finger guns, BARISTA's shaker, CHEF's kiss and stir, WELLNESS COACH's tree pose and bow, ELECTRICIAN's boogaloo wave. A spin ends on a 360° copy of the first key with `d: 0`, so the loop wraps without unwinding.
- `victory` and `taunt` (fighter mastery, every character has both; README, Progression, Fighter mastery): `{ keys, blend }` clips built with the same kit. The registry adds them as `<id>_victory` (played once and held: it replaces the dance on the WINS banner for a player who unlocked it at mastery 4) and `<id>_taunt` (played once by the last round's winner under the next ROUND card, from mastery 2; at most `TAUNT_MAX_MS`, 1.2 s, or it's cut at FIGHT). Clips only, no frame data, so nothing can press them. Flat color, no glow; a taunt makes no new defense or input. A new character also needs a line in `src/career/mastery.js` `KIT` (the pose's and the taunt's names) and in `MASTERY_RAW` in `src/career/palettes.js` (its mastery palette: id, name, body, pants, accent, flat colors away from its own); `npm run test:mastery` fails until all four are there. Nothing else: the ranks, the unlocks, the closet, character select and the fighter page's MASTERY section all come from those.
- `perfect` (optional, every character has one): the perfect round finisher (Arcade flow, Perfect rounds). `{ name, note, stamp, hand, hold, fly, slide, keys }`. `keys` is a clip built with the same kit, usually opening with `stoop()` and `lift()` from `src/characters/perfectKit.js`; it needs one key with `ev: 'grab'` (the body leaves the floor for `hand`, `'lHand'` or `'rHand'`) and one with `ev: 'release'` (it's thrown), and its last key is the pose he holds while the body slides down the glass. The registry adds it as `<id>_perfect` and warns when a key is missing. `hold` is how the body hangs from the hand: `'collar'`, `'ankle'` or `'carry'` (laid across the hands). `fly: { spin, flip, arc, wobble }` is whole turns of spin and of pancake flip (so it lands upright), the arc's height in px and a wobble. `slide: { dx, rate, chute, squeegee, blink }` is how it goes down the glass. `stamp` is the word stamped on the glass, `note` is the fighter page's line. A key's `face` (0..1, drawn only) is how far he has turned toward the camera (Perfect rounds, The throw comes at you); `stoop()` stays in the side view and `lift(kind, hand, d, face)` ends at 0.2 by default, and each clip turns on from there, square or close to it at the `release` key. `step: { grow, sink }` is the step at the lens around the release (0.06 and 8 px by default; `false` for none), and `turn: { face, depth }` scales every key's face and sets the perspective distance in px (230; smaller foreshortens harder). A spin in the clip ends on a 360° key followed by a `d: 0` key at 0°, so the idle after it doesn't unwind. Each fighter page shows it as a PERFECT card: `PerfectView` in `site/rigs.js` plays the clip on a TEMP lying in front of him and draws the thrown body with the game's own `src/fx/perfect.js` (the card stands in for the screen), beside the finisher's name, the note and the stamp.
- `moves`: `{ name: { rig, frame } }`. Names are global: never reuse a shared or another character's name (registration throws). `rig` is a clip in the `MOVE_DEFS` format, built with `RIG_KIT` from `src/fighter/index.js`: `K(pose, ms, ease, ev)`, the IK helpers `F`, `L`, `leg`, the guard feet and hip `LF`, `RF`, `HIP`, the crouch `crouchP` and `CH`, and `base(name)` with `deepKeys` to start from a shared clip. A clip has `blend`, `limb` (the joint that carries the hitbox: `lHand`, `rHand`, `lFoot`, `rFoot`, `lKnee`, `rKnee`, `lElbow`, `rElbow`; button 1 is the lead hand, 2 the rear, 3 the lead foot, 4 the rear), `keys` with one `'active'` key (more for a multi-hit) and a `'recover'` key (the cancel window runs from it), and optionally `shift` (root travel when it ends), `air`, `chainFrom`. `frame` is a `FRAME_DATA` entry (`src/combat/frameData.js`; `addFrameData` fills what's left out): `limb` ('hand' or 'foot', for its radius), `damage`, `kb`, `hitstun`, `blockstun`, `hitstop`, `knockdown`, `height` ('high', 'mid', 'low', 'overhead', 'throw'), `cost`, `cancels`, `reach`, `flashR`, `juggle`, `splat`, `groundBounce`, `ender`, `callout`, `tc`, `react` (a reaction clip), and the hooks below. A frame-only entry (no `rig`) plays another clip through `anims: ['clip']` (SAME DAY's ender plays the slide).
- `commands`: `{ button, motion, move }`. Buttons '1' to '4', '1+3', '2+4'; motions '' (neutral), 'f', 'b', 'd', 'df', 'db', 'ff' (dashing: in a forward dash, in a run, or with forward held just after either), 'run', 'rise' (rising: down just let go out of a crouch or a crouching attack), 'air', 'airDown'. A command replaces the shared move for that button and motion (every button has a shared 'ff' and a shared 'rise' move now, so a character's own still wins its button and it gets the shared ones on the rest), and the most specific motion wins. A string continuation still beats a command.
- `strings`: `{ drop: ['SKY SPIKE'], add: [{ name, keys, moves }] }`. Keys are tokens ('1'..'4', 'd1'..'d4'; after the first, a plain token also matches its down variant), moves the move for each press, the last being the ender. Strings that share a prefix must agree on its moves. Registration warns about conflicts, including a sequence that collides through the down variant (1, 2, d3 is UPTOWN).
- `mechanics`: `airJumps` (extra jumps on a fresh jump press in the air; each refreshes the air attack), `wallJump` (a jump press within 34 px of a wall kicks off it, at most twice a jump), `airJumpClip` (the clip a double jump plays, 'jump' by default; its own clip gets the jump's landing beat too). After an air jump or a wall jump, an air attack doesn't home on the body (from that height the homing swung the leg back under him and lunged the hips past the defender), it connects only once the drawn limb touches the drawn body (2 px), and seating it never slides the defender under the attacker and out the far side.
- `ai`: `style` (the CPU's default personality: null, 'turtle', 'rushdown', 'grappler', 'aerial'; an arcade stage's or the demo's style wins) and `specials`: `{ move, min, max, when, chance, run, cooldown }`. In range [min, max] px and when `when` holds (a function `(me, opp, sim, dist)`, or 'standing', 'crouching', 'guarding', 'attacking', 'whiff', 'wall', 'far'), it's pressed with that chance (scaled by difficulty); a little short of range it walks in (runs with `run: true`). The CPU also runs the character's own strings through its usual string logic. A special can carry `plan(me, opp, sim, dist, toward, kit)` instead of a single move (then `name` keys its cooldown and `cost` is the stamina it needs): it returns the CPU's own list of acts, built with the kit (`move`, `attack`, `press`, `blockFor`, `closer`, `landed`, `chance`), for what one press can't do: a jump, a second jump, a wall jump, a press of a token so a string carries on (WINDOW WASHER's and INTERN's files are the examples). An act's `until` and `onlyIf` see `(me, opp)` every step, so a plan can react mid-air.
- `moveList`: `{ name, input, note }` for the select screen, the HUD's move hints and the top of the MOVE LIST (arrows and digits, e.g. '→→ 3'). The MOVE LIST adds any own move the list leaves out and every string on its own, so the list only needs the moves worth leading with.
- `strategy`: `[{ move, tips }, { string, tips }]`, how to beat each unique move and string, for the fighter page only (Site, STRATEGY). Every unique move and string needs 2 to 4 tips or the build fails, and each tip has to come out of `node gauntlet/counters.mjs <id>`.

**Hooks** (frame data fields the sim reads, `src/combat/sim.js`):
- `travel: { vx, at, ms }`: the body glides vx px/s (x the dash stat) from `at` ms into the clip for `ms`, stopping at the body.
- `armor: { hits, dmg }`: absorbs that many hits from the press until recovery, taking `dmg` of each (0.5 by default), with an ARMOR callout. Throws still take him.
- `parry: { heights, reversal, stun, callout }` (HELPDESK's counter stance): a strike of one of those heights (high and mid by default; never a throw or an air attack) landing in the move's startup or active frames is caught. The striker reels for `stun` frames (40 by default), PARRY (or the callout) pops, and the stance turns into the `reversal` move, which then hits the ordinary way. The stance itself never strikes (its own frame data is never tested as a hit), anything the parrying side had buffered or queued is dropped, and the reversal's step-in glide or `travel` waits out the parry's freeze instead of running down during it.
- `bothSides: true` (JANITOR's Mop Spin): the strike can land behind him. A hit sends the body the way the striking limb points from his root (not toward his facing), it seats on the body on that side, a hit behind makes that his side of her for the side-keeping rule (`home`), and the move is never re-aimed at a jumper or a juggled body, so the spin keeps its shape.
- `throw: true, grab: true, range, hold, noBreak` (a command grab): range px at the press, held `hold` frames, `noBreak` refuses the 1+3 break; `unblockable`, `splat` and the stairwell toss work as on a throw.
- `hits: n` (MANAGER's multi-hit): each later `'active'` key in the clip may connect again, up to n hits. `hitKey` (a unit field) counts the active keys; the first one never re-opens, since a strike can already land in the 45 ms before its key. `rush: { ... }` holds frame data fields for every hit but the last (PERFORMANCE REVIEW's hooks: light, no knockdown, `ender: false, callout: null`); the hit on the last active key is the move itself, so only it knocks down, calls out and scores as an ender. A blocked earlier hit doesn't bounce the attacker off (the rest still meet the guard). Every hit counts in the combo, so the decay and the soft cap apply hit by hit.
- A character's own move that opens one of its strings, named directly by the CPU (`ai.specials`), starts that string from its first key, the way a shared move starts one from its button (ACCOUNTANT's Fine Print).
- `height: 'overhead'`: a mid (a crouch can't duck or low-guard it, a standing guard holds) flagged as an overhead for the CPU and the move list.
- `lowInvuln: true`: lows pass under it from the press until recovery (a hop, a flying knee).
- `wallBounce: true`: near a wall the body bounces off it into a juggle (like WALL SPIN).
- `status: { regen, speed, ms, tag }` (HR REP's Write-Up) lands on the defender with a clean hit (not a block, a juggle or an on-the-ground hit); `selfStatus` (BARISTA's Espresso Shot) lands on the attacker at the move's first active key. `regen` multiplies his stamina regen, `speed` the rate his strikes' startups play at (the rig clock in the startup phase only, so every window downstream moves with it), for `ms`; a fresh one replaces the old, a new round clears both. Plain unit fields (`regenMul`, `regenEnd`, `regenLen`, `regenTag`, `speedMul`, `speedEnd`, `speedLen`, `speedTag`), and the HUD's overhead track shows the tag with a draining bar. A `status` that knocks down would be wasted (a knockdown refills the bar), so EXIT INTERVIEW's doesn't.
- `feint: { ms, into: { pressed: comesOut } }` (SALES REP's Cold Call): a press that resolves to one of the `into` keys (a button press through the commands, or a move the CPU names), made in the strike's first `ms` of startup, replaces it at once with the mapped move. It's a designed cancel, so the botch rule treats it as legal; after the window the same press botches. Map the follow-ups to frame-only versions with their own `travel` or `range` when the strike's lunge has already started (`sr_pitchFeint`, `sr_closeFeint`).
- `react: 'clip'` with a rig-only move (no frame data) as the clip: HR REP's `hr_shoved` backpedals the root 64 px (`shift`), so a standing hit can push him out of range without a knockdown.
- `throwCounter: { reversal, callout }` (LAWYER's Objection!, a sibling of `parry` for grabs): a throw or command grab (anything with `throw: true`, ESCORT included) landing on the move in its startup or active frames is caught. The thrower's grab is dropped (he's left standing, nothing buffered), the callout pops (with the parry's flash and sound), and the stance becomes its `reversal`, which is started and grabs him at once (the reversal's first key is the catch pose, since the grab freezes it there for the hold). Strikes aren't caught: a strike in the stance's startup is a counter hit. Pair it with `noHit` so the stance itself never strikes. A fainted fighter's stance catches nothing.
- `thrownClip: true`: the victim takes the shared `thrown` reaction (lifted, flipped over, slammed down) the way the plain throw does, instead of the generic knockdown (LAWYER's reversal).
- `backThrow: true` (RECEPTIONIST's Transfer): on landing, the throw sends the body back up over the thrower's head (kb.x away from where he was, kb.y up) on the point simulation, and it comes down on the thrower's other side: a real side change you watch happen, in one continuous arc. The victim keeps facing the thrower in the air (no mirror snap) and lands facing away; both `home` sides are cleared so the side-keeping rule doesn't drag the thrower after the body; the thrower doesn't ride after it. With a wall closer than 150 px behind the thrower there's no room, and it's a plain throw forward instead.
- `invuln: { at, ms }` (THE CEO's Golden Parachute): from `at` to `at + ms` ms after the press (on the move's own clock), strikes and throws pass through him. A throw that would have grabbed him in that window loses its lock (it can't grab him later in its active frames), and each strike or throw let through is logged once (`dodge`, for measurement; `dodgedId`, a unit field). It only exists while the move plays: a botch, a hit out of it or the recovery ends it, and pressing it mid-strike botches like anything else.
- `wake: true` (the shared Wake Kick and Wake Sweep): only from lying down (`down`), invulnerable until live, a lying body until the clip's `stand` key (`wakeUp`, a unit field: 1 lying, 2 rising), presses thrown into it are lost. Its clip starts from the knockdown rest pose and ends standing with a `shift` like the getup.
- `otg: true, pin: frames` (the shared Stomp): the strike lands on a floored body for its full damage (once per knockdown, like every ground hit), counted in the combo, never cancelled, and holds the body down `pin` frames (`downPin`).
- `noHit: true`: the move never strikes (its own frame data is never tested as a hit): a stance, a step out.
- `onJuggle: { ... }` (THE CEO's Hostile Takeover): frame data fields used when the hit lands on a juggled or splatted body. The same press is a launcher from the ground and, in the air, an ender with a big kb.x and a splat that carries the body across the floor into the wall.
- `blockPush: px/s` (LAWYER's Cross-Examination): how hard the attacker bounces off a guard (520 by default; a negative value steps him in), so a throw still reaches after a blocked tick.
- `owner` (set by registration): only that character can press it, by button or by the CPU naming it.
- `stance: { moves, hold, min, exit }` (WELLNESS COACH's Tree Pose): from the press until the clip's recovery key, a press of a button in `moves` (`{ 2: 'move', 3: ..., 4: ... }`) is that move, whatever the stick says, and it comes out at once (it never botches, and a held stance button plus another isn't read as a throw). With `hold` (a button, '1'..'4') let go after `min` ms the stance ends in `exit`; held, it lasts as long as the clip's hold keys, then recovers. The stance itself never strikes (nor does a move with `noHit: true`, like the step out), and a hit or a throw ends it like any strike (a hit in its startup is a counter). `stanceOnly: true` keeps a move to the stance: pressed or named outside it, nothing comes out. No new unit state: it reads `attackStart` and the held input.
- `shock: { frames, clip }` (ELECTRICIAN's Live Wire): on a standing hit, `frames` more of stun on top of the hitstun, scaled by the combo's stun decay (`COMBO_STUN`) and once per combo (`shockUsed`, cleared when a new combo starts), so it never loops. `clip` plays on the rig's additive reaction layer (`rig.react`), a jitter over whatever the reaction is doing; `shockT` (a unit field) runs with the stun for the HUD's SHOCKED and the crackle, and a `shock` event fires.
- `root: frames` (ELECTRICIAN's Ground Fault): on a standing hit, `rootT` (a unit field) holds `frames`, counted down once the hit- or blockstun is over: no walking, running, crouch-walking or jumping (the stick does nothing but guard: holding back still blocks, crouching still works, strikes still come out). A knockdown clears it.
- `flashColor: [r, g, b]`: the hit flash's color (`fx.hit`, read by `main.js`), with `flashR` for its size (CHEF's Flambé, orange and 92 px).
- The pose channel `ext` (RECEPTIONIST's phone cord, `src/fighter/index.js` and `src/combat/hitboxes.js`): px past the striking hand that something held reaches. Keys set it like any pose field (a pose without it has none, so every other clip is bit-for-bit the same). While it's over 4 the hitbox is only its far end (two thin circles at the full length and three quarters of it, out along the forearm), so the hand itself hits nothing up close; the accessory gets the pose (`pose` in its options) to draw the object out there. The reach the CPU measures (`sim.reachOf`) includes it.
- `ai.specials` entries with `reflex: true` are checked every step (when the CPU is idle, walking, guarding, crouching or in blockstun) instead of only when it decides, so a character can answer something with a few frames of startup: LAWYER's Objection at a grab starting up, THE CEO's Golden Parachute at a strike. The chance is per step, so keep it small.

The CPU's reach comes from measuring each character's clips on its own rig (`sim.reachOf(unit, move)`, keyed `id:move`), so a new body needs nothing extra. Rollback needs nothing extra either, as long as new per-fighter state lives in plain unit fields (the snapshot copies every one; `armorLeft`, `hitN`, `airJumpsUsed`, `wallJumps` and the travel `glide` are examples) and any math the sim depends on uses `src/combat/dmath.js` (drawing code like an accessory may use `Math`). A new sim field that isn't on a unit belongs in `SIM_SCALARS` in `src/combat/snapshot.js`. Test it the way courier-test and security-test do: a phase per unique move in `PHASES` in `src/main.js`, with the character as P1 in `SCENE_CHARS`.

## Multiplayer

Two players, each on their own phone or computer, fight online. One hosts a room and gets a four-character code and a share link (`https://neonstickfight.com/room/K7QX`; the old `play/?room=` and `/?room=` links get a 301 to it, see Site, and links from before the move, `askscottpierce.com/stick-fight/...`, still land in the room, see Hosting, The move to neonstickfight.com); the other opens the link or types the code. Codes skip 0, O, 1, I and L so they survive being read aloud.

**How it fits together.**

- `worker/index.ts`: `POST /api/rooms` makes a room and returns its code; `GET /api/rooms/<code>/ws?role=host|guest` opens the room's WebSocket. Each room is one Durable Object (hibernating WebSockets). It passes every message from one player to the other unchanged, and writes only its own `{"t":"sys"}` notices: welcome, peer joined or left, and errors (`no-room`, `full`, `busy`, `flood`). What it reads for itself is what the host addresses to the room for spectators (see Spectators below). The one message it keeps instead of passing on is a page's own `{"t":"sys","ev":"result"}` at the end of a match, which goes on the books only when both players' pages send the same one (Analytics, Match bookkeeping). `GET /api/ice` returns `{ iceServers, ttl, turn }`: Cloudflare STUN plus TURN credentials good for an hour (see Hosting, Secrets), or public STUN only with `turn: false` when the Worker has no TURN key or Cloudflare doesn't answer. Each Worker isolate reuses one set of credentials for 10 minutes, so every set handed out has at least 50 minutes left.
- `src/net/link.js`: fetches `api/ice` as the link opens (2.5 seconds at most, else public STUN), then the WebSocket carries the WebRTC offer, answer and ICE candidates; game packets then go peer to peer over a DataChannel (unordered, no retransmits). With TURN, two phones on carrier NAT that can't reach each other directly still get a DataChannel, through Cloudflare's TURN servers. If the DataChannel isn't open within 5 seconds, packets go through the room's WebSocket instead (the relay); a DataChannel that opens later takes over. `link.candidate` says which path it took (`host` on the same network, `srflx` through a NAT, `relay` through TURN). `?relay=1` on the URL forces the WebSocket relay, and `?ice=relay` allows TURN candidates only, both for testing.
- **Limits** (`worker/index.ts`, `wrangler.jsonc`): per client IP (an IPv6 address counts by its /64), 10 new rooms a minute, 5 new crew rooms a minute, 30 WebSocket joins a minute (quick rooms, crew rooms and spectators together) and 20 profile and sign-in calls a minute, counted per Cloudflare location (the sign-in caps per email and per hour are in D1; see Profiles and sign-in). Over the limit, `POST /rooms` answers 429 `{"error":"busy","message":"Too many tries from your network. Try again shortly."}` with `Retry-After: 60`, and a join gets a socket that sends `{"t":"sys","ev":"error","reason":"busy"}` and closes, so the page can say why; the menus show SERVER BUSY: TRY AGAIN SHORTLY. In a room, a message over 16 KB is dropped, and each socket gets a bucket of 480 messages refilling at 240 a second (about 4x a match's traffic); past it, messages are dropped, and after 1,000 drops the socket gets `reason: "flood"` and is closed. A room nobody has joined lasts 30 minutes; once its players have all left, its code stops working after 5 minutes (time to reload a page), and the alarm then deletes its storage and itself, leaving nothing behind.
- `src/net/rollback.js`: rollback netcode, GGPO style (see Rollback below). Both machines run the whole sim. Each runs its own input after a 2-frame delay (17 ms at 120 Hz), guesses the other player's input by repeating the last one it got, and keeps going; when the real input arrives and differs from the guess, it loads the state saved before that frame and re-runs the sim to the present, silently. Each side sends its input with every input the other side hasn't acknowledged, so a lost packet is covered by the next one. Every 30 frames, once a frame is confirmed (both real inputs in), each side hashes the sim (`src/net/hash.js`: positions, velocities, states, health, stamina, timers and every rig joint, by exact bits) and sends the hash; on a mismatch the host sends the guest its confirmed state and the guest loads it and runs back up to the present (see Resync below), and both log the fields that differ to the console.
- `src/net/codec.js`: an input is 12 bits (direction, jump, guard, crouch, run, the four limbs, whether it came from the touch stick, which turns off the stick's own double taps, and `dash`, the double tap the touch stick's double flick stands for). An input packet is 15 bytes (with the match number) plus 2 per frame.
- `src/net/online.js`: the session (host, join, start, leave), the connection readout (bottom-left; centered under the timer on a touch screen: `P2P 13 MS · RB 3 · DELAY 2`, the round trip, how many frames the last correction re-ran, and the input delay; amber when relayed, plus a brief RESYNC after a resync, WAITING FOR RIVAL while the link can't keep up, and DESYNC only for a mismatch no resync has fixed in 3 seconds), and a lobby panel. The host is P1 (left), the guest P2; each plays the character picked on the pick screen in the room (`net.setCharacter(id)` sets where that screen's cursor opens and rides the `ready` message; the `pick` messages carry the live choice; the host's `start` message carries `chars: [host's, guest's]` and both sims build their fighters from it), in its own neon (a mirror match gives the guest the character's alt color; TEMP vs TEMP is white and orange as before). The HUD names them YOU and RIVAL after the character's name. Both screens show the same thing: the host is P1 on the left and the guest P2 on the right, the HUD's YOU bar on whichever side is yours (nobody's view is mirrored; two players in the same room see the same layout on both screens). When both are linked the host picks the seed and both start the `online` scene from frame 0. A match ends on a frame both machines agree on, and the room stays open for the next one (see Between matches below). If the other player leaves, nothing arrives for 15 seconds, the match has stalled for 20 seconds with packets still coming, or the two sides can't agree on the final state for 15 seconds, the match ends on both sides as CONNECTION LOST (the side that gives up tells the other with `{t:'lost'}`); with the arcade flow running that's its VERSUS ONLINE end screen (YOUR RIVAL LEFT, CONNECTION LOST), and on a `?scene=` page it's a notice and the demo.

**Starting a match.** The game's menus call the API on `window.__net` (also returned by `createOnline` in `main.js`): `net.host(opts)` resolves to `{ code, url }`, `net.join(code, opts)` (the menus pass `{ pickFirst: true }` for every room, quick or crew, so the host's first message is a `go` at match 0 and both open on the pick screen; a `?scene=` page's lobby panel leaves it off and starts at once), `net.leave()`, `net.roomFromUrl()` reads `?room=`, and `net.on('status' | 'connected' | 'start' | 'peer-left' | 'end' | 'desync', fn)`. `net.openLobby('menu' | 'host' | 'join', code?)` opens the built-in panel (keyboard, gamepad and touch: arrows or D-pad move, Enter or A presses, Esc or B backs out, and on the code box up and down turn the letter like arcade initials). `net.busy` is true while that panel is up, so the title screen shouldn't start a game then. The menu flow (`src/flow/`) owns the VERSUS ONLINE entry, its own HOST and JOIN screens and the `?room=` hand-off; it talks to `online.js` only through `src/flow/netApi.js` (`adaptOnline`), ignores its own input while `net.busy`, and gets the end of a match through the `endMatch` hook in `main.js` (which closes the lobby's notice in favor of the flow's screen).

**No mirrored view.** Online, nobody's view is flipped: both screens show the host on the left and the guest on the right. `?mirror=1` on the URL still draws any scene mirrored left-right as a debug view (`main.js` `syncMirror()`: the WebGL canvas gets a CSS `scaleX(-1)`, the HUD reads `camera.mirror`, `src/combat/input.js` flips `dx` at capture, and the stereo pan flips), but it never turns on by itself.

**Between matches (the room session).** `online.js` keeps the room's session after a match: the match number `n`, the tally, and the post-match and pick choices. The agreement rules are its, the screens the flow's.

- **The end of a match.** `rollback.js` notes the frame on which the sim first shows the result (`phase` becomes `'match'`; a correction that re-runs that frame finds it again). `online.js` halts the netcode 150 frames later (`END_HOLD`, 1.25 s of the WINS card; the sim's own 1.8 s hold would reseed and start another match) and ends the match once that frame is confirmed and both sides have said so with the same state hash (`{t:'end', n, f, h, e}`), so both machines stop on the same state and count the same result from `sim.wins`. The halt itself is the netcode's (`endHold`), so a correction that moves the result moves the halt with it. Two different end states are a desync like any other: the host's state goes to the guest and the guest says again; a word from before the other side's last resync (its epoch `e`) doesn't count against it. The finished netcode keeps sending its inputs and acks every 100 ms until the next match starts, so the other side can confirm its last frames even if the final packets were lost. Each side then hashes the final state; the host sends `{t:'result', n, tally, hash, f}` and the guest checks the hash and takes the host's tally if its own ever differs (it hasn't in testing).
- **The tally** counts matches (the host's best-of), host wins, guest wins and draws, plus rounds, for the room session; `net.tally` (and the flow's `hud().tally`, for the fight HUD) gives it from this side: `{ you, rival, draw, roundsYou, roundsRival, games }`. Each `start` message carries the host's tally too. **All time:** each browser keeps a random id (`stick-fight-pid` in localStorage) that rides the `ready` message, and a win, loss and draw count per rival id (`stick-fight-rivals`, the 30 most recent rivals). It's from this browser's side only and resets if either player clears site data. That's the record for quick rooms; in a crew match the record is the crew's, kept on the server (see Crew rooms), and this one isn't touched. The `ready` message also carries the player's name (`net.setName`), which the post-match and pick screens show in place of RIVAL.
- **Messages** (control JSON through the room, all carrying `n` so a late one from another match is dropped): `post` (each side's choice, `want`: rematch, chars or wait, its cursor, `focus`, and `poke` for a press that stops the auto countdown), `cd` (host to guest: a countdown's kind, `auto`, `agree` or `host`, and the ms left; the host's clock runs every countdown and starts the match), `go` (host to guest: NEW CHARACTERS), `pick` (each side's card and whether it's ready), `result`, `alive` (every 3 s between matches; 15 s without a word from the other side is CONNECTION LOST), `start` (`seed`, `chars`, `botch`, `n`, `tally`), `end` (the final state, above) and `lost` (this side gave up on the link). The netcode's own, tagged with the match number `m`: `hash` (`f`, `h`, the sender's resync epoch `e`), `dump`, `resync?` (the guest asks) and `sync` (the host's state in parts, below). Every input packet carries the match number (one byte) and every hash and desync dump the match number too, so nothing from one match can land in the next one's input history or hash check.
- **Pick first, tested** (2026-11-09, local Worker and Vite, Playwright, each player in its own Chromium): VERSUS ONLINE went straight to the online menu; a host remembered on INTERN and a guest from `?room=CODE&pick=security` both landed on the pick screen on those cards, saw each other's cursor, locked in (the host moved to HELPDESK first) and the match started HELPDESK vs SECURITY on both. A crew match (one desktop on the keyboard, one phone landscape by touch: tap a card, tap again) did the same with the crew's name in the subtitle. A guest closing the tab on the first pick screen gave the host YOUR RIVAL LEFT with HOST AGAIN; a guest's LEAVE ROOM there went to the online menu.
- **Without the menus** (a `?scene=` page, which only has `online.js`'s lobby panel) nobody sees a post-match screen, so the host's auto countdown starts the next match every time, as rematches did before.
- **Robustness.** Both pressing at once: the host decides, so crossed messages settle on the host's side (a guest's NEW CHARACTERS arriving after the host's REMATCH stops the agree countdown, puts the request on the host's screen, and the host presses again; a guest request that arrives after the host started is ignored, and the guest follows the `start`). A guest whose last frames confirm late still follows: a `start` or `go` that arrives before its match ended ends it and applies. A player who leaves, closes the tab or loses the connection between matches ends the room for the other with YOUR RIVAL LEFT or CONNECTION LOST. The room server only passes messages on, so none of this needed a Worker change, and a room stays alive while either player is connected. `NET_VERSION` is 22 (8 was the room session; 9 adds the name in `ready` and the crew match's `go` at match 0; 10 is the side-swap fixes and the stamina change, Controls, Sides and Stamina; 16 opens quick rooms on the pick screen too, which an older guest would sit through waiting for a `start`; 17 is resync, the agreed end of a match and the reach fix, Rollback below; 18 is the stamina refunds and the no-faint save on a connecting hit, Stamina and Faint; 19 keeps bodies on top of the floor, which moves the rig's joints (the hitboxes), Floors; 20 is the faint shake-off, Faint; 21 is getups by the sides and lows on a floored body, Getups; 22 is the perfect round finisher, Arcade flow, Perfect rounds): older builds get YOUR GAMES DIFFER: RELOAD BOTH.
- **Tested** with two browsers (Chromium host, Firefox guest) through the local Worker: an auto rematch, the guest asking for REMATCH and the host accepting, the guest asking for NEW CHARACTERS and the host overriding with REMATCH, crossed presses, NEW CHARACTERS with the guest on a gamepad and the host's START by touch, the guest leaving from the post-match screen, 32 idle seconds on it with both still in the room and the host then leaving, a guest vanishing on the pick screen, a stale room code and a version mismatch. Every match's hash checks and its final state hash matched on both sides, and both tallies agreed after every match.

**Scan to join.** The host screen and the crew lobby draw the join link as a QR code, so a friend standing next to the screen points their phone's camera at it and lands in the room. It's the same link COPY LINK copies: `link || roomLink(code)` on the host screen, `crewLink(slug)` in the crew lobby.

- **The encoder** is `src/qr.js`, written here because the game takes no npm packages at runtime: byte mode, error correction M, versions 1 to 10 (213 bytes at most; a quick-room link is 48 bytes and makes a version 4 code, 33 modules across, and the longest crew link is 74 bytes, version 5, 37 modules). A link too long for it draws no code. `encodeQR(text)` gives the modules, `qrSvg(text)` an inline `<svg>`.
- **How it's drawn.** Black modules on a white tile with the standard 4-module quiet zone inside the tile, square corners, and a thin cyan line around the outside. Nothing glows on it and it's never inverted or tinted: neon-on-dark codes fail on a lot of phone cameras. SCAN TO JOIN sits under it as plain text. It isn't a button, so the keyboard, pad and touch focus skip it.
- **Where.** On the host screen the code and link are on the left and the tile on the right, 34% of the stage's height (245 px at 1280x720, 6 px a module; 133 px on a phone held sideways). Until the room opens the tile is empty, the same size, so nothing moves when the code arrives. In the crew lobby it's a third column beside PLAY, 18% of the stage's width (230 px at 1280x720, 5 px a module). On a phone held upright the stage is only 390 by 219, so the host screen stacks everything beside a tile two thirds of the stage's height (145 px), and the crew lobby drops the FAVORITE column to make room for a 105 px tile.
- **On `npm run dev` the link points at `localhost`,** which another phone can't open, so the code scans but goes nowhere. To try it on a real phone, serve the dev server through the dev tunnel (`vite.config.ts` allows `*.dataturd.com` hosts for that) and open the game at the tunnel's address: the link and its code are built from the page's own address.
- **Tested** (`npm run test:qr`, `scripts/qr/check.mjs`; 2026-11-10). The decoder is [jsQR](https://github.com/cozmo/jsQR), a dev dependency. In Node: a quick-room link, a crew link, the longest crew link, a localhost link and a tunnel link each read back exactly, and so does a text of every length from 1 to 213 bytes (all ten versions, at 3 and 4 pixels a module). With `--url` (after `npm run build` and `wrangler dev`) it opens the host screen and a crew lobby in headless Chromium at 1280x720, 844x390 and 390x844, and jsQR reads the code out of each whole screenshot: it has to equal the link on the screen, a quick-room link and a crew link at every size. It also checks that the empty tile and the drawn one are the same size in the same place, that no button moves when the room opens, that the tile covers no button or text, that everything is inside the stage, and that the arrow keys only land on buttons. 65 checks, all passing; `--out dir` keeps the screenshots and a contact sheet. Not scanned with a real phone camera: the 2 m and 40 cm distances are judged from the module sizes above, not measured.
- **The old lobby panel** in `src/net/online.js` (its own YOUR ROOM view with COPY LINK) has no code. Nothing in the game opens that view: the menus have their own host screen, and the panel is only used for its notices and the leave confirm, so its host view shows only if `net.openLobby('host')` is called by hand.

**Spectators** (watching a quick match live). Anyone can watch a quick room's match while it's being played: from the LIVE NOW strip on the homepage, from WATCH in the game's online menu, or from a watch link, `https://neonstickfight.com/play/?watch=K7QX` (the room's code). A viewer sees the normal arena and HUD about 2.5 seconds behind the fight, with no controls. **Crew matches can't be watched**, and the host of a quick room can turn spectating off.

- **Where the inputs come from.** A match is peer to peer, so the room never sees it. The host's page tells the room (`src/net/watchfeed.js`, over the room socket it already has): when a match starts, its setup (`{t:'watch', ev:'start', n, v, seed, chars, botch, rules, names, tally}`, `v` being `NET_VERSION`); the round and score when they change and once a minute (`ev:'score'`); and when it ends (`ev:'end'` with the last frame, or `ev:'over'` if it was dropped). While anyone is watching, it also sends the inputs both machines have **confirmed**: frames with both players' real inputs in and no correction pending (`netcode.confirmed`), which no rollback can change. They go out every 500 ms as one binary message (`src/net/watchlog.js`): 13 bytes, then 4 bytes a frame (both 11-bit input codes), then the host's state hash for every 30th frame, about 250 bytes a time. The host keeps the whole match's confirmed inputs itself (4 bytes a frame, 30 KB a minute), so nothing is sent while nobody watches, and a room that has nothing yet (the first spectator turning up mid-match, or a room that restarted) asks and gets the match from frame 0 in one go: the room's `{t:'sys', ev:'watching', n, have, need}` notice says how many are watching, which match it knows, and the next frame it wants.
- **The room** (`src/net/watchroom.js`, run by the `Room` Durable Object in `worker/index.ts`) reads only what the host addresses to it; everything else still passes between the players untouched. It keeps the current match's setup and input log in memory (nothing in storage: if it forgets, the host sends it again) and hands the log to spectator sockets, `GET /api/rooms/<code>/watch`. A spectator's socket is tagged `spectator`: nothing it sends is read or passed on, a `ping` is answered without waking the object, and one that sends more than 20 messages is closed. A log stops growing at 20 minutes (576 KB).
- **The delay.** Every batch carries the host's clock at its last frame, and the room releases only frames stamped at least 1.5 s before the newest stamp it has, so no page can show the fight closer than that: a viewer can't coach a player in real time. The hold is in the host's time rather than in frames because the sim runs at a quarter speed through a KO; a hold counted in frames ran viewers dry right before every finish. A batch can be empty (time passed, nothing new confirmed), so a stall on the host (WAITING FOR RIVAL) reads as the players waiting, not as a slow network. When the match ends, everything held goes out at once with the result.
- **The spectator** (`src/net/watch.js`) starts the same `online` scene the players run, from the setup's seed, fighters, botch switch and rules, with controllers that read the log, and steps it through `watch.advance(ms)`. It plays 0.9 s behind the newest frames it has (so about 2.5 s behind the fight with the room's hold and the trip), at the sim's own speed, leaning up to 50% faster or 15% slower to stay on the host's timeline. If it keeps running out of frames on a slow connection, the buffer grows by 250 ms a time, up to 2.4 s. More than 3 seconds of frames behind (joining mid-match, or back from a hidden tab), it stops drawing and runs the sim flat out with every effect muted until it's level: CATCHING UP, with a percentage. A dropped socket reconnects by itself (six tries, backing off) and carries on from the frame it was on.
- **The same build.** A spectator whose `NET_VERSION` differs from the match's never starts the sim: the screen says THIS MATCH IS ON A NEWER VERSION OF THE GAME (with RELOAD) or ON AN OLDER VERSION. With the same build, every 30th frame is hashed like the players' and compared with the host's hash from the batch. If the host's own sim was the one that drifted, its resync (the `{t:'sync'}` state it sends the guest) passes through the room, and spectators load it at the same frame; a difference nothing has put right in 3 seconds shows as OUT OF SYNC.
- **The LIVE list.** `GET /api/live` returns `{ live: [{ code, n, chars, names, round, wins, tally, watching, at }] }`: the room's code, the match number, the two fighters, the display names, the round, the round score, the room's tally, how many are watching and when it started; the most watched first, 40 at most. It's cached for 5 seconds at the edge (and 3 per isolate). Behind it is one Durable Object, `Lobby`: a room puts its match in when it starts, on every round, when the number watching changes (at most every 10 seconds, except the last viewer leaving) and once a minute, and takes it out when the match ends, a player leaves, or the host turns spectating off. An idle object is dropped from memory after about 10 seconds (a first version that kept the list in memory only lost it between visitors), so the list is kept in the Lobby's storage, cheaply: an entry is written when its match starts, every other minute of a long match, and deleted when it ends (three writes for a three-minute match). A change of round, score or count lands in memory, and an alarm 7 seconds later writes it only if the object has gone quiet by then, so a busy list writes nothing extra and a quiet one doesn't come back from a nap with an old count. An entry nobody has refreshed in 150 seconds is dropped, so a room that died quietly falls off.
- **What you see.** The homepage has a LIVE NOW strip above the books (`site/site.js`; hidden while nothing is on, asked again every 30 seconds while the page is in view): each match with the fighters in their colors, the names, the round, the score and the number watching. The game's online menu has WATCH, the same list (asked again every 6 seconds). The watch view (`src/flow/`, state `watching`) is the arena and HUD with the players' names under the bars, a WATCHING tag with both names and `N WATCHING`, COPY LINK and LEAVE. There's no pause, and no touch controls on a phone. When the match ends it shows the result (BOB WINS 2 – 1) and WAITING FOR A REMATCH; if the players rematch it moves on to the next match by itself, and if they leave it says so and goes back to the LIVE list after 6 seconds. Before a match (the players are still picking) it says NO MATCH ON YET over the demo.
- **The players' side.** The connection readout gains `WATCHING 3` while anyone is watching. The host's HOST A GAME screen has a switch, SPECTATORS: ON (the default) or OFF, kept in localStorage (`stick-fight-watch`) for their next rooms; `net.setWatchable(on)` is the same switch, and works mid-match. Off, the room sends its spectators away (THE HOST TURNED SPECTATORS OFF), refuses new ones, leaves the LIVE list, and the host's page sends it nothing.
- **Crew matches are never watchable,** in three places. The crew room claims its match rooms as private (`claimRoom(env, true)` in `worker/crew.ts`): a private room answers a watch socket with `no-room`, as if it weren't there, ignores every `watch` message and never reaches the Lobby. The host's page doesn't feed a crew match at all. And the crew room itself has no watch route and refuses a socket that asks for a spectator role (`no-spectators`).
- **Limits.** 50 spectators a room (the 51st gets FULL HOUSE). Watch joins count against the same 30 WebSocket joins a minute per IP as players' (`JOIN_LIMIT`). Because the LIVE list makes room codes public, a room with both seats taken no longer lets a second `host` socket take over the first (it answers `full`); a waiting host can still reconnect.
- **What it costs.** Cloudflare's list prices on the paid plan (2026-11): Durable Object requests $0.15 a million (an incoming WebSocket message counts as a twentieth of one, outgoing ones are free), duration $12.50 a million GB-seconds (only while a handler runs, with hibernating sockets), storage rows written $1 a million, Worker requests $0.30 a million. One watched match of 3 minutes with 5 spectators comes to about 34 Durable Object requests: 360 input batches (18), the start, score and end notes (under 1), 5 spectator sockets (5), and about 11 calls to the Lobby. It runs handlers for about half a second in all, writes 3 storage rows, and makes 5 Worker requests for the sockets.

  | Watched matches at once, all month, 5 spectators each | Durable Object requests | Duration | Lobby storage writes | Worker requests (sockets) | A month |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | 10 (144,000 matches a month) | 5 M: $0.75 | 8,000 GB-s: $0.10 | 0.4 M: $0.43 | 0.7 M: $0.22 | about $1.50, and inside the plan's included usage in practice |
  | 1,000 (14.4 M matches a month) | 495 M: $74 | 780,000 GB-s: $10 | 43 M: $43 (inside the 50 M included) | 72 M: $22 | $105 to $150 |

  For scale, the players' own hash checks through the room (8 messages a second a match, there before this) already cost about $155 a month at 1,000 matches at once, so being watched adds less than a match costs to begin with; a match nobody watches adds only its start, score and end notes and the Lobby calls (about 9 requests). Three things were chosen for cost. The host uploads only while someone watches, and keeps the log itself, so the room stores nothing. The list is one Durable Object with two or three storage writes a match, rather than a write for every change (a D1 table with a heartbeat would be a write a minute per match, and a row read per match on every uncached `api/live`). And the list is cached, so however many pages poll it, the Lobby is asked once every 5 seconds per Cloudflare location. **The one cost that grows with visitors rather than matches is the polling itself:** every open homepage asks `api/live` every 30 seconds while it's in view, and each ask is a Worker request, so 1,000 homepages open around the clock would be 86 M requests, $26 a month. If that ever matters, the strip can ask less often, or only once it scrolls into view.
- **On the host.** The upload doesn't touch the sim or the netcode's timing, so there's no input delay to add. Each frame it copies the newly confirmed inputs into its log, and twice a second it sends one message of about 270 bytes (540 bytes a second up). Measured in Node on an M3 Pro over five minutes at 60 fps: 1.5 µs a frame on average, 31 µs at the 99th percentile (the frames that send). In headless Chromium during the browser test below, with its own timer around the work: 8.0 µs a frame over 5,258 frames. A phone six times slower is still under 0.05 ms on an ordinary frame and about 0.2 ms twice a second, against a 16.7 ms frame. The one heavier moment is the first spectator arriving mid-match: the whole log goes at once (a 5-minute match is 20 messages, 154 KB, built in 3.3 ms on the Mac).
- **Not built:** the guest taking over the upload if the host's stops. A match doesn't outlive the host's room socket (the guest's ends as YOUR RIVAL LEFT the moment it closes), so there's no state in which the guest could carry on feeding spectators.
- **`NET_VERSION` stays at 22.** Nothing between the two players changed, and the sim didn't: the new messages are between the host and the room, and an older guest ignores the room's `watching` notice. A spectator needs this build or later, and the version check covers the rest.
- **Proof** (`npm run netsim:watch`, `scripts/netsim/watch.mjs`, in Node). Two phones play real matches through the real session and rollback netcode over the bad network of the netsim harness, with the real host feed, the room's own code (`watchroom.js`, the module the Worker runs) and real spectators (`watch.js`), each on its own sim, arena and demo. It compares every 30th frame's state hash on each spectator with the host's confirmed hash of that frame, and each match's final state and result with the players'. Measured 2026-11-10, 3 seeds a scenario, 2 matches a session (30-second rounds, best of 3): 27 sessions, 42 matches, **24,899 spectator hashes compared with the host's and 28,022 checked by the spectators themselves, none different; 84 final states, none different**. Playback ran 2.30 s behind the host's own frame on average.

  | Scenario | What it covers | Result |
  |---|---|---|
  | `first-frame` (80 ms, 5% loss) | two spectators in before the match, one at 30 fps | 3,862 hashes, all the same; never out of frames |
  | `late-joiners` (120 ms, 8%) | joining 9, 31 and 55 seconds in, at 60 fps, uneven frames and 120 fps | 6,567 the same; 10 catch-ups; out of frames 13 times in 9.5 minutes of watching each |
  | `come-and-go` (150 ms, 8%) | one leaves and comes back, one's socket dies silently, one's tab is hidden for 9 s | 5,805 the same; all three picked the match back up |
  | `cell-400ms` (400 ms, 15%) | a network where the players themselves keep stalling | 3,870 the same; the playback pauses with the players |
  | `guest-knocked` | the guest's sim nudged off every 12 s and at each result (the host resyncs it) | 3,870 the same: a spectator has the host's fight throughout |
  | `host-knocked` | the host's own sim nudged off | 3,426 of the spectators' own checks, all good once the host's resync landed: 75 resyncs sent, 71 loaded by the spectators (one who joined later needs only the newest) |
  | `turned-off` | the host turns spectating off 16 s in | both spectators sent away with `off`, a new one refused, the match off the LIVE list, the players' match finished |
  | `host-leaves` | the host leaves 18 s in | the guest ends as YOUR RIVAL LEFT and both spectators see the room close |
  | `crew-match` | the same session as a crew match | the host's page sends the room nothing, both spectators are refused with `no-room`, nothing is listed |

  It found three bugs on the way that a hand test would have missed: fighter ids with a capital letter (`windowWasher`) turned into TEMP in the room's copy of the setup, so every hash differed; the host counted frames as sent that it hadn't stamped yet, so the room kept asking for small gaps and a match's last half second never arrived; and a spectator briefly disagreed with a host whose own sim had drifted, until the resync reached it.
- **In browsers** (`npm run test:watch`, `scripts/watch/e2e.mjs`; 2026-11-10, `npm run build`, `wrangler dev`, each page its own headless Chromium, scripted inputs, 30-second single-round matches). 67 checks, all passing: a spectator in before the first frame and one mid-match (caught up 905 frames undrawn in 70 ms); the LIVE list's fields in `api/live`, in the WATCH menu and on the homepage; the players' WATCHING 1 and 2; a reload, and a socket closed under a spectator (it reconnects and carries on from its frame); the result card, the list emptying at the end of a match and the rematch arriving on its own; **every confirmed hash frame the host and a spectator both had matched (48, 26, 46 and 63 frames on four spectators), plus 408 of the spectators' own checks**; the off switch mid-match (both sent away, a new one refused, off the list, the players unaffected) and back on; the host closing its tab mid-match (the guest's match ends, the spectator sees THE PLAYERS LEFT THE ROOM); through the menus, the switch on the HOST A GAME screen (off before the match refuses a spectator), a spectator waiting through the pick screen, INTERN v WINDOW WASHER with the players' names, a phone-sized touch page (no controls, the tag and LEAVE on screen), picking a match from the list and LEAVE; a spectator's keys and messages changing nothing for the players, and one that keeps sending being cut off; a crew room refusing the spectator role, having no watch route, its match room answering `no-room`, and a crew match never reaching `api/live` even when the host's page asks; and the 31st join in a minute answered `busy`. On a busy machine headless Chromium's software GL starves the frame loop, so the test pumps each page's own `advance()` from a timer and draws only now and then; the sockets, netcode, room and menus are the real ones. Not tested on a real phone.

**Crew rooms** (bookmarkable rooms for a group of friends). A quick room is a one-off four-letter code; a crew room is a permanent link the crew keeps, `https://neonstickfight.com/crew/tuesday-crew-7k2qm` (2026-11-11; the older `play/?crew=` and `/?crew=` links get a 301 to it, with any other parameter kept, and so do the same links on the old address, `askscottpierce.com/stick-fight`). Shared in a chat it unfurls as that crew: "Join TUESDAY CREW on Neon Stick Fight", the member count, and a picture with the crew's name and TAP TO JOIN (Site, Crew and room links unfurl as that room): the crew's name in lower case with dashes, then five random characters (no 0/o, 1/i/l), so it reads well and can't be guessed (28 million per name, behind the join limit). It's a path, served by the Worker as the game page with a `<base>` (Site, Crew and room links unfurl as that room); the game reads the slug from the path, or from `?crew=` on the dev server or a page that never went through the Worker (`linkFromUrl()` in `src/links.js`). While you're in the lobby the address bar holds that clean link (`history.replaceState`), whether you came by the link or from CREW ROOMS in the menus, so a bookmark taken there works; leaving the crew for the menus puts it back to `play/`, so a reload doesn't pull you back in. A quick room works the same way: `room/<CODE>` stays in the bar while you're in the room and goes back to `play/` when the room session ends.

- **Making and joining one.** CREATE CREW ROOM asks for a crew name (3 to 20 letters, digits and spaces) and `POST /api/crews` makes the room. JOIN A CREW takes the code (the part after `crew=`) typed, or the whole link pasted. Opening the link does the same. Every crew you open goes to the top of your crew list (the profile's `crews`), and the online menu shows the three most recent.
- **The lobby** (`crewLobby`): the crew's name and link, COPY LINK and SHARE (the phone's share sheet where there is one), the link's QR code (Scan to join, above), the standings (every member who has opened the link, most wins first: wins, losses, draws, your record against each of them, and each one's favorite fighter in its neon), the last three results, a status line, and PLAY (focused), ONLINE MENU and MAIN MENU (BACK goes to MAIN MENU, 2026-11-11). A square dot before each name is presence over the room's WebSocket: cyan HERE, amber READY (with a chip), pink PLAYING, hollow for a member who isn't here.
- **The lobby held upright** (2026-11-11, [twgdsx], then [twmcsg]; Arcade flow, Menus, "Held upright: every screen takes the whole phone"). Most people see it right after scanning the QR code or tapping a link in a chat, so it's for one thing: see who's here and play. Top to bottom: the crew's name; **YOU'RE <name>** with **EDIT** (or **SET YOUR NAME** when the link handed the name out), which opens the name screen and comes back to this lobby under the new name; the status line; PLAY (60 px or more) where a thumb is; then every member (up to the 50 a crew holds) under the STANDINGS, ARCADE, HORDE and STATS tabs, in a list that scrolls under its header and takes whatever height is left (495 of 844 px at 390x844, 13 rows); and **INVITE** and **MAIN MENU** across the bottom. MAIN MENU is the only way out; the online menu is one step from there. The link, its QR code (150 to 300 px, as big as the screen leaves; 281 px at 390x844), COPY LINK and SHARE wait behind INVITE (with a share mark), which puts them where the list was, so sharing is something you choose to do. Its button says DONE while they're up, and BACK closes them first. Upright the standings are just rank, the name with its dot and READY or PLAYING chip, W and L: draws, VS YOU and FAVORITE give the name their room (STATS has the history), so someone who just scanned in reads names and who's ready, not a grid of zeros. Your own row is pink, name included, and doesn't say (YOU) upright (YOU'RE <name> above says it). On every layout (YOU) also goes once your READY or PLAYING chip shows, so sideways and on a desktop "SCOTT READY" shows whole rather than "SC… (YOU) READY" (on main it read "SCOTT (YOU) REA"). Without a chip, (YOU) is what gets cut when the row is tight, never your name: sideways your idle row reads "GUEST 736 (Y…" with the digits that tell guests apart whole (on main it read "GUEST 736 (YOU"; round 3 of this task briefly cut the name instead), and `test:join` fails your idle GUEST row at 1280x720 and 844x390 if its name doesn't show whole and pink. A long name ends in an ellipsis and the chip after it always shows whole (the name is its own `.fl-snm` span; before, the chip was cut to a 6 px sliver at 390x844, and `test:join` and `menus.mjs` now fail a chip that runs past its row, and `test:join` fails your row's name if it isn't pink or doesn't show whole beside READY at 1280x720 and 844x390). The bookmark line and the last three matches aren't shown upright (the address bar keeps the clean link, and STATS has the crew's history). The stage layout's columns step aside (`display: contents`) so one grid places it all, and the focus still moves by where things are. Under 660 px tall PLAY drops to 54 px. CREATE and JOIN A CREW are the typing screens (above); CREW MATCH and NO CREW AT THAT LINK are the plain full-screen layout. Before [twmcsg] the upright lobby also showed the bookmark line, the last matches, the QR code beside the link, and ONLINE MENU and MAIN MENU, which left the list about a quarter of the screen.
- **Playing.** PLAY means "I'll fight whoever else is ready" (it turns into CANCEL while you wait; a press while still connecting counts).
