**The target, said plainly: not reached on this stand-in.** At 6x plus SwiftShader, AUTO settles on tier 0 and holds about 33 to 45 fps in close and the attract demo (target 55) and 31 to 42 in horde (target 45), depending on how busy the shared machine was; at 1080p and DPR 1.25, 24 to 35. That's 4 to 10 times the before, but not 55. With a fast CPU (1x) and software GL, tier 0 holds 56 in close and 49 in horde. What's left at 6x is the main thread, about 15 ms a frame, most of it the CPU raster of the layers and their upload (`texImage2D`, 37% of the main thread's profile), with SwiftShader's composite on top. For scale, the harness's floor: this page with no GL work at all runs about 54 fps at 6x on SwiftShader. At 10x tier 0 is about 25-33 fps, and at 20x it's down to 4-14: a machine that slow gets a playable but choppy game, since the sim keeps time down to 10 fps. The next steps, worth taking with a real Chromebook's `?perf=1` numbers in hand: upload only the part of the figures layer the fighters cover, a quarter-size emissive layer at tier 0 (2-3 ms of main thread at 6x, measured), and the HUD canvas at 1x on the 1080p panel. **Not measured on a quiet machine: the title screen with its menus.** The harness's attract row is `?scene=attract`, the demo without the DOM menus over it; one check of the real title at a load average of 58 ran about half the bare demo's rate under the same load (6 fps against 11), so the menus' own compositing may cost something on software GL. `?perf=1` on the title on a real Chromebook will say.

### The character select

Moving the cursor felt late, and the cause was the frame rate, not the input: the select screen ran at about 16 fps on a desktop. Every rig on it (18 cards and the sheet's big one) was drawn with a live `shadowBlur`, and a canvas shadow is blurred per draw call, so every limb, joint and prop got its own blur: about thirty a rig, nineteen rigs a frame. The game's own JavaScript took 3 ms a frame; Chrome's GPU process then spent 37 ms of CPU a frame on the blurs. A press was handled at once and then waited for a 50 to 60 ms frame, the highlight eased in over another 90 ms, and on touch the focus only moved on `click`, a tap's length after the finger landed.

What changed (`src/flow/select.js`, `ui.js`, `menuInput.js`):

- **One blur a rig.** The rig is drawn plain into a scratch canvas, and that canvas is stamped once with the shadow on (the same 9 px blur in the fighter's color) into a glow layer. A frame is the floor glow, the glow layer (two coats, to match the weight thirty overlapping shadows had) and the rig on top. The sheet's rig and the card under the cursor remake their glow every frame. The other cards only breathe, so their glow is remade every 0.1 s and they redraw 30 times a second, both in turns, so a frame draws a handful of rigs.
- **The sheet changes in place.** The stat and move rows are built once and rewritten, each fighter's big rig is built the first time the cursor lands on them and kept (it was a new `createFighter` and `setBody` per move), and the sheet's canvas is drawn inside the focus change, so the highlight and the sheet are always the same frame (a pad's poll can run after the game's frame).
- **No ease in.** A card or button taking the focus has no transition; the one losing it keeps its fade.
- **Touch focuses on `pointerdown`.** The first tap still only focuses and the second still picks.
- **Pad repeat** 380 / 110 ms is now 250 / 80 ms.

Measured with `node scripts/perf/select.mjs` (headless Chromium, Apple M3 Pro, 1440x810 at DPR 2, a 120 Hz frame clock; before is `main` at d75b305). The machine was busy with other test runs the whole time, so the first table is CPU time, which doesn't care, and the wall-clock table is from the quietest run (load average 17 on 12 cores) with no throttle. Read its numbers as upper bounds.

| CPU time per select-screen frame | before | after | the mode select, for scale |
|---|---|---|---|
| GPU process | 36.7 ms | 5.2 ms | 6.3 ms |
| Renderer process (all threads) | 3.6 ms | 2.2 ms | 3.1 ms |
| Frames a second it managed under that load | 11 | 105 | - |

| Wall clock, no throttle, p50 / p95 | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| Idle frame interval | 58.5 / 84 ms (16 fps) | 8.3 / 9.2 ms (120 fps) |
| Idle main-thread work a frame (of it, the select's tick) | 3.0 (2.0) ms | 1.6 (0.7) ms |
| Long frames a minute (over 25 ms) | 944 | 0 |
| Keyboard: press to the sheet drawn | 50.7 / 116 ms | 4.9 / 9.0 ms |
| Gamepad: press to the sheet drawn | 40.8 / 98 ms | 6.5 / 9.1 ms |
| Mouse: hover to the sheet drawn | 54.3 / 206 ms | 11.2 / 69 ms |
| Touch: finger down to the sheet drawn | 496 / 598 ms | 31 / 91 ms (22 of it before any page code runs) |
| Keyboard: press to the card at full size | 245 / 335 ms | 7.7 / 11.9 ms |
| Keyboard: Chrome's Event Timing, press to next paint | 384 / 488 ms | 40 / 56 ms |

Every keyboard, pad and mouse press was drawn in the first animation frame after it, before and after; the difference is how long that frame takes. Event Timing reads 32 ms (p50) for the same key on the mode select, a plain menu of six buttons, so about 32 ms is this headless browser's floor for "press to paint" and the select screen is now within a frame of it.

**At 4x CPU throttle the numbers here are not good enough to quote as a result.** The throttled runs were taken at load averages of 17 to 68 and swing by 5x from run to run. What holds: DevTools throttling slows the page's main thread only, and that was never the bottleneck. The 37 ms a frame was in the GPU process, which a slow phone has less of, not more. The main thread's share is 1.6 ms a frame unthrottled, so about 6.4 ms at 4x (it was 3.0, about 12 ms), under the 16.7 ms budget with the demo fight still running behind the menu. The one throttled run at load 17 had keyboard presses drawn in the first frame, 19.6 ms p50 after the press (14.7 ms of that waiting for the frame in progress to finish). Run the harness on a quiet machine before trusting a 4x number.

The move list's rig (`movelist.js`) still uses a live `shadowBlur`: it's one rig, not nineteen. The demo fight still runs behind the menu at full cost, which is the same 5 to 6 ms of GPU-process time a frame every menu pays.

### The tools

All in `scripts/perf/`, against `npm run build`'s `dist/`; results in `scripts/perf/results/`.
- `run.mjs`: the frame table (`--throttle 1,4,6 --tier 3,2,1,0|auto --scenes close,horde,rain,floor,attract --secs 8`, `--device pixel7|pixel7up|desktop|cros|croshd`; the table's `drawn` is pictures a second, lower than `fps` only when tier 0 draws every other frame), `--gpu` (`--angle swiftshader`, `--off bloom,up:figures,fn:arena.drawEmissive,win:<flag>` to drop a pass, an upload, a drawing function (a `window.__game` path made a no-op) or set a `window` flag), `--eval <js>` (run once the game is up) and `--init <js>` (before the page's scripts) for experiments, `--rollback`, `--load` (`--net`), `--dist <dir>` for another build, `--json <file>`.
- `table.mjs`: the before/after tables above from the results.
- `simbench.mjs`: the sim's step, save, load, hash and burst in Node.
- `profile.mjs`: a CPU profile of a scenario (`--load` for the page load, `--alloc` for allocations by function; `--angle swiftshader --device cros` for the Chromebook stand-in).
- `trace.mjs`: main-thread timeline events by total time (how the HUD flush was found); `--all` adds every thread, the GPU process's included (how the software-GL canvas raster was found), and it takes `--angle` and `--device cros` too.
- `select.mjs`: the character select's idle frame and press to paint by keyboard, a fake gamepad, touch and mouse (`--throttle 1,4 --device desktop|pixel7 --n 30 --online --shot file.png`), with a control row from the mode select.
- `compare.mjs`: the seeded before/after screenshots. `tiers.mjs`: tiers 3, 2, 1 and 0 in a two-by-two grid.
- `device.mjs` (`npm run test:device`): the capability check's buckets and start tiers in Node, then the built game on SwiftShader with a Chromebook's user agent: it starts at tier 0, and the `perf` event it really sends carries the buckets and no part of the renderer string.

## Layout

- `play/index.html`, `src/main.js` — the game. Wires the modules below.
- `index.html` (the homepage), `site/` (the fighter pages' generator, templates, styles and script) — the site around the game (see Site).
- `src/root.js` — the app's root URL for any page depth (the API, `sw.js`, `version.json`, `vendor/`), and the `<base>` it pins so `history.replaceState` never moves relative URLs.
- `src/links.js` — crew and room links: `crew/<slug>` and `room/<CODE>` off the root, reading them from the address (path first, then the old query), and moving the address bar between them and `play/`.
- `src/perf.js` — the frame profiler and the `?perf=1` overlay; `src/arena/cache.js` — the scenery's world bitmaps; `scripts/perf/` — the performance harness (see Performance). `src/device.js` — the capability check's coarse buckets, the start tier and the `perf` event's labels; `src/gfxPrefs.js` — the GRAPHICS choice and the tier this device settled on; `src/softcanvas.js` — on a software GL, every 2D canvas on the CPU (Performance, Chromebooks).
- `src/fighter/` — stick figure rig, poses, animation. Draws to a 2D context.
- `src/fx/` — lighting and effects compositor (WebGL), particles, camera shake.
- `src/combat/` — input, state machine, frame data (heights, stamina costs, enders), the string table (`strings.js`), hitboxes, AI, the neon HUD, rounds.
- `src/characters/` — the roster (see Characters): one file per character, `registry.js` (merging each one into the shared tables), `index.js` (registering them all).
- `src/flow/` — the arcade flow (see Arcade flow): `index.js` the state machine and its screens, `ui.js` the DOM overlay (neon, hard corners, scales with the stage), `select.js` the character select, `menuInput.js` keys and pad for menus, `controls.js` the one control table, the device in use and the footer legend, `ladder.js` the six stages and `buildLadder`, `scores.js` score rules and the high score tables, `scorecap.js` the most a run can score for how far it got (shared with the Worker), `netApi.js` the seam to `src/net/online.js`, `techniques.js` the happy accidents found and the botch switch (localStorage).
- `src/combat/botch.js` — botches and happy accidents: the family picker, the stamina cost, the accident windows, the flail's and the eight techniques' clips and frame data (see Botches and happy accidents).
- `src/net/` — online play (see Multiplayer): `online.js` session, readout and lobby; `watchfeed.js` the host's spectator feed, `watchroom.js` the room's side of it (shared with the Worker), `watchlog.js` the input log and its wire format, `watch.js` the spectator (see Spectators); `crew.js` the crew lobby connection; `crewScores.js` the crews' high score tables, which runs post where; `profile.js` the player id, name and sign-in; `link.js` WebSocket and WebRTC; `rollback.js` netcode and resync; `codec.js` wire format; `hash.js` state hash; `state.js` the sim's state as text for a resync. `scripts/netsim/` is the two-phone harness and the save, restore and resync proof (`npm run netsim`, `npm run netsim:proof`), plus the spectator proof (`npm run netsim:watch`); `scripts/watch/e2e.mjs` is the spectator test in real browsers. The sim's save and restore for rollback is `src/combat/snapshot.js` (plus `getState()`/`setState()` on the rig in `src/fighter/index.js`).
- `src/bookkeeping.js` — match bookkeeping on the page: what one match's result carries, and when it's sent (Analytics, Match bookkeeping). `site/stats.js` draws the numbers on the homepage and the fighter pages; `scripts/seed-stats.mjs` writes made-up numbers for local dev.
- `worker/` — the Worker: routes, ICE, the quick room Durable Object and the `Lobby` object behind the LIVE list (`index.ts`), the crew room Durable Object (`crew.ts`), profiles and email sign-in (`auth.ts`), the match bookkeeping, its rollups and `api/stats` (`stats.ts`, with the fighter ids it accepts in `roster.ts`), shared helpers and the bindings (`env.ts`), with its own `tsconfig.json`. `migrations/` is the D1 schema.
- `src/combat/dmath.js` — bit-identical trig for the sim and the rig on every browser engine (see Multiplayer, Determinism).
- `src/touch.js` — mobile touch controls (synthetic key events, active only on coarse pointers).
- `src/homescreen.js` — iPhone and iPad Safari only: the PLAY FULL SCREEN callout and the Add to Home Screen how-to that the touch layer's fullscreen pill and the title's INSTALL open (Install as an app).
- `src/fullscreen.js` — the one fullscreen toggle: F11, the desktop corner button, and the touch layer's button (Controls, Fullscreen).
- `src/qr.js` — the QR code encoder for the join links (Multiplayer, Scan to join); `scripts/qr/check.mjs` reads its codes back with jsQR, alone and off screenshots of the host screen and crew lobby (`npm run test:qr`).
- `src/corner.js` — the corner buttons (pause, menu, fullscreen): the pill's size, the icons and the desktop row, shared by the desktop buttons and the touch layer's (Controls, The corner buttons).
- `scripts/shots/portrait.mjs` — a phone held upright (Controls, Held upright: the view): seeded fight shots and the menus at 390x844, 412x915, 375x667, 395x700 and 412x640 (the last two a phone with its browser bars showing), with 844x390 and 1280x720 beside them; `--check` measures the layout and fails when the view is narrower than the screen, a control is cut off, the band or the controls touch the view, or a fighter is outside it; `--sheet before after out` makes the before and after contact sheets. `scripts/shots/square-fit.mjs` checks every frame of 16 scenes at the five upright sizes for a joint outside the view (`--scenes a,b` and `--p1 <id>` for one fighter), and `scripts/shots/camtrace.mjs <other dist>` compares the camera frame by frame between two builds at the sizes that mustn't change. `npm run test:square` runs the first two.
- `scripts/select/check.mjs` — the character select's ring: the upright carousel by touch and the grid's wrap by keys (`npm run test:select`, `--online <worker play/ URL>` for the pick screen in a room).
- `scripts/shots/perfect-sheet.mjs` — a contact sheet of the perfect finishers from `?scene=perfect-test&p1=<id>` (needs `npm run build`): six moments per fighter, three through the windup from the grab, the release, and two early in the flight, cropped around the thrower. `[--out dir] [--size 390x844] [--only chef,janitor] [--video ids] [--keep]`; run it in a worktree of the build before for the before sheet.
- `scripts/shots/menus.mjs` — every screen of the flow, crew and online ones first, at desktop (1280x720), two phones sideways (844x390, 667x375) and five upright (390x844, 412x915, 375x667, 395x700, 412x640) (Arcade flow, Menus, "Held upright: every screen takes the whole phone"). It needs the game with `./api` reaching a Worker: `npm run dev:worker` and `npx vite --port 8191` (`SF_WORKER=http://127.0.0.1:<port>` points Vite's proxy at a Worker on another port), then `node scripts/shots/menus.mjs [--out <dir>] [--check] [--only upright|sideways|desktop|390x844,...] [--states crewLobby,...|local] [--no-net] [--dump layout.json]`. It makes a crew room through the API, fills it with twelve members on sockets from Node, and plays a real room against a second browser. It reads the QR codes back from the screenshot with jsQR (a dev dependency) and fails one under 140 px. `--check` exits 1 on any problem (and takes pictures only with `--out`). `--dump` writes every element's box and font size on every screen, for diffing two builds: sideways and desktop must not move.
- `src/plaintext.js` — `PLAIN`, the dark outline every DOM stylesheet puts on text that's read (Controls, The look).
- `src/arena/` — the stage: geometry (floors, the two flights and their stairwells, doors, spawns), background art, static lights.
- `src/combat/stairNav.js` — the CPU taking the stairs to an opponent on another level.
- `src/audio/` — sound effects and music (see Sound). `main.js` makes two calls into it: `gameAudio.attach(sim)` and `gameAudio.event(ev)` from its event router. During the attract demo (and the menus over it) it holds the events back and attaches `null` instead of the sim (so the whiff watcher is quiet too), then attaches the sim again when a game starts.
- `demo/*.html` — one page per module so each can be judged alone.
- `window.__game.advance(ms)` steps the game without drawing a frame (fast, for counting events over a long demo); `window.__gauntlet.step(ms)` steps and draws.
- `src/fighter/ground.js` — bodies on top of the floor: each joint's drawn radius and `fitToGround()`, shared by the sim and the drawing (Floors, Bodies on the floor). `scripts/floorlog/` measures it (`run.mjs`, `probe.js`) and draws frame strips (`strip.mjs`); `window.__game.stepPosed(ms, fn)` steps a frame and poses it exactly as `render()` does, without painting.
- `src/fighter/life.js` — the life layer (below): eyeline, springs and cloth, fatigue, takes, per-character signatures and idle habits, drawn only.
- `gauntlet/` — the quality loop: the brief (with the owner requirements), the capture tool and the workbench log. `counters.mjs` measures what beats each fighter's unique moves, for the fighter pages' STRATEGY tips (Site, STRATEGY). The reference images and per-round critic captures stay out of the public repo (see `.gitignore`); the reference images belong to their owners.

### The life layer

What a puppeteer adds on top of the keyframes (`src/fighter/life.js`). The clips carry the acting; this layer makes the figure look like it's watching, carrying weight, getting tired, reacting to the other fighter and fidgeting like itself.

**Drawn only, always.** The rig's joints drive the hitboxes, and online play runs the rig on both machines and saves and hashes it for rollback. The life layer never touches any of that: `render()` in `src/main.js` takes the joints `frameDraw()` produced for this frame (the turnaround and snap fade already applied), and `life.apply(u, joints, sim, dt, floor)` returns a moved copy that only the drawing uses, the same way the draw smoothing works. `floor` is the floor guard (Floors, Bodies on the floor): the springs, takes and hit wave can push a joint a few px past where the clip put it, so the copy is fitted back on top of the floor before the cloth hangs off it. Its springs live in the layer, keyed by unit, and step once per rendered frame on real time, so a rollback re-running sim steps never runs them twice; they reset with the draw smoothing on a scene change and a new round, and stand still whenever the sim does (paused, a menu over the fight) and in a hit's freeze, so a contact frame still holds. The cloth's chains reach the rig's `draw()` as `rig.cloth` (one array of points per item), set before the draw and cleared after; it isn't part of `getState()`. `?life=0` turns the layer off for an A/B, and `window.__game.life.on` does the same live (`window.__game.life.extras = false` keeps the eyeline, springs and fatigue and drops the takes, signatures and habits).

- **Eyeline.** The head pitches toward what the fighter is watching: the other fighter's chest by default, the striking limb while a strike winds up at him (FRAME_DATA `joint`), the hip of a body on the floor, the loser after a KO. A critically damped turn of about 0.1 s, at most 12 degrees up and 15 down, and weighted by state (full in idle, walk, crouch and guard, about half in an attack, none launched, thrown or knocked down), so the clips' own head keys run the big moves. Think, then move: as his own strike starts (or a step), the head nods 7 degrees toward the target and leads by 2 px, gone in about 0.1 s.
- **Springs.** The head lags the neck on a loose spring (3.2 Hz, at most 3.5 px), the hands trail a little outside a strike (6 Hz, 2.5 px), and a hit sends a wave up the body after the freeze: the hips first, the chest 30 ms later, the shoulders and arms at 55 to 75, the head at 90, each a 150 ms push away from the attacker of 3.5 to 8 px by the damage. The feet never move.
- **Cloth.** Everything that hangs off a fighter is a short chain (`cloth` in the character's file, `src/fighter/cloth.js`). Each is 1 to 5 verlet points anchored on a joint and stepped at a fixed 240 Hz on real frame time: pulled toward the item's rest shape (the first link stiffest and the last at 55% of it, so the end lags the middle and a tie flips, then settles), damped against the anchor's own motion so a steady walk leaves it hanging, with a little air drag so a run streams it back, and gravity. Then follow-the-leader constraints hold every segment to its length (nothing stretches), no segment swings more than its `limit` off its rest direction, a `side` item stays clear of the body's line, so a tie never goes through the chest and a ponytail never through the head, and every point keeps its drawn thickness above the floor (`thick`, and `endR` for a badge or a cup on the end), so on a body lying down it lies along the floor and slides instead of hanging through it. The facing eases across a turn in 70 ms, so a turn swings a chain across the body instead of mirroring it. A hit flings it away from the attacker with a strength of 4 to 14 by the damage (the free end gets 38 px/s across and 14 px/s up per point of it), 1.7 times on a launch or knockdown and 2.2 times on the KO; it lands as the hit's freeze ends, and the freeze holds the chain like everything else. Launches, throws, falls and spins move the anchors fast, so they fling things on their own. Idle breathing barely moves them. A chain that jumps more than 120 px in a frame (a respawn, a reset) starts again at rest. Without the life layer (`?life=0`, the character select, the move list, the fighter pages' move cards, the build's roster sprites) the rig draws the rest shape.

| Character | What swings | Chain |
|---|---|---|
| TEMP | the lanyard; the visitor badge flips on its end (the horde's goons wear it too) | collar, 3 links, 24 px, 2.2 Hz, front |
| INTERN | the lanyard, then the badge swinging on its clip | collar, 3 links (8, 8 and a 2.5 px clip), 2.4 Hz, front |
| THE MANAGER | his necktie (the knot stays put, the blade swings) | knot, 3 links, 20.5 px, 1.8 Hz, front |
| SALES REP | the skinny tie | knot, 3 links, 21 px, 2.1 Hz, front |
| HR REP | the lanyard's clip and the ID card under it | collar, 2 links (12 and 8.6), 2.2 Hz, front |
| BARISTA | the apron's skirt under the waist tie, the pocket's cup riding on it; the tie's tail behind | waist, 2 links, 11 px, 2.6 Hz, front; tail 2 links, 8 px, back layer |
| CHEF | the apron's skirt under the waist tie; the bow's tail behind | waist, 3 links, 30 px, 2.2 Hz, damping 0.45, front; tail 2 links, back layer |
| WELLNESS COACH | a ponytail under the headband's knot (new), and the headband's two tails | knot, 4 links, 27 px, 1.7 Hz, behind the head; tails 2 links each, 16.6 px, back layer |
| ELECTRICIAN | the voltage tester on its lead off the back of the belt | belt, 2 links, 13 px |
| WINDOW WASHER | the safety line off the D-ring, the carabiner on its end | belt, 3 links, 12.5 px |
| LAWYER | the briefcase, swinging on its handle round where it used to hang | the hand, 1 link, 18 px, 3.2 Hz |
| RECEPTIONIST | the phone's coiled cord from the base at the hip to the hand, sagging and swinging between them, pulled straight when the hand reaches past it | pinned at both ends, 4 links, 40 px |

Cost and checks (2026-11-09): `life.apply` with the cloth costs 3 to 26 µs per fighter per frame in Node across the 12 characters that wear some (WELLNESS COACH's three chains the most), against about 3 µs before. In `horde-ai` at a 4x CPU throttle a stepped and drawn frame took 163.5 ms with the layer on and 162.4 ms with `?life=0` in headless Chromium's software WebGL, so the compositor dominates and the cloth is noise. The sim hashed every 100 steps (every unit's position, health, state, stamina and rig joints) matched with the layer on and with `?life=0` in ai-fight, seed 42, THE MANAGER v WELLNESS COACH, CHEF v RECEPTIONIST and TEMP v BARISTA. A scripted run of every character through a walk, run, turn, spin kick, jump and heavy hit gave no NaN and no stretched segment.

What stays rigid, on purpose: hats and glasses (COURIER's cap, JANITOR's bucket hat, ELECTRICIAN's hard hat, CHEF's toque, ACCOUNTANT's and LAWYER's glasses, SECURITY's shades), headsets (HELPDESK, RECEPTIONIST), SECURITY's earpiece wire (clipped down the neck), THE CEO's tie (tucked into his jacket at the V) and watch, the aprons' bibs above the waist tie, WINDOW WASHER's harness straps and holstered squeegee, ELECTRICIAN's pouch, WELLNESS COACH's mat (strapped to the back), and anything held in a hand (HR REP's clipboard, SALES REP's phone, the RECEPTIONIST's handset, BARISTA's cup at the mouth).

- **Fatigue.** Under 40% stamina (all of it by 10%) the shoulders, arms and head heave with the breath, 1.6 breaths a second rising to 3.2, up to 4.5 px; the shoulders round forward 2 px, the guard's hands sag 9 px toward the belt (the elbows 4), and the head hangs 3.5 px. It eases in over a quarter second and out over 0.6 as the bar refills, only in idle, walk, crouch and guard; a faint keeps its own dizzy clip.

- **Takes.** Reactions to the other fighter, the way a Muppet's best moments are its reactions. The sim emits nothing new for them: the layer compares unit fields frame to frame (`botchAt`, `faintAt`, a strike going into `hitstun` with no damage taken, `sim.phase === 'ko'` with `sim.winner`). Each is a short keyed curve (smoothstep between keys, so every key is a still point and nothing steps) on the head, torso and arms, eased in and out by a weight, and plays only over idle, walk, crouch and guard: if the fighter starts a strike, gets hit or knocked down mid-take, the weight eases out in about 70 ms. One take at a time, and a new one starts at least 0.6 s after the last began.

| Take | When | What it looks like |
|---|---|---|
| double take | the other fighter botches | a beat, the head snaps toward them, back, a second smaller look (0.9 s); with `laugh`, a little shoulder shake of a laugh |
| watching a faint | the other fighter faints | leans back, the guard drops 6 px, the head tilts, held while they're out |
| flinch | this fighter's strike is parried | "oh!": the head pulls back, the shoulders come up, at 60% over the reel clip (0.5 s) |
| settle | this fighter lands a parry | a small settle into the legs and a nod (0.55 s) |
| KO hold | this fighter wins the round | once standing, a held silhouette for up to 0.8 s (the kabuki *mie*): stand tall, chin up, guard lowered; THE MANAGER straightens his tie, the TEMP slumps, exhausted and relieved |

- **Signatures.** A character's `life` entry (in its file, next to `cloth`) tunes how it moves, so nobody moves identically. Every field is optional: `spring` and `damp` multiply the head and hand springs, `eye` how fast the eyeline turns, `breath` the breath's tempo (the fatigue heave included), `breathAmp` px of an everyday breath while standing, `sway` and `swayHz` an idle weight sway, `bounce` and `bounceHz` a light bounce on the toes, `habits` the idle habits it picks from (a name twice is twice as likely), `laugh` the laugh in its double take, `ko` its KO hold (`'tie'`, `'slump'`, or the generic stand-tall). The sway, bounce and breath play only while standing idle and ease in and out over a quarter second.
- **Habits.** Idle business, like a puppeteer's: after a randomized 1.8 to 3.6 s of quiet (standing idle, not fainted or tired, no take playing, the other fighter not attacking or jumping and more than 130 px away), a habit from the character's list plays (1 to 2.6 s), never the same one twice running when it has others, and the next waits 3.5 to 6.5 s more. Anything happening eases it out in about 80 ms. Habits move the guard a few px at most and never point a limb at the other fighter, so one never hides a guard or reads as a strike. The randomness is the layer's own (`Math.random`), never the sim's rng.

| Habit | What it looks like |
|---|---|
| `glance` | two nervous glances back over the shoulder |
| `fidget` | weight from foot to foot, a quick look up |
| `straighten` | up straight, precisely, the hands tucked in, held, then back |
| `watch` | the lead wrist comes up in front of the chest and the head tips down to it |
| `neckRoll` | a slow roll of the neck |
| `deepBreath` | one long breath in and out |
| `shrug` | a shrug |
| `shakeOut` | shaking the hands out, the guard dropping a touch |

| Character | Signature | Habits |
|---|---|---|
| TEMP | a quicker eye, a little sway; slumps when he wins | glance (twice as likely), shrug |
| INTERN | loose springs, a quick eye, a wide fast sway; laughs | fidget (twice as likely), glance |
| HELPDESK | slow, even | shrug, neckRoll |
| COURIER | quick springs, a bounce on the toes; laughs | shakeOut, glance |
| SECURITY | stiff springs, a slow eye, a wide slow sway | neckRoll |
| JANITOR | unhurried | neckRoll, shrug |
| THE MANAGER | firm springs, an unhurried eye; laughs; straightens his tie when he wins | watch |
| ACCOUNTANT | well damped, barely a sway | straighten |
| WINDOW WASHER | loose springs, a breezy sway | glance, shakeOut |
| THE CEO | firm springs, the slowest eye; laughs | straighten, watch |
| RECEPTIONIST | a quick eye | glance, straighten |
| LAWYER | damped | straighten, shrug |
| SALES REP | a bounce, a quick eye; laughs | shrug, shakeOut |
| HR REP | damped, watchful | straighten, glance |
| BARISTA | loose springs, a fast breath, a quick bounce; laughs | shakeOut, fidget |
| CHEF | a heavy sway | shakeOut, neckRoll |
| WELLNESS COACH | soft springs, a slow, deep breath you can see, a slow sway | deepBreath |
| ELECTRICIAN | quick springs | shakeOut, glance |

- **On the fighter pages.** A fighter page's big rig (`site/rigs.js`, ShowcaseView) runs the same layer on its own between moves: the signature, the cloth swinging and the habits (the next move waits for a habit to finish). The move cards and the build's roster sprites (a seamless 12-frame idle loop) don't use it.

Cost: about 3 µs per fighter per frame (`life.apply` timed in Node on two real rigs, 200,000 calls). Checked with `?life=0` against the default on the same seeded scenes, stepped at a fixed 60fps and drawn every frame: the sim's state (every unit's own fields, every rig joint and the clip clock) hashed every 100 steps matched with the layer on and off, and between two runs with it on, in ai-fight (TEMP v THE MANAGER, seed 42, 1,200 steps), combo-test, parry-test and stomp-test (900 each), with the same max combos, health and callouts.

The takes, signatures and habits, measured (2026-11-09, `vite preview`, four seeded ai-fight bouts of 1,800 steps: TEMP v THE MANAGER, INTERN v BARISTA, SECURITY v COURIER, ACCOUNTANT v WELLNESS COACH, each run three ways, everything on, `life.extras = false`, and `?life=0`): the sim hashes every 100 steps matched in all three, 72 of 72. Smoothness, counted as frames where a drawn hand or head moved more than 15 px in one 60fps frame while the fighter stood, walked or guarded (2,750 such frames in all): 111 with the takes and habits, 114 without them, 94 with the layer off. The takes and habits add none; the same walk-cycle frames show up in both lists, and the gap to "off" is the eyeline and springs above. Standing idle in Node for 40 s each, every character's fastest drawn hand or head move with the habits playing is 1 to 2.9 px a frame (0.7 without the layer), and every habit in every list fired.

Cloth is flat solid color, like the fighters: nothing on a fighter glows.

## Sound

Everything audio lives in `src/audio/`, and none of it uses sample files.

- `index.js` — the router: turns sim and fighter events into effects, pans each by the fighter's screen x (flipped by `gameAudio.setMirror(true)` in a mirrored view), and watches for whiffs (a strike reaching recovery without contact) and for which pool the music belongs in. It also owns the unlock on the first touch or key, M and N, the settings, and the phone's media player (`navigator.mediaSession`: "Stick Fight · <track>" with play, pause, next and previous). `window.__audio` exposes it for tests.
- `sfx.js` — the effects, built on the spot from a second of white noise and a few oscillators, each with a little random variation: whooshes by limb (a jab short and high, kicks longer and lower, the spinning enders longest), hits, block, clash (two limbs meeting: the block's clack made sharp, a double crack and a short ring), counter (the heaviest hit short of an ender, a second snap and a bright ping), footsteps, landings, the jump, a body fall with a slide, wall splat, wall bounce, ground bounce, the throw (a grab rustle, then a slam), whiff, the exhale of a faint, ROUND and FIGHT, KO, the slow-motion ender KO (pitched down with a long tail of echoes), combo notes that climb with the hit count, the ender stinger, a horde goon down, the match end, and UI clicks. The multi-floor events have theirs too: a floor break (a crash, debris and a railing clanging), a knock per stair tread, a vault landing, and a floor-clear stinger. The perfect round finisher has the throw's whoosh, the body on the glass, the squeak down it and the unstick (Arcade flow, Perfect rounds). A hit is built in layers scaled by its weight (a jab light, a cross a bit more, a heavy, a counter, an ender or a KO the most): a crack of a few milliseconds for the contact, a punch falling through 300 to 120 Hz that a phone speaker can play, a sub thump into 40 to 55 Hz for headphones, a lowpassed tail, a crunch through a tanh shaper on the heavies, and on an ender or a KO a long low boom with a harmonic a phone can hear. A light hit stays about 0.15 s long so a fast string doesn't smear, and the combo note and the ender stinger come in 15 and 30 ms behind the crack, under it. Effects go through a compressor, a limiter and a soft clip, so a horde pile-up can't clip. Impacts (hits, wall splats and bounces, the ground bounce) skip the compressor on a bus of their own, because through it every hit came out at the same peak, a jab as loud as a KO. At most 16 sound at once (a new one steals the oldest of the lowest priority; footsteps go first). The effects have their own AudioContext.
- `music.js` + `tracks.js` — the soundtrack, written as [Strudel](https://strudel.cc) code and played through Strudel, after cf-quarry's player. Strudel is only imported the first time music turns on. A track plays its form twice, then the next track in the current pool starts. The pool follows the game: `menu` on the title and its attract demo, the mode select, the online menus, the high score tables, game over, initials and the ending; `fight` for a normal match, `horde` in horde mode (each cleared floor moves to the next horde track), `calm` in training (the dummy scene). The arcade flow names the pool for each screen through `gameAudio.setMusicPool(pool)` (null hands it back); without the flow (a `?scene=` page) the audio module guesses it from the sim four times a second, and `?scene=attract` plays `menu`. The music ducks under the KO stingers, the slow-motion ender, the floor break and FIGHT, and gets a quick sidechain-style dip under a hit (`music.pump`): none for a jab, so a fast string doesn't wobble it, about 2.5 dB for a cross, 6 for a heavy, 8 for a counter or a throw, and 10.5 for an ender or a wall splat, down in 10 ms and back in 0.1 to 0.35 s. A silent looping `<audio>` plays beside it so a phone shows its media player.
- `ui.js` — the SETTINGS panel and the desktop menu button (the button's look and place are `src/corner.js`'s). Its sliders are drawn in the DOM and driven by pointer events, because `touch.js` cancels every touchmove on the page. The panel itself is a `data-scroll` box, so on a screen shorter than it a finger scrolls it (Controls, "What scrolls under a finger"). `key()` works it from the flow's menu keys, and it takes Escape itself.

The tracks share one theme (`THEME`, a four-bar call, answer, fall and rest), one set of changes (i VI VII i, iv VI VII v), and one form (an intro, `a` with the drums, `b` with the theme, a `drop` without drums, and `c` with everything). They use only Strudel's built-in synths and noises, so nothing is fetched to play them, and any of them pasted into strudel.cc plays there.

| Track | Pool | BPM | Key | Feel |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Overtime | fight | 128 | A minor | darksynth, a galloping bass and a sawtooth lead |
| Floor 37 | fight | 120 | E minor | synthwave, gated chords and an arpeggio |
| Freight Elevator | fight | 72 | D minor | heavy half-time, a distorted bass and a crushed snare |
| Hard Reboot | fight, horde | 136 | G minor | darksynth, an offbeat bass and stabs through a delay |
| Cubicle Riot | horde | 152 | C minor | industrial at a run, a sixteenth-note bass |
| Nightwatch | calm | 60 | F minor | pads, rain on the glass, the theme on a bell |
| Insert Coin | menu | 116 | B minor | the cabinet in the lobby: bit-crushed square arpeggios, a triangle bass, the theme on a pulse lead |

**Phones get a lighter mix.** On a touch device (`pointer: coarse`) `music.js` thins each track before playing it: supersaws drop to 2 unison voices and the distortion and bit-crush worklets come out, because the full mix was more than a phone's audio thread could keep up with alongside the game (choppy music). Reverb, delays and filters stay.

**The Strudel bundle.** `public/vendor/strudel.mjs` is the `@strudel/web` 1.3.0 bundle (850 KB), copied from cf-quarry with one marked patch (the cyclist looks 0.45s ahead instead of 0.1, so a long game frame on a phone doesn't make it skip slices of the music; search the file for "stick-fight patch"), with its license text beside it in `public/vendor/LICENSE`. It lives in `public/` so Vite copies it into the build untouched; `music.js` imports it at runtime by URL, because the bundle resolves its own helper files relative to itself. Its source is at https://codeberg.org/uzu/strudel.

The game is free software under the GNU Affero General Public License v3.0 or later; see `LICENSE`. If you serve a modified version to players, the AGPL asks you to offer them its source.

- **Music engine:** [Strudel](https://strudel.cc) by the Strudel contributors, AGPL-3.0-or-later, source at https://codeberg.org/uzu/strudel. The seven tracks in `src/audio/tracks.js` are written as Strudel patterns and played by it.
- **Fonts:** [Bungee](https://fonts.google.com/specimen/Bungee) by David Jonathan Ross and [Teko](https://fonts.google.com/specimen/Teko) by the Indian Type Foundry, both SIL Open Font License. The game (`play/`) serves its own Latin subsets from `public/fonts/` with their license files (`OFL-Bungee.txt`, `OFL-Teko.txt`); since 2026-11-11 the site's other pages load the same files, so no page asks Google Fonts for anything.
- **Inspiration:** the animation bar is Xiao Xiao, Zhu Zhiqiang's stick-figure fight series. No art from it ships with the game.

The SETTINGS panel and the attract screen link back here.

## Practices

- No framework, no npm deps at runtime. Game modules stay plain `.js` (not `.ts`) so the capture tool's raw file server can load them; the Worker is TypeScript, built by wrangler. Playwright (global) only for `gauntlet/capture.mjs` and the test scripts; jsQR is a dev dependency for `npm run test:qr` only.
- Logical resolution 1280x720, DPR-aware. Everything positions in logical pixels. The building is 2560 logical pixels wide; the camera shows part of it.
- Each module owns its folder and its demo page. Cross-module contracts live in `gauntlet/BRIEF.md`.
- Commit after every gauntlet round with the round number in the subject.
- The capture tool serves files raw, without Vite, so anything `main.js` imports has to be plain JavaScript; that's why `src/flow/` is JS with JSDoc types rather than TypeScript.
- Capture output is large (about 1.3 MB per frame). Delete capture folders when a round is done; `gauntlet/captures/` is gitignored.
- **Stop what you start.** Every agent session and critic shuts down the processes it started before it reports, whether it passed or failed: `vite`, `wrangler dev` (and its `workerd` and `esbuild` children), Playwright and Chromium browsers, perf runs. Start long-lived servers so they can be found again (note the PID, or use a port only you use) and kill them when you finish, then check with `ps -ax | grep "$PWD"` that nothing from your working copy is still running. Several cards and three critics share one Mac. Leftover servers piled up there on 2026-11-11: 23 from finished cards held a load average of 80 to 120 on 12 cores, critic rounds took 20 to 40 minutes, and the join tests flaked from timeouts.
